# Decisions (ADRs)

Format: context · options · decision · consequences. **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** marks choices the owner should confirm.

**Owner review list** (every ADR tagged NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW, kept in step with the tags): ADR-001 storage location · ADR-002 layout and index format · ADR-005 platform floors · ADR-006 standalone package (amended by ADR-051: the iOS Simulator device containers, owner-accepted) · ADR-012 layout deviations (amended in round 4: STAMPS drops `lastMaintenanceAt`) · ADR-013 staging names · ADR-017 deletion grace (consumer contract) · ADR-026 background session and key exposure · ADR-027 Apple-tier pack retention · ADR-033 compatibility shims · ADR-035 storage policy defaults · ADR-036 network and power defaults · ADR-037 facade deviations · ADR-042 `.shutDown` signatures · ADR-044 facade additions · ADR-047 log-sink seam (public signatures; added in round 4) · ADR-048 configuration knobs (public signatures) · ADR-049 Stage 2 level-1 merge (A-4.5, S3-B-001: the R-MAN-1 deviation, UTF-16/UTF-32 manifests rejected, so the owner confirms no published manifest is UTF-16; A-7, S3-B-002: the R-OBS-1 interval seam, public API; the heading carries the tag since the Stage 3 merge, ADR-057) · ADR-050 Stage 2 level-2 merge (B-3 `index.bin` byte layout; D-10 an explicit `shutdown()` cancels background downloads; D-19.2 the background task tag `ztak1 <name> <size | -> [resume]`, a format the OS persists in `taskDescription`; the public `BackgroundSessionDriver.startDownload(…resumeData:)` signature) · ADR-052 Stage 2 level-3 merge (deviations from R-STO-11 / store.md §7: C-12 checksum-less legacy files without a local checksum are downloaded again; C-13 an undecodable legacy manifest adopts nothing and its files stay in the legacy root; C-14 a legacy root on another volume is dropped; the C-14 cycle-2 deferral bound, three failed attempts per lifetime, with its crash residual; C-15 phase 4 keeps unattributable files and a non-empty root) · ADR-055 Stage 2 level-5 WP-K merge (the R-CAR-6 shims: K-2 a write-once install point, `installed = nil` and re-installation ignored; K-3 `@MainActor` lookups; K-5 the providers describe a client tier and no longer download, the app key by option (a): documentation and a manifest source that sends the key; K-6 one client per install point, and `CDNLocalStore(baseDirectory: X)` rooting the store at X's parent's `ZTronAssetKit/v1` (storage location), one store per parent directory; ## ADR-001 — Storage location · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** Promise 1 ("no mid-use waiting") cannot hold if the OS may silently purge essential assets. The prior art used `Library/Caches/CDNAssets` with the sync stamp in `UserDefaults` (D-6).
- **Options.** (a) `Caches/` — OS-purgeable, no backup cost, no module-owned eviction needed. (b) `Application Support/` excluded from backup, module-owned quota/eviction. (c) `Documents/` — visible in Files, backed up.
- **Decision.** (b) by default: `Application Support/ZTronAssetKit/v1/`, `isExcludedFromBackup = true`, file protection `completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication`. `StorageLocation.caches` and `.custom(URL)` remain selectable (R-STO-10).
- **Consequences.** The module must own eviction (R-CLN-4) and OS-purge recovery still matters for `.caches` (R-CLN-7). Storage is not reclaimed by the OS under pressure — the budget and `purge` API are the user's levers.

## ADR-002 — On-disk layout and index format · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** D-10: a pretty-path layout makes a directory change a set of non-atomic moves; D-14: manifest text became path components.
- **Options.** (a) Keep pretty paths. (b) Content-addressed blobs + one atomically replaced index (SPEC §4.3). (c) SQLite.
- **Decision.** (b). `blobs/<aa>/<sha256>.<ext>`, `staging/<uuid>.part|.resume`, `store.lock`, and `index.bin`: a compact, versioned binary format (magic, `schemaVersion`, `generation`, length-prefixed records, SHA-256 trailer to detect torn writes) replaced via write-temp → `F_FULLFSYNC` → rename. Final details are fixed in `ARCHITECTURE.md` (Stage 1). SQLite is rejected: a dependency-free binary index meets P-2 and keeps crash enumeration (R-STO-5) tractable through the `FileSystem` seam.
- **Consequences.** Directory changes are index edits; identical bytes dedupe; the index is rebuildable from blob names + the last manifest. Legacy files are adopted by hash (R-STO-11). Debugging needs a dump tool rather than `cat` (provided via `diagnosticReport()`).

## ADR-003 — Blocking work off the cooperative pool
- **Context.** C2 / §4.2: hashing, copying and directory enumeration must not block a cooperative-pool thread for more than ~10 ms. At an iOS 15 floor there is no `Mutex`, `OSAllocatedUnfairLock` or custom executors.
- **Options.** (a) Dedicated GCD queue(s) bridged with `withCheckedThrowingContinuation` + `withTaskCancellationHandler`. (b) Chunked work on the pool with `Task.yield()` between chunks.
- **Decision.** (a) for anything that touches the file system: production code never performs a blocking file-system call directly from an async context; it submits *bounded* units (one rename, one ≤ 1 MiB read, one index commit) to an injected blocking-I/O executor — a dedicated `DispatchQueue` in `.live()`, bridged with `withCheckedThrowingContinuation`. Long operations (hashing 256 MiB, enumerating 10,000 blobs) are decomposed into such units with `Task.checkCancellation()` between them, so cancellation needs no shared mutable flag (no lock, no `@unchecked`). CPU-only work on ≤ 1 MiB (one SHA-256 update, < 1 ms) may run inline. (b) is used for pure in-memory work (diff/plan over 10,000 entries), yielding periodically. Stage 1 fixes the exact interfaces in `ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- **Consequences.** P-9 (heartbeat gap) and P-6 (≥ 0.7× one-shot hash throughput despite the hops) prove it. Tests inject an executor that runs inline so they stay deterministic.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, 2026-10-09, ADR-050): `Mutex` exception.** `Synchronization.Mutex` is allowed in production only inside code gated `@available(iOS 26.0, macOS 26.0, *)` (the Apple-hosted tier), never `@unchecked`, with no `await` and no I/O inside a critical section. Exactly three types use it: `PackTransferStop` (request, read, claim), `HashingChunkSource` (the hasher update of one ≤ 1 MiB chunk and the finalise, the stop flag; the base reader's I/O runs outside the lock) and `FileHandleChunkReader` (publishes the handle only; the open and every read run outside the lock). Code at the iOS 15 floor keeps the rule above; the gated `Mutex` of test fakes stays ADR-014's. APPLE-API-VERIFICATION #19 and §4 item 7 carry the same exception.

## ADR-004 — Typed throws
- **Context.** R-ENV-6 (SHOULD). Typed throws are a compile-time feature; concrete error types back-deploy.
- **Decision.** Public async APIs throw `AssetError` concretely (`throws(AssetError)`) wherever the compiler accepts it at the iOS 15 floor; APIs that run caller-supplied throwing closures (`withAsset`) stay untyped. Internal layers use their own closed error types (`ManifestError`, `TransferError`, `StoreError`) mapped once at the facade.
- **Consequences.** Callers can switch exhaustively over `AssetError` (R-CAR-7).

## ADR-005 — Platform floors · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Decision.** iOS **15.0** (owner requirement, matches ZTronCarouselCore; R-ENV-2). macOS **12.0** for the test host — the macOS release that matches iOS 15's API set (`OSSignposter`, async `URLSession`, `os.Logger`), so code that compiles for macOS tests the same availability surface it ships on iOS. The Apple-hosted tier is `@available(iOS 26.0, macOS 26.0, *)` behind `#if canImport(BackgroundAssets)`.

## ADR-006 — Standalone package; PROMPT Stage 4 replaced · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** The owner instructed (2026-10-04): build a standalone package, do not modify any other package; read-only access to the siblings only. PROMPT Stage 4 planned the adoption inside `ZTronCarouselCore` on a branch.
- **Decision.** No file outside this repository is written. R-CAR-6 is delivered *inside this repo*: drop-in compatibility shims for every pre-existing public CDN symbol, compiled and exercised by a test target here (so the shims are proven against the real `AssetClient`), plus `docs/MIGRATION.md` describing how to adopt them in ZTronCarouselCore. Q-10 ("pre-existing CDN tests pass through the shims in ZTronCarouselCore") is reported as **not verified** in `VERIFICATION.md` — and there were no pre-existing CDN tests to port (`BASELINE.md`).
- **Consequences.** Adoption is a follow-up the owner performs; `verify.sh` never depended on it.
- **Amendment (iOS Simulator test build, 2026-10-09, ADR-051).** One owner-accepted exception: running the tests on an iOS Simulator installs the test host and bundles into that device's container under `~/Library/Developer/CoreSimulator` and writes there while they run. Agents use one named simulator each, never the owner's booted one, shut it down afterwards (`xcrun simctl shutdown <UDID>`), and give every `xcodebuild` a `-derivedDataPath` inside the repository.

## ADR-007 — Harness fixes before the freeze
- **Context.** PROMPT Stage 0 step 6 — make the harness real on this toolchain.
- **Decision.** Two fixes, both strengthening: (1) Swift 6.4's `swiftbuild` engine builds one `.xctest` bundle per test target but `--show-codecov-path` exports coverage for only one of them, so the coverage gate re-exports the merged profile over every bundle (`llvm-cov export -summary-only`); (2) `gates.py` filters on `"/Sources/"`, which relative paths never contain, so the unsafe and docs gates checked nothing — `verify.sh` now passes absolute paths. Both were proven: per-target coverage appears for all six targets; an injected `@unchecked Sendable` and an undocumented `public func` now fail their gates. `quality-gates.json` is untouched (sha256 `2725907c…026f`).

## ADR-008 — Fixtures for the "shipped example manifests"
- **Context.** `Manifests/` does not exist in either sibling (RECON §1).
- **Decision.** Author the series under `Tests/Fixtures/manifests/` from real fixture payloads (so every checksum is a real SHA-256): AWS tier (`remotePath = media/<sha256[:16]>/<name>.<ext>`) and Apple tier (`remotePath = <packID>/<path>`), each as v1 → checksum change → directory change → removal, in both wire shapes (bare array and envelope).

## ADR-009 — Worktree location
- **Context.** CLAUDE.md P2 suggests `../asset-kit-wt-<wp>`; this session may only write inside this repository.
- **Decision.** Worktrees live at `.worktrees/<wp>` (git-ignored), branches `wp/<wp>`. Same isolation, same merge discipline.

## ADR-010 — Benchmarks target
- **Decision.** All `P-n` benchmarks live in the test target `ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks` (suites named `*Benchmarks`), so `--skip Benchmarks` / `--filter Benchmarks` select them exactly. Thresholds are read at run time from `Scripts/quality-gates.json` (located via `#filePath`).
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 3).** One parent suite, `@Suite(.serialized) struct ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks {}` (`Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks.swift`, orchestrator-owned), serializes every benchmark (sync-2 S2-14): independent top-level suites run concurrently and contaminate P-1, P-6, P-9 and P-12. Each work package adds its benchmarks as a suite nested in `extension ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks` in its own file (`BenchModel.swift`, `BenchStorage.swift`, `BenchTransport.swift`, `BenchSync.swift`, `BenchKit.swift`; ARCHITECTURE §1.2, §11), never as a top-level suite. The type shares the target's name; that compiles and runs (verified: `--filter Benchmarks` runs `ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks` → `BudgetsBenchmarks`).

## ADR-011 — No test-only dependencies
- **Decision.** None. Swift Testing ships with the toolchain; the loopback HTTP server is hand-written on `Network.framework` in `ZTronAssetTestSupport`.

## Stage 1 contract decisions (contract-fix round 1)

ADR-012…041 were written by the contract author of contract-fix round 1 from the three designs (`docs/design/*.md`) and the six adversarial reviews (`docs/design/reviews/*.md`). Every finding they resolve is listed in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §12; the contract code cites them by number.

The designs proposed ADRs under their own provisional numbers. They map to the final numbers as follows: store.md ADR-012 (layout) → ADR-012; store.md ADR-013 (durability levels) → ADR-015; store.md ADR-014 (fake availability funnel) → ADR-014; store.md ADR-015 (policy defaults) → ADR-035. sync.md ADR-S1 → ADR-020; S2 → ADR-021 (handle instead of control stream); S3 → ARCHITECTURE §10; S4 → ADR-014, ADR-017, ADR-022; S5 → ADR-023; S6 → ADR-037; S7 → ADR-036. transport.md ADR-T1 → ADR-026; T2 → ADR-027; T3 → ADR-013 (names) and ADR-019 (data tasks replace the stateless download path); T4 (resume data at rest) → moot under ADR-019; T5 → ADR-021; T6 → ADR-019 (reversed: data tasks); T7 → ARCHITECTURE §6.6 (single `#available`); T8 → ARCHITECTURE §6.7 (FIN, never RST, in gating tests).

## ADR-012 — Layout deviations from SPEC §4.3 · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** SPEC §4.3 recommends `store.lock`, `index.bin`, `blobs/<aa>/<sha256>.<ext>`, `staging/<uuid>.part|.resume`. Crash safety (H-1) and recovery need more (store.md §2).
- **Options.** (a) SPEC layout verbatim (one index file, UUID staging names). (b) SPEC layout plus a previous generation and a transient temp file, a persisted doomed set, and deterministic staging names. (c) A write-ahead log.
- **Decision.** (b). Under `<base>/ZTronAssetKit/v1/`: `store.lock`; `index.bin` (current), `index.prev` (previous generation, a fallback against single-file corruption), `index.tmp` (transient); `blobs/<aa>/<64 hex>.<ext>`; `staging/<TransferID>.<epoch>-<sequence>.<kind>` (ADR-013). The index is the binary format of store.md §2.3 with these amendments: DOOMED entries carry a `DoomReason` (ADR-018); the header records the launch epoch; every header field is range-checked before arithmetic (store-1 S1-04). Every file is listed in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3.
- **Consequences.** Two extra index names and one header field to document and fixture (R-STO-12 golden fixtures per schema). Load cost stays far inside P-2 (three candidates read: about 2× of 2.8 ms measured).
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 4).** STAMPS has no `lastMaintenanceAt`: store.md §2.3 listed it, but nothing read it and no API the engine reaches could set it, so `CleanupState.lastMaintenanceAt` was removed rather than persisted as an always-absent field (ADR-046 amendment). The schema is not shipped; `schemaVersion` stays 1.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / B-3 · NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW): the v1 bytes.** 64-byte little-endian header: magic `ZTAKIDX\0`, `schemaVersion` u16, `sectionCount` u16, flags u32 (bit 0 degraded), `generation` u64, `bodyLength` u64 (≤ 64 MiB), `writtenAtMillis` i64, launch epoch u64, `storeID` 16 bytes (store.md's `headerLength` is dropped: 64 is implied by the schema). Body: sections `(tag, varint length, payload)` in increasing tag order, STAMPS `0x01`, STRINGS `0x02`, RECORDS `0x03`, DOOMED `0x04`; unknown tags `< 0x80` are malformed, `≥ 0x80` skipped. SHA-256 trailer over header and body. Dates are the IEEE bit pattern of `timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate` (store.md's whole seconds dropped); strings interned by their UTF-8 bytes; exactly one encoding per state, so a valid file re-encodes byte-identically (golden fixture `Tests/Fixtures/index/v1/golden.bin` + `golden.json`, R-STO-12). The owner confirms the layout before schema 1 ships; a schema change adds `v2` fixtures and a decoder.

## ADR-013 — Staging names are `<TransferID>.<epoch>-<sequence>.<kind>` · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** SPEC §4.3 says `staging/<uuid>`. transport.md proposed `<TransferID>.part` (deterministic, self-describing at relaunch). transport-1 TR1-1 and transport-2 T2-02 showed that one name per content lets two producers write the same path while the store hashes it; T2-06 showed per-process attempt numbers are not comparable across launches.
- **Options.** (a) UUIDs from the seeded random source. (b) `<TransferID>.part`. (c) `<TransferID>.<epoch>-<sequence>.<kind>`, with `epoch` = the store's launch epoch (the index generation allocated at `open()`, persisted, strictly increasing) and `sequence` an engine counter.
- **Decision.** (c), implemented by `StagingFileName` and `TransferAttempt` (Model). Kinds: `part`, `parttmp`, `resume` (our own JSON `ResumeSidecar`), `resumetmp`, `verifying` (claimed by the store for publication, store-1 S1-12). No manifest text in any name (R-SEC-2); no randomness (C4); unique per start; self-describing at relaunch (H-6). Every rename into or out of staging is exclusive (`RENAME_EXCL`): an `EEXIST` is a uniqueness bug, never a replace.
- **Consequences.** A resumed attempt first moves the previous attempt's partial to its own name (`StagingSink.continuePartial`). Staging GC parses names; anything that does not parse is foreign and collected (R-CLN-3).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / B-5, B-34, B-36): epoch uniqueness.** The launch epoch is the generation the launch commit carries (persisted; `nextGeneration = 1 + max(generation, launch epoch)` over the headers that parsed). It is unique only across launches whose launch commit succeeded over a readable index: a read-only launch (an index file could not be read, B-34) computes it without that header and may take the previous launch's epoch, and after a launch whose root barrier and launch commit both failed (B-36) a power loss can bring back older names, so a later launch may take that epoch again; a wiped root starts again at 1. Staging reconciliation and staging GC therefore never take epoch equality alone for "this launch" (or the engine stages nothing while the store is `storageUnavailable(.ioFailure)`), and exclusive renames report `nameInUse` on a collision. The "strictly increasing" of the options above holds only across those launches.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** Names parse only in the canonical spelling the module writes: each part of an attempt token is ASCII digits without a leading zero, or `0` alone, so `StagingFileName(parsing:)` is again `nil` for anything that is not exactly one of our names, and `BackgroundTaskTag(decoding:)` and `StagingFileName`'s decoding inherit it (S3AH-2). The written format is unchanged, so the tag is unchanged.

## ADR-014 — Test fakes and availability
- **Context.** The unsafe gate scans all of `Sources/`, `ZTronAssetTestSupport` included (≤ 3 escapes in total; target 0). Fakes with synchronous APIs (`FileSystem`, `TimeSource`, `LegacyDefaults`, `LogTap`, transfer handles) need a lock. `Mutex` is iOS 18 / macOS 15. The reviewer of the Stage 1 contracts verified with the compiler that `@available` on a `@Suite` is rejected ("Attribute 'Suite' cannot be applied to this structure because it has been marked @available…"), and so is a `@Test` inside such a suite.
- **Options.** (a) `@unchecked Sendable` lock boxes (burns the escape budget). (b) Actor fakes only (impossible for synchronous protocol requirements). (c) Actors where the protocol is async; `Mutex` fakes behind `@available(macOS 15.0, iOS 18.0, *)` elsewhere, gated **per `@Test` function**. (d) An unannotated factory funnel with `#available` + `preconditionFailure`.
- **Decision.** (c). Async-protocol fakes are actors and ungated (`FakeDeviceConditionsSource`, `FakeMemoryPressureSource`, `SteppedBlockingExecutor`, `ScriptedSnapshotSink`). `ManualTimeSource`, `FakeFileSystem`, `FakeLegacyDefaults`, `RecordingLogTap`, `FakeTransferHandle`, `FakeRemoteSource`, `FakeTransferBackend`, `FakeStagingSink` are gated `@available(macOS 15.0, iOS 18.0, *)`. Tests that use them carry **single-platform** `@available(macOS 15.0, *)` on each `@Test` function, never on a `@Suite`. Verified in round 1: `SyncContractTests` and `AppleHostedContractTests` build and pass that way; `ZTronAssetTestSupport` typechecks at `arm64-apple-ios15.0` with zero diagnostics. **(d) is rejected** (contract-fix round 2): its `#available` would be a second one under `Sources/`, and the R-APL-5 scan (`R-APL-5: the package contains exactly one #available`) covers **all** of `Sources/`, `ZTronAssetTestSupport` included. A cast to an ungated protocol (`StagingAttachable`, ADR-045) is the pattern when ungated code must reach a gated fake.
- **Consequences.** Test hosts need macOS 15+ (this host is macOS 27). Production floors are unchanged (ADR-005). Zero escapes in `ZTronAssetTestSupport`; exactly one `#available` in `Sources/` (`AppleHostedTier.resolve`).
- **Amendment (iOS Simulator test build, 2026-10-09, ADR-051).** The single-platform gate builds on macOS but vanishes on iOS, where the gated fakes need iOS 18, so the tests did not build for the simulator. Tests that use the gated fakes now carry `@available(macOS 15.0, iOS 18.0, *)` on each `@Test` function (or on the helper type or function that uses the fake), never on a `@Suite`; Apple-tier tests carry `@available(macOS 26.0, iOS 26.0, *)`; every gate names both platforms (ADR-051's convention). The two-platform form on a `@Test` builds and passes with this toolchain on macOS and on the iOS Simulator, so store.md's claim that it is rejected does not reproduce. Consequences: on iOS the gated tests need an iOS 18+ simulator (the Apple-tier ones iOS 26+); the suite runs on the iOS 27.0 Simulator.

## ADR-015 — Index commit durability and directory barriers
- **Context.** store.md D1 and its proposed "two-level durability" ADR. store-1 S1-01 (state built on a namespace a killed process never synced), S1-02 (the root's own entry in `<base>` never synced), S1-07 (ENOSPC livelock), S1-14 (`F_FULLFSYNC` 4×/s), S1-15 (`fsync` fallback masks EIO).
- **Decision.** Ordered commits sync `index.tmp` with `F_BARRIERFSYNC` and the root with a barrier; durable commits (`flush`, `close`, migration "done", before an Apple pack release) use `F_FULLFSYNC` on the file, the root, `ZTronAssetKit` and `<base>`. After creating `ZTronAssetKit` the store barrier-syncs `<base>` (and any ancestor it created). `open()` marks `blobs/` dirty and barrier-syncs the root before any launch deletion; every adoption (launch, `apply`, migration) dirties its fan-out. On ENOSPC at commit step 2 the unit removes `index.tmp` and `index.prev` and retries once; `open()` never fails because a post-load commit failed (read-only `degradedReadOnly`). The sync fallback to `fsync` applies only to ENOTSUP/EINVAL/ENOTTY. `FakeFileSystem`'s `processKill` keeps the current/durable split, and R-STO-5 enumerates kill→power-loss pairs across two lifetimes.
- **Consequences.** One extra barrier per launch and per adopted fan-out (≈ 0.37 ms each); P-5 keeps its margin because ordered commits no longer `F_FULLFSYNC`.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / B-28, B-29, B-34, B-36).** On ENOSPC at step 2 the unit removes `index.tmp`, and `index.prev` only while `index.bin` is the file the store knows to hold the committed generation (from the load's full validation or its own renames, never from a header), then retries once; a loaded `index.tmp` is synced and promoted before the next write (ARCHITECTURE §4.1 step 0); a step-4 or step-5 failure is undone, so `index.bin` is never missing after a failed commit. The scheduled commit backs off after failures to at most 5 minutes; explicit callers try at once after the coalescing window (B-29). `open()` never fails because a post-load commit failed: the store serves the loaded state with the commit due; when an index file could not be read, that lifetime is read-only and degraded and commits nothing (writes throw `storageUnavailable(.ioFailure)`, B-34); nothing counts as committed until a root sync ordered the names the load read (B-36). The "(read-only `degradedReadOnly`)" above is superseded: the merged store never produces that reason (it remains a Model case of `StorageUnavailableReason`); a failed commit leaves the store writable with the commit due, and `requireCommitted` throws `.fileSystem(.noSpace)`, which maps to `insufficientSpace`.

## ADR-016 — Snapshot acknowledgement and URL holders
- **Context.** H-2/I-3 rest on "the mirror installed a snapshot without the blob". store-2 S2-02: an ignored install still returned, and a reopened store restarted versions; S2-12: engine-held snapshot copies hand out URLs no acknowledgement covers; S2-13: allocation points unspecified; sync-2 S2-12: releasing a large snapshot on main costs milliseconds.
- **Decision.** `SnapshotKey(epoch, version)`, `epoch` = launch epoch, `version` allocated synchronously when the snapshot is built. `SnapshotSink.install` returns `InstallAck { installed, accesses, retired }`; the store counts an acknowledgement only when `installed.epoch == myEpoch`; the mirror swaps only for a greater key and has an explicit `reset()`; the replaced snapshot is handed back in `retired` and released off the main actor. `AssetSnapshot` is `package`; only the mirror derives URLs from it; the engine resolves through `AssetStore.location(for:extensions:recordingAccess:)` (current actor state).
- **Consequences.** Implemented in `Sources/ZTronAssetStorage/Snapshot/AssetSnapshot.swift` and `Sources/ZTronAssetKit/Mirror/SnapshotMirror.swift`; tested (`R-STO-13: the mirror ignores an out-of-order snapshot and acknowledges what readers see`).
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 3): install rate.** The store is the only caller of `install`, so it owns the ≤ 10 Hz rule of sync-2 S2-12. `publish(_:binding:expecting:urgency: AssetPriority)`: `.userInitiated` installs before returning; `.prefetch` and `.bulk` install at most once per `StoreConfiguration.snapshotInstallInterval` (from `SyncTuning.snapshotInstallInterval`, default 0.1 s, clamped ≥ 0), the trailing install being a store deadline (`nextDeadline()`, `runDueWork()`, `StoreWorkReport.installedSnapshot`; ADR-022). Options considered: an `installSnapshotNow()` call (rejected: one more await on every user publish, and the lane is known at the call) and rate-limiting everything (rejected: delays the page the user just opened). Consequence: O(N) snapshot builds and main-actor hops fall from one per commit to ≤ 10 per second during a bulk sync; returned locations come from actor state, so a coalesced install never delays a `resolve`. Regression test `R-STO-13: bulk publishes install at most one snapshot per snapshotInstallInterval and a user-initiated publish installs at once` (WP-B).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / B-17): what counts as an acknowledgement.** The store counts an acknowledgement only when the sink swapped in exactly the snapshot it offered, of its own epoch (`retired != nil` and `installed == offered`); a refusal never counts, even one in favour of a newer snapshot of the same store. The epoch comes from the index files alone and can repeat (a wiped root, ADR-013's amendment), so after a `rootVanished` reopen the facade calls `SnapshotMirror.reset()` before the replacement store's first install (WP-G); otherwise readers keep the old store's snapshot until the new store's key passes it, which never happens when its epoch is smaller (a wiped root starts at 1). Regression test `R-CLN-5: a replacement store whose epoch repeats never counts the previous store's snapshot as acknowledged` (WP-B).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 WP-K merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-055 / L5K-R21, L5K-R24): user-initiated answers.** A user-initiated *publication* installs at once; a user-initiated *answer* need not wait for the readers' snapshot. The engine's pre-transfer read (`answeredFromStore`) alone installs before it answers a user-initiated asker after a read that installed nothing at once (L5K-R21, which WP-K's coalesced adoptions made necessary); the commit path, `ensureAvailable`'s present members, the resolve's fast hit and joins answered from the ledger keep this ADR's lag: their answer, a location valid at once, can precede the readers' snapshot by at most about two install intervals (0.2 s by default; RE-S40's shortcut), until the trailing install the engine schedules (L5K-R24; MIGRATION.md §4; Stage 3 holds an optional `installIfDue()`).

## ADR-017 — Deletion grace, the awake clock and the reaper · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (consumer contract)
- **Context.** store.md D4. store-1 S1-13 and store-2 S2-03: the reaper busy-loops when a key is past its deadline but blocked by a lease, a missing ack or a lagging commit. store-2 S2-14: `UIImage(contentsOfFile:)` reloads purged data from the path, and `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` keeps counting while the device sleeps, so a suspended app loses its grace.
- **Decision.** A blob is deleted only when: an acknowledged snapshot of the store's epoch lacks it; `deletionGrace` (default 10 s) has passed on the **awake clock** (`TimeSource.uptimeNow()`, `CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW`); the generation that dropped it is committed; no lease holds it; no publication of the key is in flight; and one fresh no-op install after the deadline proved the main actor ran. There is no reaper loop: `AssetStore.nextDeadline()` reports a deadline only for keys whose sole remaining blocker is time, and lease release, acknowledgement and commit completion re-arm the engine's alarm (ADR-022). Public contract, on `AssetLocation`: a URL stays readable for at least `deletionGrace` of awake time after the snapshot without it is installed and for the whole of a lease; consumers that may re-read a path later load through `Data` or hold a lease (`withAsset`).
- **Consequences.** `TimeSource` gained `uptimeNow()`; `ManualTimeSource.sleepDevice(for:)` models device sleep. The R-CAR-6 adoption guidance must tell the carousel to load images through `Data` or a lease.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 WP-K merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-055 / K-11, L5K-R5).** The consequence for the adoption guidance is discharged in MIGRATION.md §2 (the `existingLocalURL` row) and §4 and in `existingLocalURL`'s doc comment: the URL names stored content, the old file is removed `deletionGrace` after the snapshot without it, and a consumer that re-reads the path later (`BasicVideoPage`'s player) looks it up again when the read fails, or holds a lease (`AssetClient.withAsset`). The guide gives that form, not "load through `Data`".

## ADR-018 — Blob reference discipline and doom reasons
- **Context.** store-2 S2-01 (concurrent publications corrupt `refCount`), S2-04 (a committed generation with `DOOMED ∋ k` and a present reference to `k`), store-1 S1-03 (a power loss resurrects a purged or quarantined blob that launch adoption re-binds as verified), S1-05 (dedupe, rescue, checksum-less publishes and retried `apply` re-download after a kill), S1-06 (a wrong-bytes blob reused forever), S1-11 (degraded fallback deletes the older generation's DOOMED keys).
- **Decision.** One operation gains a reference (`acquireReference`): it awaits an in-flight publication (`publishing: [BlobKey: Task]`) or deletion of the same key, rescues `doomed(.unreferenced)`, increments `live`. Every table update after an await is read-modify-write. `DOOMED` is derived from the blob table at commit capture, never maintained separately; at load the store repairs `DOOMED \ referenced` and counts it (`StoreMetrics.doomedRepairs`). Doom reasons: `unreferenced` (adoptable, rescuable), `purged` and `corrupt` (never re-adopted, never rescued; a verified publish replaces a corrupt name with `rename(.replacing)`). Launch adoption iterates records and binds only `absent(neverFetched|evicted|healing)`, `needsHeal` and stale records. `apply` adopts before it dereferences. Checksum-less and rescue publishes are committed before they return. In degraded mode DOOMED keys are protected like orphans.
- **Consequences.** Implemented by WP-B/C against `DoomReason`, `Residency`, `AbsenceReason` (`Sources/ZTronAssetStorage/Store/AssetRecord.swift`).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / B-6, B-12, B-24).** DOOMED is derived inside the commit unit from the blob table captured with the state, including the doom of every key whose deletion unit is in flight (`deletingDooms`) and of every key under replacement (`replacingDooms`: such a key is `live` in the table while a verified file replaces a `purged`/`corrupt` name, so a crash before that rename never adopts the rotten bytes). Launch and `apply` adoption bind a record that accepts adoption to any listed key that is live, unreferenced, or doomed `unreferenced` (rescued), never to a `purged` or `corrupt` doom (B-12), so a deduplicated publish that returned before a kill is not downloaded again. In degraded mode the reaper deletes nothing at all, not only orphans and DOOMED keys: the doom set itself is untrusted (B-6).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L3 merge, 2026-10-10, ADR-052 / rulings R1–R3, C-23).** `apply` and `admit` never adopt a record `absent(.evicted)`: `ApplyPlan.compute` does not list it and `adoptKnownBlob` refuses it, so only the launch adoption re-adopts an evicted record (R3, kept literal at Level 3: OI-15). `apply` binds a launch orphan only while `state.core.orphans[key]` still equals the size its re-stat confirmed (R1). Orphan GC dooms unknown on-disk blobs `unreferenced` through `doomOrphans(_:)` instead of unlinking them, with `eligibleAt` = doom time + `deletionGrace` on the awake clock (the only doom whose grace starts at the doom), so a publication (`acquireReference`) or an adoption before the reap rescues them (R2, C-23).

## ADR-019 — Foreground transfers are data tasks streaming into staging
- **Context.** transport.md chose download tasks. transport-2 T2-01 (critical) measured that every interrupted foreground download task leaves `CFNetworkDownload_*.tmp` in the per-user temp directory, outside the store root and invisible to GC, quota and reports; this team's probes left about 794 MiB there (still present; the human must remove them, `ARCHITECTURE.md` §15). transport-1 TR1-2, TR1-4, TR1-5 and T2-07 trace further defects to URLSession resume data (it embeds the key, fails with −1002 when stale, and lives in a private archive).
- **Options.** (a) Keep download tasks and sweep `CFNetworkDownload_*.tmp` at launch (writes outside the root, still loses partials). (b) Data tasks whose delegate appends each chunk into `staging/<name>.part` through `StagingSink` (the `FileSystem` seam), resuming with `Range` + `If-Range` from our own sidecar.
- **Decision.** (b) for every foreground transfer, manifest included. Partial bytes never leave the root; GC, quota, `StorageReport` and purge see them; resume data no longer exists on the foreground path, so no key is ever at rest in staging (ADR-T4 is moot). The size guard measures bytes written into staging and aborts early on the manifest's `byteSize` (`SizeGuard`). Only the opt-in background session (ADR-026) still uses download tasks, whose temp files the OS keeps in the daemon's space and whose completed files are moved into staging before the delegate returns. **Loopback tests and benchmarks (P-10, P-11) use only the data-task path.**
- **Consequences.** `StagingSink` gained `createPartial`, `appendPartial`, `continuePartial`; `ResumeSidecar` and `ResumePoint` replace resume data. Checksum-less transfers are never resumed (transport-1 TR1-3).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / D-14.2).** On the opt-in background path a pause's URLSession resume data is kept in memory only (never in staging), for checksummed content only, at most `maxTasks` entries (oldest dropped), and is handed once to the engine's next attempt of the same content (`BackgroundSessionDriver.startDownload(…, resumeData:)`). So "no key is ever at rest in staging" still holds, and resume data still never exists on the foreground path.

## ADR-020 — Single-flight per content (`TransferID`)
- **Context.** sync.md single-flighted per `AssetID`; transport-1 TR1-1: two manifest entries with one checksum then run two transfers into one staging name.
- **Decision.** The engine keys jobs by `TransferID` (`c-<sha256>` for checksummed content, `l-<sha256(id ‖ 0 ‖ remotePath)>` for legacy entries, `m-manifest`); interests carry the asset ids. One verified staged file is published for every id expecting that content (`AssetStore.publish(_:binding:expecting:urgency:)`). A job's lane is the maximum over its interests.
- **Consequences.** Dedupe costs zero extra downloads; per-asset waiters still resume independently (H-3).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L4 merge, 2026-10-17, ADR-053).** "Zero extra downloads" has one accepted exception: a late joiner of shared content whose new content is already live in another blob downloads it a second time, because the store has no call that binds an id to a live blob; the store dedupes the bytes at publication, and since L4-R5 that download can no longer mark anything failed. Accepted residue for Stage 3 with OI-15 (L4-R5, L4-R24(5); `f.md` F-20, F-21).
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** The accepted residue stays through Stage 3, now with S3B-11's CCR for WP-F (`bindToLiveContent`, a package primitive that binds an evicted record or a late joiner to a live key); one redundant download per such record, never a correctness bug.

## ADR-021 — `TransferHandle` with synchronous controls
- **Context.** sync.md proposed a per-transfer control stream; transport.md a handle. sync-1 S1-06: `.bufferingNewest(1)` drops a `pause` followed by `setPriority`, and promotion was not ordered before the next start; sync-2 S2-19: an unfinished control stream leaks a consumer task per transfer.
- **Decision.** `TransferHandle` (Model) with `setPriority`, `pause`, `cancel` synchronous and nonisolated, plus a single-consumer `updates` stream whose terminal element is always last. No control stream exists. A restart is a new attempt with a new handle, never a restart inside a handle (transport-2 T2-08).
- **Consequences.** The engine promotes and preempts inside synchronous actor code; `FakeTransferHandle` records controls in call order under a lock, so the R-NET-10 ordering test is deterministic.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** "No control stream exists" now holds for the Model contract only: inside Transport each background handle passes its controls through an internal, unbounded per-attach stream read by one control task (S3D-1). Neither hazard returns: S1-06's coalesced `pause` cannot happen because the stream is unbounded, and S2-19's leaked consumer cannot happen because the stream ends with the attach or the handle, as `R-SYN-14: a background handle's control task ends with its attach, however the attach ends` and its dropped-handle sibling pin.

## ADR-022 — The store owns no task: the engine's alarm drives store deadlines
- **Context.** store.md gave the store a reaper task and a deferred-commit task; sync.md's X-4 claimed either owner satisfies P-14. sync-2 S2-02 (critical): with a store-owned reaper, `blocked(.awaitingSpace)` has no wake-up. store-2 S2-03 (reaper busy loop) and S2-08 (store-owned tasks make R-STO-5 op sequences nondeterministic).
- **Decision.** `AssetStore` owns no task and no timer. It reports `nextDeadline()` (time-only blockers) and executes `runDueWork()` (coalesced commit, reap batches) when the engine's single alarm fires; `StoreWorkReport.reapedBytes` wakes `awaitingSpace` jobs. Store calls that may change a deadline (lease release, acknowledgement, commit) are routed through the engine, which re-arms its alarm.
- **Consequences.** One owner for every timer (P-14, R-SYN-14); store tests drive `runDueWork()` explicitly, so op order is deterministic.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L4 merge, 2026-10-17, ADR-053).** `reapedBytes` is not the only wake. The store may count pending reclaim for which `nextDeadline()` schedules nothing (a blocker of `.acknowledgement`, `.commit` or `.freshAcknowledgement(unanswered: true)`; reported to WP-B, Stage 3), so a job waits `awaitingSpace` only while something will run the store's due work (a store deadline, the alarm's store work, a deadline re-read, a settling read or an owed sweep); otherwise it is decided on a fresh free-space read. A purge's reclaim re-admits the jobs parked for space at once, on a fresh read (`f.md` F-25, F-26, F-28; ARCHITECTURE §7.6, §7.10).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 WP-K merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-055 / L5K-R16).** "Store calls that may change a deadline … are routed through the engine, which re-arms its alarm" widens: the engine re-reads `nextDeadline()` (RE-S40) after every store call that can leave an install due (every engine call of a store method is enumerated in `k.md` "L5K-R16"), skipping the read in O(1) after a publication while its alarm already wakes for the store within about two install intervals (F-12; the other re-arms read in full); an answer of the store's deadline applied after another update landed while it was in flight keeps the earlier deadline (`applyStoreDeadline`); and the test-only tripwire `invariantViolations(storeDeadlineDue:)` checks that a settled engine holds a store entry whenever the store reports a deadline (ARCHITECTURE §7.1, §7.8, §10.2).
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** The item reported to WP-B (ADR-053) is answered by S3-B-015: four commit failures of a pruning `apply` left `pendingReclaim` at the pruned bytes but `nextDeadline()` never `nil`; a doomed key counts with no deadline only in the documented states (unavailable, degraded, leased, acknowledgement-blocked after an unacknowledged install), which fits F-25's single sighting. The engine's fresh read stays.

## ADR-023 — Event broadcast and backpressure
- **Context.** sync.md §10 (pull-based queues). sync-2 S2-17: subject bound ignored pruned ids, bulk terminal events flood the UI, a stored stream keeps its subscription; sync-1 S1-19.
- **Decision.** Pull-based per-subscriber queues (`AsyncStream(unfolding:onCancel:)`), progress newest-wins per key at ≤ 10 Hz with a trailing emission after a quiet period, terminal events coalesced per subject and never dropped. Per-asset terminal events of **bulk** work are folded into `syncProgress`/`syncFinished` instead of being broadcast. A pruned id's subject is retired with its `assetRemoved` event. Transfer progress keys are retired with their transfer. A subscription registers on the first pull and is released on cancel, on deinit of the stream and at shutdown.
- **Consequences.** Per-subscriber memory is bounded by `progress keys (≤ max + 3) + subjects (≤ non-bulk assets with unread terminal events + 5)`; documented on `AssetEvent`.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 merge, 2026-10-22, ADR-054 / L5-R19).** `clientStarted` and `clientShutDown` have a terminal slot each (`EventSlot.client`, `.clientEnd`) and never coalesce with each other, so a shutdown never replaces an unread `clientStarted`; `clientShutDown` is still the last event, because nothing is published after it. The per-subscriber bound grows by that one constant slot: `progress keys (≤ max + 3) + subjects (≤ non-bulk assets with unread terminal events + 6)`. The facade, which hides a retired engine's `clientShutDown` across a reopen, relies on it (ARCHITECTURE §7.9, §8; `AssetEvent`'s doc; test `R-SYN-12: a subscriber that reads nothing between clientStarted and clientShutDown receives both, clientStarted first`).

## ADR-024 — Keyed randomness
- **Context.** sync-2 S2-16: one RNG consumed in actor-arrival order gave 3 different schedules for one seed in 200 runs.
- **Decision.** `RandomSource` exposes one seed; every draw is `KeyedRandom.next64(seed:key:counter:)` keyed by what is drawn (asset, attempt, breaker trip), never by draw order. `StableHash` (FNV-1a) derives keys reproducibly across processes.
- **Consequences.** `Backoff.delay` is implemented this way and tested (`R-SYN-5: backoff delays are full jitter keyed by asset and attempt and reproducible from the seed`).

## ADR-025 — Over-budget catalogs admit the largest fitting prefix
- **Context.** sync-2 S2-07: an all-or-nothing space check never syncs anything, not even `essential` assets, when the catalog exceeds the default budget; S2-06 and S2-08 (spurious failures with nothing to fetch, unknown sizes estimated at 8 MiB).
- **Decision.** `StoragePolicy.admitLargestFittingPrefix` (default `true`): the planner admits the longest prefix of the stable fetch order (essential first) that fits after permitted eviction, and skips the rest as `overBudget`; with `false`, an over-budget plan fails with no side effect (R-SYN-11). A plan with zero fetch bytes always fits. Unknown sizes are not estimated in the plan; the per-fetch headroom ledger admits them.
- **Consequences.** A run that skipped entries as `overBudget` never ends `.complete`, so the stamp is not written and the sync stays due; the report lists the skipped bytes.

## ADR-026 — Background `URLSession` and app-key exposure · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** R-NET-3 (SHOULD) wants a background session for bulk transfers. In a background session the OS follows redirects without calling the delegate (NSURLSession.h:1732), so the app key is sent to any redirect target (transport.md E-13), and neither the 5-hop limit (R-NET-4) nor the size cap (R-NET-7) can be enforced while the app is not running (transport-2 T2-09).
- **Options.** (a) No background session. (b) Background always on (violates R-NET-4/R-NET-7, MUSTs). (c) Built, off by default, used only for bulk, key-free requests, unless the owner opts in.
- **Decision.** (c). `BackgroundTransferPolicy.disabled` is the default. `.enabled(options)` requires the app to forward the AppDelegate hook. `KeyRedirectExposure.refuse` (default) keeps every key-bearing request in the foreground; `.acceptedByOwner` is the owner's explicit acceptance that, for background bulk transfers only, the key may follow OS redirects and the hop limit and size cap are checked only after the fact (bytes from a cross-origin redirect are discarded). R-NET-4 tests cover the default `.refuse`.
- **Consequences.** With today's AWS tier (key always required) production bulk transfers stay foreground unless the owner opts in. The owner must decide whether to keep `.acceptedByOwner` in the public API or remove it.
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 3): production wiring.** `HTTPRemoteSource(configuration:staging:log:)` builds its background backend with `makeBackgroundBackend(_:staging:policy:)`: `nil` when disabled or the namespace is invalid, else `BackgroundTransferCoordinator(driver:staging:options:)` over `LiveBackgroundSessionDriver` and a `.background` `SessionDelegate` (construction only; the session is created by `activate()` after the store opened, ADR-028). `SessionDelegate` carries a session-event stream created in `init` (`sessionEvents`), the delivery path for background completions and for the foreground `didBecomeInvalidWithError` that `invalidate()` waits for (transport-2 T2-04). WP-D owns every Transport body, Stage 1 ones included. Background-delegate tests call the delegate methods directly with unresumed tasks and files inside the repository, never real download tasks (ARCHITECTURE §14).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / D-7, D-8, D-14, D-15, D-19).** `makeBackgroundBackend(_:staging:policy:appKey:requestTimeout:log:makeDriver:)` (every added parameter defaulted) passes the app key to the coordinator only when `keyRedirectExposure == .acceptedByOwner`, independently of routing, so under the default `.refuse` no background request can carry the key even if a routing defect sent one there. The source routes to the background only `.backgroundEligible` + `.bulk` + `.asset` transfers without a Range resume whose `byteSize` is known, ≥ 0 and ≤ the cap (the OS downloads a background task whole, so only the staged size can be checked), that carry no key unless the owner accepted redirect exposure, and only through `startIfAdmitted(_:)`, which checks the task cap and reserves a slot in one actor turn; anything else runs in the foreground with background left enabled. A background pause keeps URLSession resume data in memory for checksummed content and the engine's restart continues from it once (ADR-019's amendment); resume data embeds the archived request, so a continuation sends the key that was current when the task was created (a key change builds a new source and coordinator, whose table starts empty). A background 206 is accepted only for a task created from resume data for checksummed content, a fact carried in the task tag `ztak1 <name> <size | -> [resume]` across relaunch (D-19.1, D-19.2). The round-3 amendment's `BackgroundTransferCoordinator(driver:staging:options:)` is now the keyless package initialiser; production uses `init(driver:staging:options:policy:appKey:requestTimeout:log:)`.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 merge, 2026-10-22, ADR-054 / L5-R5).** `.enabled(options)` is the app's attestation that it forwards the hook, and in Stage 2 that attestation is what "wired" means for R-NET-3's fallback: the default `.disabled` keeps every transfer in the foreground (test `R-NET-3: background falls back to foreground when the background-events hook is not wired`). The run-time detection of an attested but unwired hook (transport.md F-26; CCR-G1: disable background transfers on an unclaimed `.backgroundEventsFinished`) is declined: it would misfire on a finish that arrives before anybody waits, which `BackgroundTransferCoordinator.awaitBackgroundEvents()` tolerates (`unclaimedFinishes`) and the facade's asynchronous hand-off makes normal. The run-time half is Not verified and goes into the device test plan; Stage 3 may revisit it only with a detection that cannot misfire on an early finish. No WP-D change.

## ADR-027 — Apple-tier pack retention (H-7) · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** No stable pack-file URL exists (APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §1.4); removal is whole-pack (§1.5). transport-2 T2-13: "release when every entry is adopted" never fires with a scope or quota; pack bytes are invisible to quota and reports. store-1 S1-10: releasing after a non-durable publish loses the asset on power loss.
- **Decision.** Adopt-and-release: stream the pack file into staging, hash, publish into `blobs/`; release a media pack (never the catalog pack) once every catalog entry mapped to it is adopted **in a durably committed generation** or excluded by the keep-scope (`PackReleasePlanner`, implemented and tested), at most once per pack per catalog generation. Default `PackRetention.releaseAfterAdoption`; `.keepPacks` keeps the prior art's double residency. `StorageReport.sourceCopyBytes` reports bytes the OS still holds. Apple-adopted assets rank after ordinary evictable ones, because evicting one after its pack was released costs a whole-pack re-download.
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 2): the seams behind this decision.**
  - *Keep-scope = the client's retention scope.* `AssetClientConfiguration.retentionScope: AssetScope` (default `.all`) and `AssetClient.setRetentionScope(_:)` carry what the app wants kept (R-CAR-5: the paywall maps to it at launch and whenever it changes). The engine uses it as the scope of every bulk run; the store (`AssetStore.setRetentionScope(_:)`, in-memory, pushed by the engine after `open()`) evicts entries outside it before any entry inside it and lists them in `AdoptionReport.excludedRemotePaths`, so a pack whose remaining entries the paywall excludes is released (transport-2 T2-13). Changing the scope deletes nothing and starts no run; `purge(.scope(_:))` deletes, `ensureAvailable(scope:)`/`sync(.force)` fetch.
  - *Atomic durable adoption report.* `AssetStore.adoptionReport()` captures the present records, the applied manifest and the exclusions synchronously, commits the holding generation durably, and returns only what it captured (REENTRANCY R15), so a publish that lands during the commit is never reported adopted (store-1 S1-10).
  - *Source-held bytes reach the report through the Model.* `RemoteSource.sourceCopyBytes()` (0 for HTTP; the Apple source sums `PackBackend.installedBytes` of adopted, unreleased packs); `SyncEngine.storageReport()` fills `StorageReport.sourceCopyBytes` with it, because Sync reaches AppleHosted only through Model protocols.
- **Consequences.** Double residency is bounded by one pack between first adoption and release. Owner decides the default and confirms the retention-scope semantics (no proactive deletion).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, 2026-10-09, ADR-050): the rule applies literally.**
  - *Once per generation, never re-armed.* A pack is released at most once per pack per catalog generation (the catalog pseudo-ETag) and is never re-armed within a generation (E-10(b)'s re-arm is withdrawn). A pack downloaded again on demand after its release stays installed until a reclaim in a later catalog generation releases it. Released packs are tracked in memory, once per process; persisting them needs a store field (an index format change) and is deferred, so after a relaunch a pack the OS holds again may be released once more in that process (bounded, never a loop).
  - *Source-held bytes (amends the bullet above).* `RemoteSource.sourceCopyBytes()` for the Apple source sums `PackBackend.installedBytes` (`nil` and negative count 0; the sum saturates at `Int64.max`) over adopted packs, released or not (the packs holding adopted content in the last `AdoptionReport`), and packs whose planned removal has not yet succeeded (this set lands with the L2 follow-up `wp/l2fu`; at 645caee the sum covers only the adopted and the released packs), and over every pack it released in this process (a removed pack reports no bytes; one that came back counts until a later generation releases it). The set lives in memory: a fresh source reports 0 until its first `reclaimSourceCopies`. Doc-only; no signature changed.
  - *Consequences (amended).* Double residency lasts from first adoption until release, and also from an on-demand re-download after release until a reclaim in a later catalog generation releases it (at most one download and one removal per pack per generation, never a loop). A pack id can appear in `ReclaimReport.released` again in a later generation: the engine treats `released` as "removed now", not as a set it accumulates.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L3 merge, 2026-10-10, ADR-052 / C-6, ruling R5).** "Apple-adopted assets rank after ordinary evictable ones" is not applied in the store: records carry no tier and a store serves one tier at a time, so eviction ranks every blob alike (accepted as documented, no review tag). A store that mixes tiers (a kill switch flipped) would need a per-record flag, an index-format change (NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW if ever proposed).

## ADR-028 — Launch reconciliation order and staging GC
- **Context.** transport-2 T2-03 (critical): staging listed before the background session existed loses work the OS delivers in between; an offline launch's empty plan cancelled every live task. store-2 S2-11, store-1 S1-17, transport.md X-T6.
- **Decision.** `start()` runs: `store.open()` (lock, load, reconcile blobs; staging **not** collected) → build the remote source → `activate()` the background session if enabled → `reattachedTransfers()` → `store.stagingInventory()` → `TransferReconciler.plan(live:staged:wanted:)` with `wanted` = applied entries that are not present (never the run's network plan) → execute → `store.collectStaging(keeping:)`, which also keeps any unclaimed file younger than `stagingClaimGrace` (1 h). A complete `.part` makes a `.resume` for the same transfer garbage.
- **Consequences.** H-6's "not lost, not duplicated" holds in the most common case; the fake background driver delivers completions only on `activate()` so the test encodes the real OS order.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L3 merge, 2026-10-10, ADR-052 / C-9).** `collectStaging(keeping:)` keeps only the caller's claims and unclaimed files younger than `stagingClaimGrace`; the store adds no reservations of its own, so the engine claims every transfer it still works on, those with a publication in flight included. A name of ours older than `stagingMaxAge` is collected even when claimed, a foreign name or anything but a regular file at once; a file without a modification time, or with one in the future, is kept; launch epochs are never compared.

## ADR-029 — Legacy migration phase 2 runs after `open()`
- **Context.** store-2 S2-09 and sync-2 S2-11: hashing the whole legacy catalog inside `open()` blocks every API (seconds on the upgrade launch) and the "publish every 64" snapshots would precede reconciliation.
- **Decision.** `open()` performs lock, load, reconciliation and legacy phase 1 (plan from `manifest.json` + `local-checksums.json`, one ordered commit), then publishes the first snapshot. Phase 2 (hash and rename) runs as an engine-owned, resumable task through `AssetStore.migrateLegacy(maxFiles:)`, publishing progressively; a `resolve` or lease of a legacy id not yet adopted calls `adoptLegacyNow` first (one hash, far cheaper than a download). Phases 3–4 run when nothing remains. The UserDefaults key is removed only after a durable "done" commit.
- **Consequences.** Lookups hit adopted legacy files early; no snapshot precedes reconciliation.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L3 merge, 2026-10-10, ADR-052 / C-12…C-15, C-24, ruling R9).** "Phases 3–4 run when nothing remains": phase 3 may leave its decision to a later call (`remaining == 0 && !finished` after three interrupted passes of its chunked check), and the engine calls again while `!progress.finished`, backing off on such a return (R9). Phase "done" is committed durably before phase 4 on every call, and the key goes only once the tree is done. The deviations from store.md §7 (C-12…C-15 and the C-14 deferral bound) are ADR-052's, tagged NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 WP-K merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-055).** "A `resolve` or lease of a legacy id not yet adopted calls `adoptLegacyNow` first" holds for every job's pre-transfer read while migration is pending (L5K-R1), at the job's lane's urgency (`adoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)`, L5K-R14), with one adoption per legacy file whoever asks (L5K-R13). "Publishing progressively": a batch that finds the queue empty and every claimed file an adopt-on-demand's waits for one such processing at a time instead of returning at once and finishes in the same call, unless an adoption it waited for put its file back in the queue (its caller cancelled, a transient failure), which the next call processes (L5K-R17), and no batch suspends behind its own coalesced publication: with files left once its publication returned it returns right after it, otherwise it installs at once (L5K-R20, L5K-R23). "Lookups hit adopted legacy files early" holds once each adoption's snapshot is installed (K-15: on a launch during migration the shims' lookups miss a prior-art file until then; ARCHITECTURE §4.7, §7.1).

## ADR-030 — Manifest application API
- **Context.** sync.md planned a `ReconcileStep` applied with `expectedGeneration`; store.md applied a manifest itself. sync-1 S1-12: a whole-snapshot generation guard makes `ensureAvailable` fail under a running bulk sync. X-1, X-2, X-3.
- **Decision.** `AssetStore.apply(_:etag:eviction:)` diffs against current state (no generation guard) using the same `ManifestDiff`; a seeded property test pins `ApplyReport == SyncPlan.reconcile`. Space is decided before any mutation with the dry run `evictionPlan(toFree:protecting:)`; the plan is re-validated per victim inside `apply`. Publication is one call with a per-entry content guard (`publish(_:binding:expecting:urgency:)` → `.superseded`).
- **Consequences.** Concurrent commits never force re-plans; R-SYN-11's "no partial side effects" still holds because the space decision precedes `apply`.

## ADR-031 — Circuit-breaker liveness and the 403 family
- **Context.** sync-1 S1-03 and sync-2 S2-01 (critical): a half-open probe that is cancelled or fails for another cause wedges the breaker forever. sync-2 S2-05: an ambiguous object 403 was quarantined until the entry changes. transport-1 TR1-5/6/7/11.
- **Decision.** Probe resolution is total: success closes; a same-cause failure re-opens with `trips + 1`; any other exit (different cause, offline, cancelled, paused, superseded, shut down) returns to `halfOpen(probe: nil)`; a watchdog deadline (`CircuitBreakerPolicy.probeWatchdog`, 60 s) re-arms a silent probe; the probe is never a preemption victim. An ambiguous object 403 fails the asset for the run (`.nextRun`) and feeds the auth-suspect counter; it never quarantines. A 403 after our own key stripping is `rejected(.keyWithheldOnRedirect)`, never key rejection; 401 and −1012/−1013 are `transient(.authenticationChallenge)` (proxies, captive portals). The 403 hint checks body shape before purpose.
- **Consequences.** `CircuitBreaker.Outcome.abandoned` and `.neutral` exist in the contract.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L4 merge, 2026-10-17, ADR-053; L4-R20, L4-R25).** The exits without a verdict are a 404, a 400, an integrity failure, offline, a different cause and abandoned (cancelled, paused, superseded, shut down). Each returns to `halfOpen(probe: nil)`, re-admits in that turn every job the breaker parked, drops the old probe's watchdog and owes a pump. A probe past its watchdog lets the gate admit the next transfer lazily (`CircuitBreaker.admission(of:at:)`), and a manifest probe schedules its watchdog like a transfer probe. A run that starts while the breaker admits only a probe holds that probe for its manifest fetch from its start turn, unless the origin is throttled at `now` or the run joins a manifest fetch already past the gates (`f.md` F-40, F-41, F-42; ARCHITECTURE §7.7).

## ADR-032 — Integrity bookkeeping lives in the store
- **Context.** sync-2 S2-04: integrity counters in the engine's `Job` die with it, and the interactive retry cap (2) is below `maxIntegrityAttempts` (3), so every visit to a corrupt asset re-downloads it. transport-2 T2-12: Apple-tier catalog/pack skew classified as integrity quarantines good content.
- **Decision.** `IntegrityHistory` is persisted per record (`AssetStore.recordIntegrityFailure`); the engine reads `nextEligibleAt` and never fetches earlier, user-initiated requests included. Catalog/pack skew is `transient(.sourceVersionSkew)`, which triggers one best-effort update check and never quarantines.
- **Consequences.** No loop survives a relaunch (D-16).
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 3).** (1) *404/410 is persisted too.* `AssetStore.recordFailure(_:identity:reason:)` (WP-C) marks records still at `identity` `failed(reason)` (`.notFound` for R-NET-6, `.unreadable`) and persists it; `apply` (WP-B) clears `failed` and `quarantined` and resets `IntegrityHistory` when an entry's content identity changes. So `RetryDisposition.untilEntryChanges` really is persisted for both causes ARCHITECTURE §7.6 names, and a relaunch never re-requests a 404. Test `R-NET-6: a 404 is not requested again until its manifest entry changes, across a relaunch` (WP-F). (2) *One recording path.* The engine records integrity bookkeeping (`recordIntegrityFailure`) for transport **and** store integrity failures; `publish` deletes the staged bytes, counts the metric and throws, and never calls the WP-C stub itself (ADR-046).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L4 merge, 2026-10-17, ADR-053; L4-R5, L4-R16).** `recordFailure(_:identity:reason:)` leaves a record bound to a live blob at `identity` unchanged (a failed transfer of content the store already serves never fails it), while a stale record still fails with its old blob doomed `unreferenced`; the engine also re-reads presence before the call (row f-10). `recordIntegrityFailure` has no such guard yet (L4-R24(3), Stage 3). WP-F made the edit in WP-C's file under authorisation; ownership is unchanged.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** Since Stage 3, `recordIntegrityFailure` has the same guard: a record bound to a live blob at the failed identity is left unchanged (L4-R24(3); Stage 3 ruling S3C-9).

## ADR-033 — R-CAR-6 compatibility shims live in `Tests/ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests` · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** ADR-006 moved R-CAR-6 into this repository; Package.swift had no target for it.
- **Decision.** A test target `ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests` (depends on `ZTronAssetKit`, `ZTronAssetTestSupport`) holds the shim sources under `Shims/` and the R-CAR-6 tests. Being a test target, the shims never ship in the product, Combine (`CDNSyncCoordinator` is an `ObservableObject` in the prior art) stays out of every production target (R-CAR-9), and the unsafe and docs gates (which scan `Sources/`) do not apply to them. The owner copies `Shims/` into ZTronCarouselCore when adopting (MIGRATION.md). Symbols: `CDNAsset`, `CDNSyncProgress`, `CDNRefreshPolicy`, `CDNProviding` (this round), and the adapters over `AssetClient` (written by WP-K, round-4 amendment): `CDNLocalStore`, `CDNAssetResolver.installed`, `CDNSyncCoordinator`, `NetworkAvailabilityMonitor`, `CDNProviderFactory`/`CDNConfiguration`. `CDNRefreshPolicy`, `CDNProviderFactory` and `CDNConfiguration` do not exist in the prior art on disk (RECON §1); they are provided because SPEC R-CAR-6 names them.
- **Consequences.** Q-10 stays "not verified" in this repository (ADR-006); the shim public API is the owner's to review.
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 4).** The adapters over `AssetClient` are written by **WP-K** (compatibility shims, level L5 after WP-G), which owns `Tests/ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests/**` except `CompatibilityShimTests.swift` (ADR-046), not by WP-G; ARCHITECTURE §11 assigns R-CAR-6 to K. WP-G's facade is merged before K starts, so K needs no WP-G file.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 WP-K merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-055).** WP-K wrote the adapters (K-1…K-15). `NetworkAvailabilityMonitor` adapts `AssetEnvironment.conditions` (`DeviceConditionsSource`), not an `AssetClient` (K-8, L5K-R7: an idle client observes nothing; `shared` builds its own live source, a second path monitor in the process). The shim public API gains `CDNInstallPoint` (K-2) and `CDNShimError` (K-5, K-9), the explicit-client initialisers `CDNLocalStore.init(client:)`, `init(installPoint:)`, `CDNAssetResolver.init(client:)` and `CDNSyncCoordinator.init(client:cdn:)`, `CDNSyncCoordinator.lastReconciliation` (K-7), `NetworkAvailabilityMonitor.init(conditions:)` (K-8, its only public initialiser), and `Equatable`/`Hashable` on the providers' error enums (K-5), among others (the complete list is in ADR-055's "Ratified contract changes" (5)); the shim public API stays the owner's to review under this ADR's tag, with K-2, K-3, K-5, K-6 and K-15 tagged in ADR-055. The shims import only `Foundation`, `ZTronAssetKit` and, for the coordinator, `Combine`, with no `package` member and no `@testable` (K-1), so they stay copyable into ZTronCarouselCore. `docs/MIGRATION.md` is WP-K's file (L5K-R10).

## ADR-034 — Checksum-less content identity follows `remotePath` only
- **Context.** R-MAN-5: entries without a checksum are re-downloaded "only when remotePath changes". The Stage 1 Model classified a `byteSize`-only change as `contentChanged`, while `TransferID.legacy` ignored the size, so the diff and the single-flight key disagreed.
- **Options.** (a) Drop `byteSize` from `ContentIdentity.unverified`. (b) Add `byteSize` to `TransferID.legacy` and deviate from R-MAN-5.
- **Decision.** (a): `ContentIdentity.unverified(remotePath:)`. A size-only change is metadata; the stored blob keeps existence-only trust.
- **Consequences.** Tested: `R-MAN-5: a checksum-less entry changes content identity only when its remotePath changes`.

## ADR-035 — Storage policy defaults · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Decision.** `StoragePolicy()` defaults: budget `.fractionOfFreeSpace(0.5)`; `reserveFreeBytes` 256 MiB; hysteresis 0.9; `minimumResidency` 24 h; `deletionGrace` 10 s (awake time, ADR-017); `orphanGrace` 1 h; `stagingClaimGrace` 1 h (ADR-028); `stagingMaxAge` 72 h; integrity 3 attempts, 60 s × 10; `allowUnverifiedAssets` true (compatibility); `admitLargestFittingPrefix` true (ADR-025). Store mechanics: commits ≤ 4/s (0.25 s), deletion units of 64, hash units of 1 MiB.
- **Consequences.** Product choices the owner should confirm; every value is configurable.
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 3).** `evictionPermitted` true (ADR-048): a run may evict to make room; maintenance quota eviction runs regardless.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / B-10, B-29).** Deletion units are at most `deletionBatchSize` (64, configurable) unlinks **and** at most `BlobDeletionUnit.budget` = 1 ms of the injected monotonic clock. A scheduled commit after failures backs off to at most 5 minutes (`maxCommitBackoff`). Both are store constants, not configuration: "every value is configurable" above does not hold for them.

## ADR-036 — Network and power policy defaults · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Decision.** `NetworkPolicy()`: bulk on expensive networks yes (prior-art parity), bulk in Low Data Mode no, prefetch on expensive yes, prefetch in Low Data Mode no, user-initiated overrides the network policy. `PowerPolicy()`: bulk in Low Power Mode no, prefetch yes, background lanes pause at `.serious` and resume at `.fair` (hysteresis, sync-2 S2-25), one user-initiated transfer at `.critical`. `SyncTuning()`: concurrency 4 (1…16), one reserved interactive slot, pipeline depth 2, bulk aging 30 s, progress ≤ 10 Hz, lookahead 16, pending-run debounce 5 s, manifest fetch deadline 30 s, bulk snapshot installs ≤ 10 Hz.
- **Consequences.** Product choices the owner should confirm.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** S3F-7(b) (S3-CONF-04; a record, no code): the one user-initiated transfer at `.critical` applies to dispatch decisions taken with the conditions known; user-initiated work dispatched while they are unknown (after the idle teardown) is not trimmed when a critical thermal reading then arrives. S3F-7(a)'s test pins the cap once the conditions are known, and `interactiveConcurrencyAtCritical`'s doc says so (`NetworkPolicy.swift:40-41`).

## ADR-037 — Facade deviations from the prior art's fallback chain · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**
- **Context.** sync.md R-6, R-7; sync-2 S2-13 (the bundle step did file-system work on main, 14–18 ms, invisible to P-8).
- **Decision.** (1) `lookup` finds local assets only after `start()` installed the first snapshot (the prior art read its manifest on main, D-15); early pages fall through to the asynchronous step, which completes from disk. (2) The extension filter applies at the local step too, so an `.mp4` is never handed to an image decoder. (3) The bundle step reads an index built off the main thread by `AssetFallbackChain.prepare(kinds:)` through `BundleLookup`; asset catalogs stay in the carousel's UIKit adapter.
- **Consequences.** Behavioural differences the shims document in MIGRATION.md.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** A fourth deviation of the fallback chain is K-11's (accepted by ADR-055, documented in `MIGRATION.md:220`): `fetch` and the shim's `resolveViaNetwork` check the candidate extension after `resolve` returned, so an entry of another kind is downloaded at `.userInitiated` and stored before `notFound`; the prior art checked the manifest entry first. S3-CONF-07 is refuted as a defect on that ground (S3G-4); whether K-11 joins this tag is the owner's information item in ADR-057.

## ADR-038 — Environment protocols live in the Model
- **Context.** SPEC §4.2 wants one `AssetEnvironment` value carrying file system, time, randomness, reachability, power and remote sources; SPEC §4.1 places the `FileSystem` abstraction in Storage; `.live()` must name conformers from Storage, Transport and AppleHosted.
- **Decision.** The protocols (`FileSystem` and its value types, `BlockingExecutor`, `TimeSource`, `RandomSource`, `DeviceConditionsSource`, `RemoteSourceFactory`, `AppVersionSource`, `LegacyDefaults`, `LogTap`, `MemoryPressureSource`) and `AssetEnvironment` live in `ZTronAssetModel`. Live conformers live where SPEC §4.1 puts their effects: `LiveFileSystem` and `LiveLegacyDefaults` in Storage (the only POSIX/`FileManager`/`UserDefaults` code), `LiveDeviceConditionsSource` in Transport, `LiveAssetPackBackend` in AppleHosted, `LiveTimeSource`, `SystemRandomSource` and `DispatchBlockingExecutor` in the Model. `AssetEnvironment.live()` is assembled in `ZTronAssetKit`, the composition root. **This also fixes ADR-003's interface:** `BlockingExecutor.run(_:)` takes a non-throwing `@Sendable` unit; typed errors travel as `Result` and `run(throwing:)` rethrows them, because a typed-throws closure must never become runtime metadata at the iOS 15 floor (verified: full compile and link at `arm64-apple-ios15.0`).
- **Consequences.** R-STO-6 holds (one conformer touches the file system); the facade's `init(configuration:environment: = .live())` performs no I/O (since round 3 `environment: AssetEnvironment? = nil`, resolving to `.live(legacyDefaultsSuiteName:)`, ADR-048).
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 2).** (1) *Scope of R-STO-6.* R-STO-6 governs the **store's** effects: no production code outside `LiveFileSystem` touches `FileManager` or POSIX for the store root. The other real-world effects are each behind their own seam with exactly one live conformer, built only by `.live()`: `Bundle` (`MainBundleLookup`, `LiveAppVersionSource`), `UserDefaults` (`LiveLegacyDefaults`), the pack `FileHandle` (`LiveAssetPackBackend`), URLSession (`HTTPRemoteSource`'s backends), `NWPathMonitor` and power notifications (`LiveDeviceConditionsSource`). (2) *C2 for those seams.* Their blocking first reads run in one `BlockingExecutor` unit, never on main or the cooperative pool: `AssetFallbackChain.prepare(kinds:)` enumerates the bundle on the client's bulk executor (sync-2 measured 14–18 ms), and `LiveAppVersionSource(executor:)` reads `Info.plist` on the metadata executor. (3) *No `static var`.* `AssetEnvironment.live` became `static func live(legacyDefaultsSuiteName: String? = nil)`; SPEC §4.2's literal "no `static var`" holds and every call builds fresh conformers, so two clients never share executors or observers.

## ADR-039 — Engine-owned entry tasks and shutdown discipline
- **Context.** sync-1 S1-04 (critical): shutdown closed the store under a purge asleep in its grace window and under `start()`; S1-08 (shared work in a caller's task); S1-09 (owned-task keys reused); store-1 S1-09 (close races unit continuations).
- **Decision.** Every store-touching entry point (`start`, `purge`, `ensureAvailable`, `performMaintenance`, `reportUnreadable`, `setPinned`, `setRetentionScope`, leases, migration) runs as an engine-owned task, keyed by a unique spawn id, joined by its caller through the waiter pattern; a caller's cancellation withdraws only its interest. `shutdown()`: lifecycle `shuttingDown` → cancel and join every owned task (the memory-pressure observer included) → resume every continuation table with `.shutDown` → `remote.invalidate()` → `store.close()` (drains in-flight units, durable flush, unlock) → `clientShutDown` → `shutDown`. `invalidate()` returns after the **foreground** delegate's last callback (transport-2 T2-04) and after the Apple source's in-flight `writeStaged` calls; the opt-in background session is released without waiting for the OS's tasks (contract-fix round 2: `finishTasksAndInvalidate` would make shutdown wait for hours), and a file it moves into staging after close is an ordinary staged file for the next launch's reconciliation (ADR-028). `StagingSink` therefore promises nothing after close (an immutable sink cannot observe it without a lock). Shutdown in `created` never opens the store. Inside the store every resumption re-checks the phase. After shutdown every API fails with `.shutDown` (ADR-042).
- **Consequences.** R-SYN-15's "no task leaks" is provable with the census (`EngineIntrospection.ownedTasks`, `ManualTimeSource.pendingSleepers`, `FakeRemoteSource.activeTransfers`, `FakeDeviceConditionsSource.activeObservers`).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L2 merge, ADR-050 / D-10 · NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW): shutdown and background downloads.** Invalidation releases the background session without cancelling the OS's tasks (`finishTasksAndInvalidate`), but shutdown cancels the engine's transfer drivers before `remote.invalidate()`, and terminating a background handle's stream before its terminal element cancels the OS task (the Model's drop-to-cancel rule), so an explicit `shutdown()` cancels in-flight background downloads; a killed or suspended process cancels nothing. The cancellation is a best-effort hop: one that reaches the coordinator after `invalidate()` began cancels nothing. If background downloads should survive an explicit `shutdown()`, the Model's "terminating the stream cancels the transfer" (`TransferHandle`) needs a background exception: the owner decides (the coordinator cannot tell a consumer that went away from an engine about to invalidate it).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 merge, 2026-10-22, ADR-054 / L5-R2).** From every state, `created` included, `shutdown()` cancels and joins every owned task and resumes every continuation with `.shutDown` before it finishes: `spawn` allows owned tasks in `created`, and three sites are reachable there (a group's join, the memory-pressure observer, and the release follow-up, which continues in any state but shutdown). From `created` it still never opens the store and never logs: there is no source to invalidate and no store to close, and a joined join's `ensureStarted()` refuses during `shuttingDown` (`Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineCreatedShutdownTests.swift`; G-14). The facade's `shutdown()` and `deinit` go through its lifecycle: no reopen afterwards, a running reopen awaited, then the current engine's `shutdown()` (ARCHITECTURE §7.10).
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).**
  - D-10's cancellation now travels through the handle's control task, behind any control made before it, such as a priority change still listing; one that reaches the coordinator after `invalidate()` began still cancels nothing (S3D-6, S3-B-052).
  - Stage 3 (S3F-4, S3-PERF-01; S3F-14, S3-B-070): `shutdown()`'s order is now `shuttingDown`; cancel every owned task; cancel the running attempts' handles; drain and settle the continuations and tables, `shutdownStepsPerTurn` (512) steps a turn; join each owned task, draining again after each; `remote.invalidate()`; `closeStore()` (marks `closingStore`, then `store.close()`); `clientShutDown`; `shutDown`. Answers a resolution already owed resume with their stored values, not `.shutDown`. The Decision's "cancel and join every owned task … → resume every continuation table with `.shutDown`" is superseded by that order (ARCHITECTURE §7.10's Shutdown; REENTRANCY RE-S43; `SyncEngine.shutdown()`'s doc).

## ADR-040 — Test-support placement
- **Context.** `ZTronAssetTestSupport` depends on Model and Storage only (SPEC §4.1). Some fakes implement protocols declared elsewhere; P-6/P-7 memory probes need `task_info`, i.e. a mutable pointer.
- **Decision.** `RemoteSource`, `TransferBackend`, `StagingSink` and every environment protocol are in the Model, so their fakes live in TestSupport. `FakePackBackend` lives in `Tests/ZTronAssetAppleHostedTests` (its protocol is in AppleHosted) and `FakeBackgroundSessionDriver` in `Tests/ZTronAssetTransportTests`; Kit-level doubles (`RecordingBundleLookup`) live in `Tests/ZTronAssetKitTests`. Memory-measurement helpers live in `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`, which the unsafe gate does not scan; they never move into `Sources/`.
- **Consequences.** TestSupport keeps 0 escapes and its declared dependencies.

## ADR-041 — Swift Testing traits and the macOS floor
- **Context.** APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §3.2 said `.timeLimit` needs macOS 13 and proposed per-test gating or raising the floor. The Stage 1 contract reviewer found that SwiftPM builds test targets at macOS 14.
