- **Decision.** Verified in this round: the build manifest links the 8 test bundles at `arm64-apple-macos14.0` and the 7 libraries at `macos12.0`; an ungated test with `.timeLimit(.minutes(1))` builds under `-warnings-as-errors` and passes. Test targets use `.timeLimit` ungated; the macOS floor stays 12 (ADR-005); Swift Testing traits stay out of the `ZTronAssetTestSupport` library (which builds at macOS 12). APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §3.2 is corrected.
- **Consequences.** No floor change; no per-test gating for the time limit.
- **Amendment (iOS Simulator test build, 2026-10-09, ADR-051).** The same split holds on iOS: `xcodebuild build-for-testing` for the simulator builds the 7 libraries (`ZTronAssetTestSupport` included) at `arm64-apple-ios15.0-simulator` and the 8 test bundles (Benchmarks included) at `arm64-apple-ios17.0-simulator`, so `.timeLimit` (iOS 16) also compiles ungated in test bundles on iOS and no `iOS 16.0` gate is used. This holds for Xcode 27.0 (27A266a); if a toolchain ever builds test bundles at the package floor, the simulator build flags every ungated `.timeLimit`, which then takes `iOS 16.0` beside `macOS 13.0` (every toolchain change re-runs the simulator build).

---

## Contract-fix round 2

ADR-042…045 were written by the contract author of contract-fix round 2, resolving the independent review of the Stage 1 contracts (12 issues, 2 blocking). Every issue and its disposition is in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §13.2. ADR-014, ADR-027, ADR-038 and ADR-039 carry round-2 amendments.

## ADR-042 — After `shutdown()` every API fails with `.shutDown` (R-SYN-15) · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (public signatures)
- **Context.** R-SYN-15 (MUST): "any API used afterwards fails with a typed error". The round-1 facade declared `availability`, `isSyncDue`, `lastSyncedAt`, `storageReport`, `performMaintenance`, `reportUnreadable`, `metrics`, `diagnosticReport`, `subscribe` and `start` non-throwing, so after shutdown they could only return zeros or do nothing; `prefetch` was silent too. SPEC §4.5's sketch (illustrative; "contracts may not" change) also shows `availability`, `storageReport`, `performMaintenance` and `start` non-throwing, so the SPEC conflicted with itself.
- **Options.** (a) Make those APIs `async throws(AssetError)` and have the engine return `Result<_, AssetError>` (as `purge` and `ensureAvailable` already did; R-ENV-6, ADR-004). (b) Narrow R-SYN-15 by ADR: queries return empty reports, commands become no-ops.
- **Decision.** (a). Every async facade API except `sync` and `shutdown` is `async throws(AssetError)` and throws `.shutDown` after shutdown: `start` (also `.storageUnavailable` when the store cannot open), `resolve`, `availability`, `ensureAvailable`, `subscribe`, `isSyncDue`, `lastSyncedAt`, `setRetentionScope`, `pin`, `unpin`, `storageReport`, `purge`, `performMaintenance` (now returns its `MaintenanceReport`, `@discardableResult`), `reportUnreadable`, `metrics`, `diagnosticReport`; `withAsset` throws it untyped (ADR-004). The engine methods behind them return `Result`. The remaining APIs fail in the typed way their shape allows: `sync` returns `SyncOutcome.failed(.shutDown)`; `prefetch` stays synchronous (P-8) and its handle's new `awaitRegistration() async throws(AssetError)` reports `.shutDown` while the handle holds nothing; `register(_:)` (main actor) throws `.shutDown`; a new `events` stream yields `clientShutDown` and finishes; `lookup` returns `nil` (mirror reset). Releases stay idempotent no-ops (`PrefetchHandle.cancel()`, `unregister(_:)`, lease release) and so does `shutdown()` itself; `handleBackgroundEvents` completes UIKit's handler at once.
- **Consequences.** Callers write `try await` on queries; the compatibility shims (`CDNSyncCoordinator` and friends) absorb it. Test `R-SYN-15: every API called after shutdown fails with shutDown` (WP-F/WP-G, ARCHITECTURE §11) walks the whole list; `R-SYN-15: a prefetch handle made after shutdown reports shutDown and holds nothing` exists.

## ADR-043 — Logging lives in the Model; lines are rendered with hashed identifiers (R-OBS-1)
- **Context.** `AssetLog` (subsystem, loggers, signposters) lived in the facade, which Storage, Sync, Transport and AppleHosted cannot import, yet they must log under `store`, `sync`, `transport` and `apple` and emit the plan, transfer, verify, commit, gc and eviction intervals. `LogLine` had no subsystem, so a test could not check it through the `LogTap`.
- **Options.** (a) Each target restates the subsystem. (b) Move `AssetLog` into the Model (`os` is importable there) with one helper that writes to `os.Logger` and the tap.
- **Decision.** (b). `ZTronAssetModel/Environment/AssetLog.swift`: `subsystem`, `logger(_:)`, `signposter(_:)`, `hashed(_:)` (`id#` + 16 hex digits of the identifier's FNV-1a hash, stable across processes), `emit(_:tap:)` and `log(_:_:_:tap:)`. `LogLine` gained `subsystem` (defaulting to `AssetLog.subsystem`). A caller renders identifiers through `hashed(_:)` and never interpolates a secret; the rendered line goes to `os.Logger` as one `.public` string and to the tap, so the tapped line is exactly what the unified log receives. The facade re-exports it through `@_exported import ZTronAssetModel`, so the public symbol is unchanged for apps.
- **Consequences.** Tested: `R-OBS-1: log lines carry the module subsystem and their category and render identifiers as stable hashes`. Per-field `os_log` privacy masks are not used; identifiers are hashed by us instead, which the tap can verify.
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 3).** The destination is now a seam: `emit(_:tap:)` (always `os.Logger`) became `emit(_:sink:tap:)` (ADR-047).

## ADR-044 — Facade additions: retention scope, pins, purgeable caches, diagnostics · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (public API)
- **Context.** Contract review round 2: the keep-scope of ADR-027 had no carrier (T2-13 again); `StorageReport.pinnedBytes`, `PurgeRequest.evictable` and R-CLN-4 refer to pins but only `AssetStore.setPinned` could create one; R-CLN-11 (MAY) had no registration entry point and nothing consumed `AssetEnvironment.memoryPressure`; `diagnosticReport` needed the engine's error ring.
- **Decision.** (1) `retentionScope` in the configuration plus `setRetentionScope(_:)` (ADR-027). (2) `pin(_:)` / `unpin(_:)` through `SyncEngine.setPinned(_:pinned:)` (an owned task, ADR-039). (3) `@MainActor register(_:) throws(AssetError)` / `unregister(_:)`: `PurgeableCacheRelay` holds caches weakly on the main actor and allocates an epoch per observer start or stop; `SyncEngine.observeMemoryPressure(epoch:handler:)` owns one observer task only while a live cache is registered, applies only the newest epoch, and is stopped by shutdown; a pressure event purges every live cache on the main actor. (4) `SyncEngine.diagnostics()` returns `EngineDiagnostics` (counters, the last 32 `DiagnosticError`s, app version, launch epoch, index generation, stamp, retention scope); `DiagnosticError` moved to the Model so the engine records it.
- **Consequences.** R-SYN-14 and P-14 are measured with no cache registered (the observer is work while one is). Tested now: `R-CLN-11: the cache relay purges every live cache, stops observing when none is left and refuses registration after shutdown`; the end-to-end `R-CLN-11: a registered purgeable cache is purged on a memory-pressure event` is WP-G's.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 merge, 2026-10-22, ADR-054).** Across the facade's reopen after a replaced root (ARCHITECTURE §8): (1) the run-time retention scope is recorded by the facade before it is applied, survives a reopen and is the replacement's (G-3); (2) pins live in the store's records and are lost with a vanished root (L5-R8); (3) the newest memory-pressure request is recorded and applied to each replacement engine, and `observeMemoryPressure` never waits for a reopen (G-12); (4) `diagnosticReport()` reads one engine's diagnostics, metrics and storage report per report, never mixed across generations, with empty figures when the store cannot answer (L4-R9), summarises the configuration field by field without secrets and reports `moduleVersion` "1.0.0" (G-4). The end-to-end `R-CLN-11: a registered purgeable cache is purged on a memory-pressure event` exists.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 WP-I documentation merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-056).** `AssetEnvironment.live()` passes no `MemoryPressureSource`: the UIKit conformer lives outside the module (R-CLN-11, R-CAR-9). `observeMemoryPressure` owns an observer only when `environment.memoryPressure` is non-nil; otherwise the request is recorded and nothing is owned. So with the live environment a registered `PurgeableCache` is never purged. An app that registers caches sets `memoryPressure` on `.live(legacyDefaultsSuiteName: configuration.legacy.defaultsSuiteName)` and passes that environment to `AssetClient(configuration:environment:)` (ARCHITECTURE §8; the README shows the three lines). Decision (3)'s "only while a live cache is registered" is a necessary condition, not a sufficient one (WPI-R3).

## ADR-045 — Seams for engine and store tests
- **Context.** Contract review round 2: the store's staging sink exists only after `open()`, inside `SyncEngine.start()`, and `FakeRemoteSourceFactory` threw it away, so `FakeTransferStep.stage(Data)` could not be implemented (R-SYN-4/6/8/15, R-CLN-*, P-7). Reentrancy tests (H-5 rows R2–R14) need a store unit suspended at a known point; Storage and Sync tests cannot import the facade's `SnapshotMirror`; the conditions census dropped after an unobservable hop; ARCHITECTURE §14 promised an injectable planner the contract lacked; the S1-05 prefetch regression could not be tested without the engine.
- **Decision.** All in `ZTronAssetTestSupport` unless noted, all without escapes and without an availability gate on the protocol side: `StagingAttachable` (`attach(staging:)`; `FakeRemoteSource` conforms; `FakeRemoteSourceFactory` attaches the sink before returning the source, through a cast, so no `#available`); `SteppedBlockingExecutor` (actor; holds each unit until released, records submission and completion order, `waitForHeldUnits(atLeast:)`); `ScriptedSnapshotSink` (package actor; acknowledges, ignores, or reports an older or foreign key; returns recorded accesses; `waitForInstalls(atLeast:)`); `FakeDeviceConditionsSource.waitForObservers(equalTo:)` and `FakeMemoryPressureSource` (event-driven censuses); `FakeRemoteSource.setSourceCopyBytes(_:)`. In Sync: `SyncPlanning` with `StandardSyncPlanner` and `SyncEngine.init(store:environment:configuration:planner:)`. In Kit: `PrefetchRegistry` (`SyncEngine` conforms) and `package PrefetchHandle.init(registry:ids:priority:)`.
- **Consequences.** Implemented and tested now (they are small): the factory attachment, the executor, the sink, the census waits, the memory-pressure fake, the S1-05 regression (`R-CAR-3: a re-laned prefetch handle releases its old interest only after the successor registered`, shown failing on the old release logic first). `FakeRemoteSource`'s scripting remains WP-H's.
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 3).** (1) `FakeRemoteSource.scriptReattached(_:)` scripts `reattachedTransfers()` (returned once per launch) for the H-6 tests (R-NET-3 adoption from staging, R-CLN-3 survival through launch GC). (2) `FakeTransferStep.stageGenerated(seed:byteCount:)` streams `GeneratedPayload` bytes through the sink in 256 KiB chunks, so P-7's catalog never sits in memory; `GeneratedPayload` (bytes as a pure function of seed and offset, chunked SHA-256 for manifest checksums) is implemented and tested. (3) Every await in a TestSupport actor carries a `// REENTRANCY:` comment (ARCHITECTURE §10.3). (4) `FakeRemoteSourceFactory.makeRemoteSource` takes the `log:` route (ADR-047) and ignores it.
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 4).** (1) *One ordered transport log.* `FakeRemoteSource` recorded starts on its actor and each `FakeTransferHandle` its controls under its own lock, so the H-4 test `R-NET-10: a promoted asset's priority change reaches the transport before any other transfer starts` could not see whether a `setPriority` preceded the next start (`TransferHandle` controls are synchronous, ADR-021). `FakeTransportLog` (one `Mutex` over one array of `FakeTransportEvent.started`/`.controlled`) is shared by the source (`transportLog`) and every handle it creates (`FakeTransferHandle(id:attempt:transportLog:)`); `starts` and `controlLog` are now projections of it. Implemented and tested (`Test support: a fake source and its handles record starts and controls in one global order`); `transfer(_:)`'s body, which records the start and passes the log, stays WP-H's. (2) *The R-STO-6 conformance suite is an API.* `FileSystemConformance.violations(of:in:)` (WP-H stub) returns the clauses a `FileSystem` conformer breaks, so WP-H runs it against `FakeFileSystem` in its own tests and WP-B against `LiveFileSystem` in `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Core/`, and neither edits the other's test files. It returns values instead of recording issues, so `ZTronAssetTestSupport` stays free of Swift Testing (ADR-041). (3) *Live conditions.* No `NWPath` change can be injected in-process; the R-NET-9 test posts the power or thermal notification on `LiveDeviceConditionsSource`'s injected `center`, and WP-D may add a `package` path-ingest seam in its own files (ARCHITECTURE §6.5).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L4 merge, 2026-10-17, ADR-053).** WP-F's internal test seams in `Sources/ZTronAssetSync` are ratified on the terms of E-13 (ADR-050): none is public, each is inert in production, and none adds an await on a production path except the alarm-turn hold (RE-S16) and the apply follow-up hold in `applyAndFollow` (f-8), each reached only while a test arms it. They are `holdAlarmTurns`/`releaseAlarmTurns`, `holdRunSettlementDelivery`, `holdManifestOutcomeDelivery`, `holdReadmissionFollowUps`, `holdSpaceSweepFollowUps`, `holdNextApplyFollow`/`releaseApplyFollow`, `EngineState.ownedTaskPriorityFloor` (`nil` in production), `awaitCondition`, `waitForAllHandles`, `invariantViolations`, `jobsCreated` and `ActiveAttempt.startSequence`; `clockFence`, `readForTests` and `setOwnedTaskPriorityFloor` are test-target extensions (`Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineHarness.swift`; `readForTests` in `EngineRegressionCycle3Tests.swift`), not production code. Their awaits are rows RE-S16 and f-8 (ARCHITECTURE §10.2); the docs gate stays at 1281/1281.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 merge, 2026-10-22, ADR-054).** In Kit, the client's handles register through `ClientLifecycle`, the facade's `PrefetchRegistry`, which binds each facade token to the engine token of its generation (G-1); `SyncEngine` still conforms, and the handle tests keep the gated fake. WP-G's internal test seams are ratified on the terms of E-13 (ADR-050): none is public, and each is inert in production. In `ZTronAssetKit`: `LifecycleSeamPoint` with `ClientLifecycle.setSeam(_:)` (six points; its awaits are rows g-9 and g-4), `setFollowerObserver(_:)`, `liveFollowers`, `recordedRetentionScope`, `EventForwarder`'s `offerParked` and `stopped` hooks and `bufferedEvents`, and `AssetClient.currentEngine()`. In WP-F's files: the pure split of the registration into `recordGroup` and `spawnJoin` (no production seam, L5-R17) and `endClaims(of:)` made internal so a test can give claims up in one turn (L5-R15). The docs gate stays at 1281/1281.

## Contract-fix round 3

ADR-046…048 were written by the contract fixer of round 3, resolving the independent round-3 review of the Stage 1 contracts (8 issues, 1 blocking). Every issue and its disposition is in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §13.3. ADR-010, ADR-016, ADR-026, ADR-032, ADR-035, ADR-043 and ADR-045 carry round-3 amendments.

## ADR-046 — Stage 2 dependency levels and the storage ownership split (orchestrator decision)
- **Context.** PROMPT §5 ran WP-B and WP-C in parallel with write sets `{FileSystem,Store,Index,Blobs,Lock,Snapshot}` and `{Integrity,Cleanup,Eviction,Migration}`, but the round-2 contract put all 16 of WP-C's `AssetStore` methods as stubs inside `Store/AssetStore.swift` over `private` state: WP-C could not implement them in its own directories (an actor extension can neither add stored properties nor read `private` state), and both packages would edit one actor body, a guaranteed merge conflict (CLAUDE.md P2). `Staging/` and `Layout/` were in nobody's write set. The orchestrator also decided the Stage 2 order.
- **Options.** (a) Keep parallel B and C and grant both `AssetStore.swift` (conflicts by construction). (b) Sequence C after B and grant C `AssetStore.swift` (ownership by time, not by path). (c) Sequence C after B **and** split by path: WP-C's API in `extension AssetStore` files in its directories, the shared state declared in the contract.
- **Decision.** (c). Work packages run in dependency levels, each in its own worktree `.worktrees/<wp>` (ADR-009) with its own scratch path: **L1** {H test support, A model} → **L2** {B storage core, D transport, E Apple tier} → **L3** {C integrity, cleanup, eviction, migration, on merged B} → **L4** {F sync engine} → **L5** {G facade, then K compatibility shims in `Tests/ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests`}; **I** (docs) at the end. The orchestrator merges and commits a level before the next starts. Storage: `AssetStore.environment` and `AssetStore.state: StoreState` are internal; `StoreState` holds one sub-struct per owner (`core`: WP-B; `cleanup`, `eviction`, `integrity`, `migration`: WP-C), each in its owner's directory, extendable only by its owner. WP-C's methods live in `Eviction/`, `Cleanup/`, `Integrity/`, `Migration/` (signatures unchanged). WP-B provides primitives for WP-C (`Store/StoreInternals.swift`: `ensureOpen`, `setResidency(_:of:dooming:)`, `markDirty`, `requireCommitted`, `publishSnapshot(urgency:)`, `reapDeletable(maxKeys:)`, `hashFile(at:)`, `adoptVerifiedFile(at:digest:byteCount:for:)`); WP-B calls WP-C only through two opt-in hooks (`runLegacyPhaseOne()` when legacy migration is enabled, `applyEviction(_:)` when `apply` gets a plan), so WP-B is complete and testable before WP-C exists. `Staging/` and `Layout/` belong to WP-B. `recordAccesses`, the I-3 deletion machinery, the streaming hash and launch reconciliation are WP-B's, because they sit on WP-B's commit and acknowledgement paths; §11's WP column follows (R-INT-1/2/5/6, R-CLN-1, R-CLN-7, P-6, P-13 → B; R-CLN-5 → B and C). Every WP writes only its own test files and its own benchmark file (ARCHITECTURE §1.2); contract test files, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` stay orchestrator-owned, and a WP drafts its ADRs in `docs/adr-drafts/<wp>.md` for the orchestrator to number.
- **Consequences.** File ownership is decided by path alone, so a merge conflict is an ownership bug, as P2 requires. WP-C depends on WP-B's internals (the `core` fields and primitives), which the sequencing makes safe. B and C no longer overlap in time, which lengthens the critical path by one level; D and E absorb the parallelism of L2. PROMPT §5's parallel "B, C, D, E" is superseded by this plan (PROMPT.md itself is not edited).
- **Amendment (contract-fix round 4).** The round-4 review found writes and tests the plan above left without an owner. (1) *Package-wide scans.* R-CAR-9 and R-SEC-3 were WP-I's and R-ENV-4 "all"'s, but WP-I owns no test path and "all" is nobody, so the trace gate's MUST-coverage could not be reached under the stated ownership. The orchestrator owns all six package-wide scans (R-CAR-9, R-SEC-3, R-ENV-2, R-ENV-3, R-ENV-4, R-ENV-5) in `Tests/ZTronAssetModelTests/PackageScanContractTests.swift`, written and green in this round; the R-ENV-4 scan reads its patterns and cap from `Scripts/quality-gates.json`. The alternative, reassigning R-CAR-9, R-SEC-3 and R-ENV-4 to WP-A (whose tests already host the R-ENV-1 source scan), was not taken: all six pass today, so landing them now costs less than scheduling them, and three of them check `Package.swift`, which is the orchestrator's file anyway. (2) *WP-C's legacy writes.* Legacy phase 1 may install the legacy manifest as `appliedManifest` (no ETag) while none is applied, insert `absent(.neverFetched)` records with `expectedBlob` for ids the index lacks, and set `degraded` on an undecodable legacy manifest; phase 3 may set `syncDue` (directly or through `markSyncDue()`). No other route exists: `adoptVerifiedFile` only marks existing records present and `apply` cannot run inside `open()` (REENTRANCY R1). (3) *Cleanup stamps.* `lastCleanupAt` is stamped only by WP-C's own GC, eviction and purge (it is `StorageReport.lastCleanupAt`, "the last GC, eviction or purge"), never by WP-B's reaper; `lastMaintenanceAt` is removed (no reader, no reachable writer; ADR-012 amendment). (4) *Writers with a path at the right level.* Every multi-WP row of ARCHITECTURE §11 names each test's writer: R-CAR-6 → K (ADR-033 amendment); P-7 → F (`BenchSync.swift`); the R-STO-1 foreground-transfer test and the R-CLN-10 retention-scope test → F (they need WP-D's backend and WP-C's `setRetentionScope`, merged only by L4); the R-STO-6 conformance suite is H's API that B calls (ADR-045 amendment). (5) *Shared documents.* `docs/adr-drafts/<wp>.md` is carved out of WP-I's `docs/**`, one file per WP; `docs/TRACEABILITY.md` is orchestrator-regenerated after each merge and a WP restores it before handoff (or runs only `--quick`); REENTRANCY rows a WP needs are proposed in its ADR draft and merged by the orchestrator, and the contract's already-required awaits got rows now (ARCHITECTURE §10, R16–R18, RE-S35–RE-S42).
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** "WP-B calls WP-C only through two opt-in hooks" gains one exception: WP-B writes WP-C's contract field `releasedBytes`, raising it for `apply`'s and `admit`'s own releases and lowering it when such a doom is rescued (S3B-9; `AssetStore+References.swift`).

## ADR-047 — A log-sink seam: tests never write to the unified log · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (public signatures)
- **Context.** `AssetLog.emit` always wrote to `os.Logger`. Once implementations log at `.info` or above, every test exercising those paths would persist lines into the system's unified-log store, outside this repository (owner directive); the existing R-OBS-1 test already emitted one `.debug` line. Transport and the Apple tier had no route to the environment's tap at all.
- **Options.** (a) Log only to the tap in tests by convention (unenforceable). (b) A `LogSink` protocol in the environment, live conformer over `os.Logger`, a test conformer that writes nothing, and one value carrying sink and tap to every layer built from the environment.
- **Decision.** (b). `LogSink` (`write(_:)`, `signposter(_:) -> OSSignposter?`) with `OSLogSink` (live; the only writer to the unified log; named only by `.live()`, like the other Model live conformers of ADR-038) and `TapOnlyLogSink` (writes nothing, no signposts). `AssetEnvironment.logSink` defaults to `TapOnlyLogSink` in `init`, so an environment assembled by hand (every test) never reaches the unified log unless it names `OSLogSink`; `.live()` names it. `LogRoute` (sink + optional tap) is how the environment's logging reaches the store (`StoreEnvironment.log`) and the remote sources (`RemoteSourceFactory.makeRemoteSource(configuration:staging:log:)`, `HTTPRemoteSource.log`, `AppleHostedRemoteSource.log`, `AppleHostedTier.resolve(…, log:, …)`). `AssetLog.emit(_:sink:tap:)` and `log(_:_:_:sink:tap:)` replace the `os.Logger`-only variants; signpost intervals are taken from the sink, so tests emit none.
- **Consequences.** Tested: `R-OBS-1: every line reaches the environment's sink and tap once, and an environment of fakes keeps the unified log untouched`; the round-2 R-OBS-1 test now routes through `TapOnlyLogSink`. An app that builds its own environment must pass `OSLogSink()` to keep unified logging (documented on `AssetEnvironment.init`). Public signatures changed: `RemoteSourceFactory.makeRemoteSource(configuration:staging:log:)`, `HTTPRemoteSource.init(configuration:staging:log:)`, `AppleHostedRemoteSource.init(configuration:backend:staging:log:)`, `AppleHostedTier.resolve(configuration:staging:packBackend:log:makeAWS:)`, `AssetEnvironment.init(…, logSink:, logTap:)`; none has shipped.
- **Review tag (contract-fix round 4).** Tagged NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW like ADR-042, ADR-044 and ADR-048: it changes public signatures (`RemoteSourceFactory.makeRemoteSource(configuration:staging:log:)`, a protocol requirement custom factories must adopt; `AssetEnvironment.init(…, logSink:, …)`; `HTTPRemoteSource.init(configuration:staging:log:)`; `AppleHostedRemoteSource.init`; `AppleHostedTier.resolve`) and makes an environment an app builds by hand silent in the unified log by default (`TapOnlyLogSink`) unless it passes `OSLogSink()`. Listed in the owner review list at the top of this file.

## ADR-048 — Every configuration knob reaches its consumer · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (public signatures)
- **Context.** Round-3 review: five settings never reached the code that needs them. `LegacyMigrationPolicy.defaultsSuiteName` was never read (the default `.live()` read `.standard`, so a configured suite's legacy stamp was neither migrated nor removed, R-STO-2/R-STO-11); `prefetchRadius` was unused; `PlanningPolicy.evictionPermitted` had no source (R-SYN-11 "policy permitting"); the store's publication cap had no `maxAssetBytes`; `DiagnosticReport.indexSchemaVersion` had no exported constant.
- **Options.** For the suite: remove the field, or derive the live environment from it. For the rest: wire each knob, or document it as unused.
- **Decision.** Wire all five. (a) `AssetClient.init(configuration:environment: AssetEnvironment? = nil)`: `nil` resolves to `.live(legacyDefaultsSuiteName: configuration.legacy.defaultsSuiteName)` (`AssetClient.resolvedEnvironment(_:for:)`); an explicit environment wins (an app passing its own `.live(…)` passes the same suite). (b) `PrefetchWindowController(client:radius: Int? = nil)` defaults to `configuration.prefetchRadius`. (c) `StoragePolicy.evictionPermitted` (default `true`; when `false` a run evicts nothing to make room, maintenance quota eviction still runs) and `PlanningPolicy(storage:includeOnDemand:now:)`, which copies it with `allowUnverifiedAssets` and `admitLargestFittingPrefix`. (d) `StoreConfiguration.limits: ManifestLimits` (publication cap, legacy decoding limits) and `snapshotInstallInterval` (ADR-016), built by `AssetClient.storeConfiguration(for:)`. (e) `StoreLayout.indexSchemaVersion: UInt16 = 1`.
- **Consequences.** Tested: `R-STO-11: the default live environment reads the legacy stamp from the configured UserDefaults suite`, `R-CAR-3: the window controller's radius defaults to the client configuration's prefetchRadius`, `R-SYN-11: the planning policy takes eviction permission, unverified admission and the prefix rule from StoragePolicy`, `R-SYN-11: the eviction switch defaults to permitted and an out-of-range snapshot install interval is clamped`, `R-STO-13: the store receives the client's snapshot install interval, manifest limits, policy and legacy layout`. Public changes for the owner to confirm: the optional `environment` of `AssetClient.init`, the optional `radius` of `PrefetchWindowController.init`, the new `StoragePolicy.evictionPermitted`.

## Contract-fix round 4

No new ADR: the contract fixer of round 4 resolved the independent round-4 review of the Stage 1 contracts (7 issues, 1 blocking) by amending ADR-012, ADR-033, ADR-045 and ADR-046 and tagging ADR-047 NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW. Every issue and its disposition is in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §13.4.

## ADR-049 — Stage 2 level 1 merge: WP-H and WP-A decisions · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (A-4.5: the R-MAN-1 deviation; A-7: the R-OBS-1 interval seam, public API)
- **Context.** WP-H (test support) and WP-A (Model) were implemented, independently verified in three rounds plus a close-out round (0 blocking findings), and merged into `feature/asset-kit`. Their decision records are `docs/adr-drafts/h.md` (H-1…H-8) and `docs/adr-drafts/a.md` (A-1…A-15); those drafts are normative detail for this ADR.
- **Ratified contract additions.** TestSupport grows additively only (no existing signature changed, checked against tag `contracts`): the 22 public declarations listed in `h.md` "Additive public API", including `FakeTransferHandle.setPauseResumable(_:)` and the explicit `DiskImage` `==`/`hash` over observable state. Model: `ManifestError.decodedSizeExceeded(bytes:limit:)` (`payloadTooLarge` keeps the single meaning "raw payload over `maxPayloadBytes`"); bounded quarantine reporting (`ValidatedManifest.quarantinedCount` exact, diagnostics capped by a `ManifestLimits` field); `RemotePathViolation.queryOrFragment`; the R-OBS-1 interval seam (A-7, **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**, public API). Declined: `ManifestEntryViolation.duplicateIdentifier`, `ManifestLimits.maxPathComponents`.
- **R-MAN-3 allocation bound (A-8).** A two-pass streaming decoder checks an estimate before building anything: build pass ≤ 3.75× payload, survey pass ≤ 2.3× payload, measured in fresh processes on this Mac including large-block reuse (counted at 2×). Consequence: extremely entry-dense manifests (entry array > 2 MiB with ~111-byte entries) are rejected with `decodedSizeExceeded`; realistic catalogs (250–350 bytes per entry) are unaffected. **Not verified:** iOS device allocators.
- **R-MAN-1 deviation · NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW.** UTF-16/UTF-32 manifests, which the prior art's `JSONDecoder` would have detected, are rejected (A-4.5): transcoding would spend up to 1.5× of the 4× budget, and the publisher emits UTF-8 only. Confirm no published manifest is UTF-16.
- **Fake fidelity limits (documented on the types).** `FakeFileSystem` treats `.barrier` and `.full` sync identically (durable at the call) and `FSOpRecord` does not record the level, so ordered-vs-durable commits are distinguishable only through the store's own commit API (WP-B tests it there). `FakeTransferBackend` retains every handle (its `handles` promise), so drop-to-cancel is observable only through `FakeRemoteSource`. The conformance suite creates its symbolic-link fixture with `FileManager` inside the caller's root — an accepted exception to ADR-038's "LiveFileSystem is the only FileManager code" for this test-only fixture.
- **Lock clause.** The conformance suite's stale-token clause stays; WP-B's `LiveFileSystem.releaseLock` must satisfy it.
- **REENTRANCY rows.** The TestSupport rows proposed as `h-1`…`h-9` keep those identifiers as their final numbers in ARCHITECTURE §10.3 (cited in code as "h-n (proposed)"; the word is historical).
- **Hygiene.** Toolchain side effects outside the repository that environment variables cannot redirect (Swift macro-expansion dumps and empty `TemporaryDirectory.*` folders in the per-user temp directory, a default DerivedData folder from one `xcodebuild -list`, two error result bundles) are listed for the owner in `VERIFICATION.md`; agents must pass `-derivedDataPath` to every `xcodebuild` and never run an unscoped `pkill`.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).** A-4.7 no longer allows `.`: an empty or `.` (also `%2E`) `/`-separated segment and any backslash are `RemotePathViolation.parentSegment`, counted after the leading slashes `absolute` and `authority` report; `%2F` and `%5C` stay literal to that rule while the `..` check still treats them as separators; `..foo`, `...` and `%41` stay allowed (S3AH-1). Declined as well: A-11.7's hashed log description (`AssetLog.describe`). S3-B-001 (the R-MAN-1 UTF-16/UTF-32 deviation) and S3-B-002 (the R-OBS-1 interval seam, public API) stay **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** as ratified; this heading and the owner review list now carry the tag that the body already gave them.

## ADR-050 — Stage 2 level 2 merge: WP-B, WP-D and WP-E decisions · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (B-3, D-10, D-19.2, startDownload; P-13 ruled by the owner on 2026-10-29)
- **Context.** WP-B (storage core), WP-D (transport) and WP-E (Apple tier) were implemented and independently verified (main run: author, two verifiers, up to two fix rounds; then five close-out cycles; the final close-out verification found 0 blocking findings for B, D and E) and merged into `feature/asset-kit` in the order b, d, e (merge commits 645e7a3, 35fbae6, 645caee). The orchestrator's checks on the merged head: `verify.sh --quick` `VERIFY: PASS`; release build and iOS `xcodebuild` green; unsafe 0; docs 1277/1277; trace: 13 MUSTs without a test, all owned by WP-C, WP-F or WP-G. Their decision records are `docs/adr-drafts/b.md` (B-1…B-38), `d.md` (D-1…D-20) and `e.md` (E-1…E-17); those drafts, with the orchestrator rulings recorded in each, are normative detail for this ADR. Where a draft's own wording and a later ruling disagree, the ruling wins (see "Rulings over the drafts").
- **Ratified contract changes** (checked against tag `l1-merged`).
  - *WP-B, doc-only, no declaration changed:* `FileLockToken.handle` and `FileSystem.releaseLock` (the live handle is `nonce << 32 | descriptor`, the nonce parked in the descriptor's file offset; releasing a stale token releases nothing, B-1); `OpenReport.generation` (0 when the launch could not order the names it read and its launch commit failed, B-36); `StagingSink.discard` (completes regardless of the caller's cancellation, B-37; WP-E discards from cancelled driver tasks and relies on it, re-checked at this merge: satisfied). Additive package API in WP-B's own files: `AssetRecord.referencedBlob`, `expectedKey`, `isStale` (for WP-F's planner, B-13) and `FileSystemErrorMapping` (`posix`, `cocoa`, `syncFallsBackToFsync(errno:)`, `posixCode(of:)`, `stagingError`, `storeError`).
  - *WP-D:* public `BackgroundSessionDriver.startDownload(_:name:priority:expectedBytes:resumeData:)` gains `resumeData: ResumeData?` (`nil` = fresh), so a background `paused(resumable: true)` is true (D-14.2) · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (public API change; no R-CAR-6 shim is affected). Package `HTTPRemoteSource.makeBackgroundBackend(_:staging:policy:appKey:requestTimeout:log:makeDriver:)` gains defaulted parameters, so the contract call is unchanged; the key reaches background requests only under `.acceptedByOwner`; the internal overload is deleted (D-14.1). Acknowledged package additions: `BackgroundTransferCoordinator.init(driver:staging:options:policy:appKey:requestTimeout:log:)`, `admits()`, `startIfAdmitted(_:)`, `reattached()`, `awaitBackgroundEvents()`, `keptResumeData(for:)`, `isPumping`, `control(_:token:keepingPartial:)`, `consumerGone(_:token:)`, `setPriority(_:of:token:)`; the class `BackgroundTransferHandle` (`name`, `token`); `ForegroundTransferBackend.init(delegate:maxConnectionsPerHost:requestTimeout:appKey:log:)`; `LiveBackgroundSessionDriver.hasSession`; `TaskMarker` (`record`, `requestIfUnmarked`, `requestDiscard`); `SessionDelegate.foregroundOutcome`, `receive`, `finishDownload`; `BackgroundTaskTag` (`label(_:priority:)`, `createdFromResumeData`, `forStart(name:expectedBytes:resumeData:)`, its coding); `BackgroundSessionConfiguration.make(identifier:discretionary:)`; `PathObserving`, `LiveDeviceConditionsSource.init(center:makePathObserver:power:)`, `activeObservations`, `waitForObservations(equalTo:)`, `processPower`, `livePathObserver`. Public protocol witnesses on `SessionDelegate` (its URLSession delegate methods and the `URLSessionDownloadDelegate` conformance), authorised by ARCHITECTURE §6.1. Body or doc only in contract files: `SizeGuard` saturates at the bounds of `Int64`; internal `TransferJob.backgroundByteSize`, `SizeGuard.clampedLimit`, `TransferClassifier.asResumed`; `RedirectPolicy.decide` implemented. `TransportContractTests.swift` is unchanged.
  - *WP-E:* `PackID.init?(rawValue:)` and `PackLocation.init?(remotePath:)` bodies are stricter (split at `/` scalars, ASCII fast path; E-15), signatures unchanged; the internal seam `AppleHostedTier.resolve(…, select:)` (E-13; inert in production, since `TierSelection.select` never names a tier the configuration cannot build). No public or package signature changed. Declined: an `AdoptionReport` field carrying the applied digest (E-10(c)). Deferred: a persisted "released packs per catalog generation" store field (E-10).
- **Benchmarks** (release, this Mac; the orchestrator's reference runs on the merged head with no other agent running, load average 1.9–2.6 for runs 2–3, about 5 for run 1). Statistics as ruled on B-9: P-2 the p95 of cold loads; P-5 the p95 of **both** the ordered and the durable commit, commits per second a hard maximum over the worst sliding second; P-6 the best of five runs; **P-13 the maximum deletion-unit duration** (SPEC §6 "in batches ≤ `batch_ms_max` ms between suspension points" bounds every batch), p50 and p95 printed beside it. Results: P-2 p95 8.50 / 8.59 ms (budget 25); P-3 p50 16.6–17.9 ms (80); P-5 ordered p95 13.2 / 13.3 ms, durable p95 32.0 / 29.0 ms (40), ≤ 4 commits/s; P-6 2,546 MB/s, 0.815× one-shot, resident delta ≤ 0.016 MiB; P-10 8 connections (max 8). **P-13 fails in all three runs:** total 0.58–0.63 s (2.0 s), unit p50 0.96 ms, p95 0.99 ms, but unit max 9.2 / 11.0 / 11.6 ms (5.0 ms). Every over-budget unit (2–3 of about 490) holds exactly one blob `unlink` that blocked in the kernel for 5.2–10.8 ms, against a volume profile of p50 0.04 ms and p99 0.055 ms; the unit's own on-CPU time is ≤ 1.02 ms (B-10's 1 ms budget).
- **P-13 proposal to the owner · NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (SPEC §6 "propose a new number"). A single APFS `unlink` occasionally stalls about 10 ms, so no unit size can bound the maximum below that. Option A (recommended): read P-13's per-batch bound as **p99**, as P-1 and P-9 state their latency bounds (measured p99 ≈ 1 ms). Option B: keep the maximum and raise `batch_ms_max` to 15 ms. Either needs the owner's edit of SPEC §6 or `Scripts/quality-gates.json`; neither is edited, and `BenchStorage.swift` keeps asserting the maximum and fails until the owner rules.
- **Ratified decisions — WP-B.** B-3 the v1 `index.bin` byte layout · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (on-disk format; ADR-012 amendment). B-9 as ruled above. B-10 time-bounded deletion units: at most 64 keys **and** at most `BlobDeletionUnit.budget` (1 ms) of the injected clock, the last unlink predicted (ARCHITECTURE §4.4; ADR-035 amendment). B-12 adoption binds records to live, unreferenced or rescued keys, never to `purged`/`corrupt` dooms (§4.5; ADR-018 amendment). B-16 internal test-target seams (`testSetLease`, `testClearLeases`, `testSetRetentionScope`, `testSetIntegrity`, `testBumpPurgeEpoch`, `testExpect`, `testInsertRecord`, and B-23's `testRaiseApplyFence`) write WP-C's fields the way WP-C's APIs will; never public, never in `Sources/`. B-18 (binders of one key serialised; an extra-extension copy is hashed and placed only with exactly `byteCount` bytes hashing to its name), B-19 (the actor is never held for long at 10,000 assets), B-27 (a fan-out created by a failed placement still dirties `blobs/`). B-28 with its cycle 2, 3 and 5 amendments: the ENOSPC fallback keeps the newest valid generation, step 5 rolls back step 4, a loaded `index.tmp` is promoted first, a rename that took effect but reported failure is undone, and the committed file is tracked by name (`CommitBookkeeping.committedFile`), never inferred from a header (§4.1; ADR-015 amendment). B-29 commit backoff. B-30 an admission gives the applied manifest a digest no fetched manifest has (`AssetStore.admittedDigest(after:revision:)`). B-31. B-32 and B-35 lifecycle (every return of `close()` means flushed durably and unlocked; an `open()` parked on a running open returns that open's report, or `.notOpen`). B-33 the exhaustive state-space test, with cycle 4 amendment 3's narrower reading of I-a: the test counts as durable the newest valid generation under the crash's view and every generation the store counted committed, and excludes a failed attempt's synced `index.tmp`. B-34 one read-only lifetime after an index stat or read error; the relaxation for an unreadable `index.prev` beside a valid `index.bin` is declined. B-36 (with its cycle-5 amendment) nothing counts as committed until a root sync ordered the names the load read. B-37, B-38. B-5's option to expose the read-only state on `OpenReport` is declined (`OpenReport.degraded` suffices); its epoch amendment is ADR-013's amendment. B-17 option (c) in the store (ADR-016 amendment); the mirror reset is WP-G's. Recorded by reference, normative detail in `b.md`: B-1, B-2, B-4, B-6…B-8, B-11, B-13…B-15, B-20…B-26.
- **Ratified decisions — WP-D.** D-10 an explicit `shutdown()` cancels in-flight background downloads (the Model's drop-to-cancel rule) · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (ADR-039 amendment). D-19.1 a background 206 continues only a task created from resume data for checksummed content; any other 206 is `unexpectedStatus(206)` (device check pending, see "Not verified"). D-19.2 the "created from resume data" flag travels in the task tag across relaunch (`ztak1 <name> <size | -> [resume]`, at most 256 bytes; a fresh task keeps the three-field tag of earlier builds) · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (a persisted format: the OS keeps the tag in `taskDescription` across relaunch, so every later build must decode it; orchestrator ruling at the Level 2 close, as `d.md` tags it). The overflow saturation and size clamping of review round 3 and close-out cycle 1 (D-13, D-18.1). ADR-019 and ADR-026 amendments. Recorded by reference, normative detail in `d.md`: D-1…D-9, D-11…D-18, D-20 (among them `TaskMarker`, the staged size read back from staging, the status table, sidecars, URL building, routing, the coordinator, `SessionGate`, the conditions seam, the Retry-After two-digit-year pivot).
- **Ratified decisions — WP-E.** The `Mutex` exception (ADR-003 amendment; APPLE-API-VERIFICATION #19, §4 items 6 and 7). ADR-027 applies literally: a pack is released at most once per pack per catalog generation and never re-armed within one (E-10(b) withdrawn); a pack downloaded again on demand after its release stays installed until a reclaim in a later catalog generation releases it. `sourceCopyBytes()` counts adopted packs, packs released in this process that came back, and packs whose planned removal has not yet succeeded (the third set lands with the L2 follow-up `wp/l2fu`; ADR-027 amendment). E-10 released-pack tracking stays in memory; persisting it needs a store field and is deferred. E-1 (the catalog digest and the asset check are computed inside the staging pull: one extra SHA-256 pass per adoption detects skew). E-4 (a file missing after its pack was made available is `transient(.sourceVersionSkew)` with one update check, per ADR-032, a deviation from transport.md §11.3 step 4; a missing pack stays `permanentMissing`). E-13 (internal, documented test seams in production targets, never public, inert on production paths; the four `Seams:` tests carry no requirement ID). E-15. E-17: BackgroundAssets cannot honour `TransferRequest.access` / `ManifestRequest.access`, so the tier ignores them by necessity and WP-F gates. Recorded by reference, normative detail in `e.md`: E-2, E-3, E-5…E-9, E-11, E-12, E-14, E-16.
- **Rulings over the drafts.** B-9's Decision bullet and its fix-round-1 decline (P-5 durable on the median, P-13 on the p95) are superseded by the ruling above. B-28's decision item 1, its cycle-3 amendment 1 and their wording infer the committed file from a header (`currentHolds`, which no longer exists); the cycle-5 amendment (tracked by name) is what holds. B-5's "`OpenReport.generation` is then the loaded one" is superseded by B-36 (0 when the launch could not order the names it read and its launch commit failed). E-1's reading of the `Mutex` rule as "floor-15 code only" would admit any code gated iOS 18 or later; the ruling is narrower (iOS 26-gated code, three types). The drafts' "the orchestrator numbers these" for REENTRANCY rows: the identifiers are final.
- **Assignments to later work packages** (ARCHITECTURE §11 rows set accordingly; §1.1, §5, §7).
  - *WP-F:* the engine half of R-NET-8, `R-NET-8: a refused or invalid resume discards the partial and restarts from zero in the next attempt` and `R-NET-8: an invalid resume yields resumeRejected and the engine's next attempt starts fresh` (WP-D's transport half is accepted as is); gating Apple-tier dispatch and update-checking manifest fetches on the engine's own `DeviceConditions` (E-17), with the test named here `R-SYN-7: Apple-tier transfers and update-checking manifest fetches start only when the engine's DeviceConditions satisfy their NetworkAccess`; `R-STO-1: a cancelled, paused or failed foreground transfer leaves no byte outside the store root` (already F's); the end-to-end `R-APL-3: an asset adopted from a FakePackBackend source is served from blobs/ after its pack is released`; notes B-7 (checksum-less and rescue publications commit before returning and share the ≤ 4 commits/s window, so a checksum-less bulk sync publishes at most `concurrency × 4` assets per second) and B-30 (an admitted manifest's digest never equals a fetched one, so a planner digest short-circuit stays correct).
  - *WP-G:* `R-NET-3: background falls back to foreground when the background-events hook is not wired` (needs `BackgroundEventsRelay`); B-17: after a `rootVanished` reopen, which the facade owns (B-15), it calls `SnapshotMirror.reset()` before the replacement store's first install.
  - *WP-C:* the B-36 test, named here `R-STO-5: a legacy phase-1 commit before open()'s barrier with a pending promotion and a failed root sync never counts the surviving generation, and committedFile names a file that holds what it counts`; coverage of the `AssetLease`, `EvictionVictim`, `EvictionPlan` and `VerificationReport` package initialisers through WP-C's real APIs (no coverage-only test, G4; the orchestrator re-checks Storage per-file coverage after L3); replacing the B-16 seams by real APIs where they exist; the merged-store notes of ARCHITECTURE §1.1 rule 4.
  - *ARCHITECTURE §14:* `LiveFileSystem.standardDirectory` is a known deliberately untested line (exercising it would create directories under `~/Library`).
- **REENTRANCY rows.** The proposed rows keep their identifiers as final numbers: b-1…b-9 in ARCHITECTURE §10.1, D-1…D-12 in the new §10.4 (REENTRANCY rows, distinct from the decision entries D-1…D-20 of the same draft), e-1…e-8 in the new §10.5. The code keeps citing "<id> (proposed)"; the word is historical, as for h-n (ADR-049). The TestSupport rows h-1…h-9, which ADR-049 placed in §10.3 but which were missing there, are copied in this merge. Rows R1, R5 and R6 are reworded to what the code's comments rely on (B-35, B-28/B-29, B-17).
- **Not verified** (carried to `docs/VERIFICATION.md` at Stage 5): the "Not verified here" sections of `b.md` and `d.md` by reference, and for WP-E (whose draft has no such section) E-3, E-5, E-6, E-8, E-9, E-12, E-16 and E-17. Notably: `F_BARRIERFSYNC`/`F_FULLFSYNC` semantics and file protection on device; iOS 15 runtime behaviour; a real `rename(2)` that takes effect and reports failure, and an I/O error from `lstat`/`pread` on an index file (modelled only by the fake, B-33, B-34); how long a real volume keeps a killed process's rename undoable (B-36); what the index state-space enumeration does not cover (B-33: bit rot after load, faults combined with crashes beyond the volume scripts, forged headers with unordered names); `LiveFileSystem` catch paths that APFS inside the repository cannot provoke; real background sessions (relaunch re-attachment, `taskDescription` persistence, whether `nsurlsessiond` ever finishes a fresh download with a ranged 206, the order of `cancelByProducingResumeData`'s completion, background resume data end to end); real `NWPathMonitor` transitions; HTTP/2 against CloudFront; `LiveAssetPackBackend` against real Apple-hosted packs (`installedBytes ≈ downloadSize` is UNVERIFIED, cancellation of `ensureLocalAvailability`, concrete BackgroundAssets error types including a full disk, the networks pack downloads use); the sliced release survey's timing at 100,000 entries (a one-off probe only); two WP-E behaviours checked at source level only: a stopped copy that outlives `invalidate()` is left for WP-C's staging GC, and the source test asserts only that the file is left (E-9), and a consumer that drops the stream between the stop check and the yield has no suspension point to hold, so that race is tested only through `deliver` on an already-ended stream (E-8, `PackTransfer.swift`). Device items are rows of APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §5.
- **Resolved at the Level 2 close** (orchestrator rulings; branch `wp/l2cdocs`). (1) Contract doc comments, comments only, now state the ratified behaviour: `TransferAttempt` (`TransferID.swift`), `SnapshotKey` and `SnapshotSink` (`AssetSnapshot.swift`) and the `AssetStore+Open.swift` comment (the epoch is unique only across launches whose launch commit succeeded over a readable index); `OpenReport.degraded` (the B-6/B-34 conditions, `StoreTypes.swift`); `TransferUpdate.paused(resumable:)`, `TransferControl.pause`, `TransferHandle.pause()`, `TransientCause.resumeRejected` and `.backgroundSessionUnavailable` (D-3, D-4, D-5, D-14.2, D-15); `StorageReport.sourceCopyBytes` and `NetworkAccess` (E-11, E-17). (2) `UNSAFE-AUDIT.md` states the `Mutex` exception (unsafe gate `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`). (3) ARCHITECTURE §12.1/§12.2: S1-05 and S2-08 name the merged WP-B tests that verify them; S1-06, S2-01 (two names), S2-04, S2-07 and S2-13 go to WP-C, S1-09 and S2-12 to WP-F, each also a §11 row of that WP. (4) §12.6 TR1-9 amended: the background path makes no pre-resume check of the archived request and needs none under R-NET-4 (the job's URL is checked before every start, resume data never outlives the coordinator whose fixed policy it was kept under, a download from another origin is never adopted, and under `.refuse` no background request carries the key). (5) §11, WP-F: the R-APL-4 engine half, R-CLN-9's relaunch case, the ENOENT rule of §7.6 under R-SYN-4 (D-13), and `SyncEngine.waitUntil` ending on its caller's cancellation under R-SYN-14 (§7.2); WP-G: the B-17 mirror reset under R-STO-13. (6) §10.3 gains l2fu-1 and l2fu-2, and TS2 and h-4 end on the caller's cancellation too (B-38, `wp/l2fu`); a row for `ConditionsObservation.wait(for:)`, if the parallel code pass adds one, is the orchestrator's to copy. (7) D-19.2 carries **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** and is on the owner review list. (8) D-20.4 is resolved without a re-run: the scratchpad collision could only make a mutant's apply step fail or apply nothing, which shows as a surviving mutant; every result that verifier reported was a kill, so none is in doubt. (9) Wording: ADR-016's B-17 amendment, this heading, ARCHITECTURE §3 "Names" and §14's fixture directories, and "stays installed until a reclaim in a later catalog generation releases it" in `e.md` and `PackTransfer.swift`.
- **Deferred** (optional): a timed P-9 case of the sliced release survey at 100,000 entries (E-16; WP-G's `BenchKit.swift` under §11's two-package rule); the `Int64` edge rows of `TransportContractTests.swift`'s size-guard table (D-18.4; WP-D's own `R-NET-7: the size guard decides at the edges of Int64 without trapping` already covers them).
- **Level 2 close, after the close pass** (orchestrator). The close-pass verifiers' last notes were applied by the orchestrator: `SnapshotKey`'s doc states the mirror reset as WP-G's obligation; §12.2 S2-07 and §12.5 TR1-4 made precise (a changed validator yields a 200 that restarts from zero); rows `l2close-1` (amending D-11) and `l2close-2` added to §10.4; `R-NET-9: terminating the conditions stream…` switched to the throwing `requireObservations`. **Assigned to WP-G** (orchestrator-owned contract test, edit authorised then): `KitContractTests.swift`'s `GatedRegistry.waitUntil` is a census wait on a bare continuation, so a regression that never meets its predicate hangs the run; give it the `CensusWaiting` pattern (cancellation ends it) when WP-G implements the facade.
- **Process.** Verifiers of one WP never share a SwiftPM scratch path or the session scratchpad (two incidents: a deleted coverage profile, a replaced mutant script; the level script now gives each verifier its own path). Correctness verifiers found bugs of one class one probe at a time until an exhaustive state-space test was demanded (B-33 then found two more on its first run): demand the enumeration as soon as a class repeats. Run TSan and benchmarks only on an otherwise idle machine (the storage target took 160 s under TSan at load 7–9). `.verify-logs` is shared by concurrent `verify.sh` runs and a stray `default.profraw` can appear, so agents read the `VERIFY:` line from their own output and set `LLVM_PROFILE_FILE` inside their scratch path. Verifiers must never run `xcodebuild -list` (it wrote result bundles to the per-user temp directory); the owner may delete the leftover `~/Library/Developer/Xcode/DerivedData/{l2fu-*,ZTronAssetKit-*}` folders and `ResultBundle_2026-09-10_*.xcresult` bundles (agents may not).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L3 merge, 2026-10-10, ADR-052).** B-12 ("`apply`'s adoption binds by the same rule") is narrowed by ruling R3: `apply` and `admit` never adopt `absent(.evicted)` (ADR-018 amendment). The B-16 seams were replaced by real APIs except `testExpect`, `testInsertRecord` and `testRaiseApplyFence` (C-17). The WP-C assignments are done: the B-36 test and the S1-06, S2-01, S2-04, S2-07 and S2-13 regression tests exist (ARCHITECTURE §12); the per-file coverage re-check is ADR-052's open item.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L4 merge, 2026-10-17, ADR-053).** The WP-F assignments are done (`f.md` F-7, F-9, F-21): the engine half of R-NET-8 with both named tests (`R-NET-8: a refused or invalid resume discards the partial and restarts from zero in the next attempt`, `R-NET-8: an invalid resume yields resumeRejected and the engine's next attempt starts fresh`); Apple-tier dispatch and update-checking manifest fetches gated on the engine's own `DeviceConditions` (E-17), test `R-SYN-7: Apple-tier transfers and update-checking manifest fetches start only when the engine's DeviceConditions satisfy their NetworkAccess`; `R-STO-1: a cancelled, paused or failed foreground transfer leaves no byte outside the store root`; the end-to-end `R-APL-3: an asset adopted from a FakePackBackend source is served from blobs/ after its pack is released`, over WP-F's own `FakePackBackend` subset and the test-only AppleHosted dependency (L4-R1). The items of the Level 2 close's resolution (5) exist too: the R-APL-4 engine half (`R-APL-4: catalog and pack skew triggers one update check and a later retry`), R-CLN-9's relaunch case, the ENOENT rule of §7.6 (`R-SYN-4: publishing a transfer the engine cancelled treats ENOENT as that cancellation and never retries it`, D-13) and `SyncEngine.waitUntil` ending on its caller's cancellation (`R-SYN-14: waitUntil ends when its caller is cancelled, with the predicate unmet, and a later mutation resumes only the waiters still registered`). B-30 holds (F-6: the engine never skips `apply` on digest equality); B-7 is a throughput note that needs no engine change.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 merge, 2026-10-22, ADR-054).** The WP-G assignments are done: `R-NET-3: background falls back to foreground when the background-events hook is not wired` exists for the attestation half (L5-R5; ADR-026 amendment), and B-17's mirror reset before the replacement store's first install is part of the facade's reopen (G-1; test `R-STO-13: after a rootVanished reopen the facade resets the mirror before the replacement store's first install, so readers see the replacement's snapshot even when its epoch is smaller`); so is the Level 2 close's assignment (`KitContractTests.swift`'s `GatedRegistry.waitUntil` ends on its caller's cancellation, G-8, `585807a`, assertions unchanged). Still deferred: the optional timed P-9 case of the sliced release survey at 100,000 entries (E-16), not written at Level 5 and now Stage 3's (ADR-054); P-13 still awaits the owner.
- **Amendment (Stage 3, 2026-10-29): the owner's P-13 ruling, option A.** The owner ruled "P99": SPEC §6's per-batch bound for P-13 is judged on the **p99** of the deletion-unit durations between suspension points (nearest rank, `StorageBench.percentile`), as P-1 and P-9 state their latency bounds. No number changes: `batch_ms_max` stays 5 ms and `gc_total_s_max` 2 s, and `Scripts/quality-gates.json` is not edited (G1). SPEC §6's P-13 row carries the ruling. `BenchStorage.swift`'s P-13 test asserts the p99 against `batch_ms_max` and keeps printing the maximum, p50, p95, the over-budget units and the stalled unlinks; the orchestrator makes that edit after merging `wp/s3b`, and an agent that did not write it verifies it. Evidence behind the ruling (Stage 3 S3-PERF-03, confirmed; `s3b` S3B-12): one `unlink(2)` stalls in the kernel about every 850–1,060 unlinks with the fan-out layout, every 490–550 in a flat directory, whatever the store controls, so every quiet run had 2–4 units over 5 ms out of 490–562, and their on-CPU time stayed ≤ 1.02 ms. At about 560 units the nearest-rank p99 is the sixth-longest unit, so a run passes with up to five stalled units. Consequence: a run whose stalls exceed that (heavy load, which also adds preemption at the metadata queue's utility QoS) fails P-13 and is reported with its figures, never re-judged on another percentile without the owner.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).**
  - The deferred P-9 case at 100,000 manifest entries (E-16) stays optional and unwritten at Stage 3 (S3-B-093, a known miss under S3G-5).
  - B-10's deletion-unit bounds are unchanged; the queue's carry is a cursor (S3B-5). S3B-12's options (B: 15 ms; A: a percentile, p98 "robust"; C: judge actor holds) and its "longest <= batchBudget" failure are superseded by the owner's ruling; S3B-12 is that ruling's evidence, and its p98 view is information only. The P-13 records are corrected (P3, P4; not independently verified): the owner's words (29 Oct, 14:25 UTC) were "Do not set any budget stop. List P99"; the orchestrator read them as option A, recorded the reading and told the owner at 14:30 UTC, and no correction was found. Option A's premise "measured p99 ≈ 1 ms" no longer describes the ruled statistic: on `e840b0e` the p99 is itself a stalled unit, 4.31-4.89 ms, while p95 is about 0.99 ms and the on-CPU maximum about 1.0 ms. "Every quiet run had 2–4 units over 5 ms" and "a run passes with up to five stalled units" are wrong: the nearest-rank p99 tolerates 4 units over 5 ms for n < 500, 5 for 500-599 and 6 for n ≥ 600; each of the four runs on `e840b0e` had exactly 5 over (525, 528, 509 and 519 units) and the cycle-20 run at load 3.6 had 7; quiet runs have failed (8 of 494 over at load 1.95-2.56, `.build-orch/l3-bench.log:116`; 7 of 494 at 2.3-3.7; 9 of 507 at 5.24; 5 of 478 at 2.6-5.6), so a failure is not confined to heavy load. P-13's verifier (`wf_19408ffa-9ba`, PASS_WITH_NOTES, 0 blocking) estimates that about 30 % of runs fail the p99 (17 of 53, Wilson 95 % 0.21-0.45). Only the owner can change the statistic or the number (P8, owner information).
  - D-10 stays **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** with cycle 3's nuance (S3D-6, S3-B-052): the background handle's controls travel through one control task per handle in FIFO order, ending with the attach or the handle (`d.md` D-10's one short-lived task per control is superseded), so the engine's cancel can queue behind a priority change still listing and reach no OS task once `remote.invalidate()` released the session with `finishTasksAndInvalidate()` (probe on the cycle-3 tree, in a scratch copy: `PROBE shutdownCancelQueuedBehindPriority cancellations=[] priorityChanges=[]`); not a MUST and not new in kind, since each control's own task raced `invalidate()` before, and `consumerGone` stays an independent path. If the owner keeps D-10 as a guarantee, the options are that `invalidate()` cancels the live names' OS tasks before it releases the session, or that a `.cancel` control supersedes pending `.priority` controls; neither was taken, since each changes D-10's or S3-CONC-01's recorded behaviour. The engine's cancel is `cancelRunningHandles()` (`EngineShutdown.swift:158-162`), called at `EngineLifecycle.swift:495`, and `remote.invalidate()` is at `:510`. D-19.2 (S3-B-048) and the public `startDownload` (S3-B-053) stay tagged, unchanged.

## ADR-051 — iOS Simulator test build (owner request, 2026-10-09)
- **Context.** The owner runs the tests in Xcode on an iOS Simulator, and they did not build: with its trailing `*`, a test gate `@available(macOS 15.0, *)` vanishes on iOS, while the Mutex-backed fakes of `ZTronAssetTestSupport` are `@available(macOS 15.0, iOS 18.0, *)` (first error: `AppleTierFixture.swift:107:10: error: 'RecordingLogTap' is only available in iOS 18.0 or newer`). Owner requirement (2026-10-09): every test target builds under `-warnings-as-errors` and passes on the iOS Simulator as well as on macOS. The orchestrator's record says "at the iOS 15 floor", which holds as "the package floors stay iOS 15.0 / macOS 12.0": with Xcode 27.0 (27A266a) the 7 library targets build at `arm64-apple-ios15.0-simulator` and the 8 test bundles (Benchmarks included) at `arm64-apple-ios17.0-simulator`, mirroring macOS (12.0 / 14.0, ADR-041), and the tests run on the iOS 27.0 Simulator; no test builds or runs at iOS 15. Workflow `wf_ef55ec48-1f6` (a fixer and two verifiers, two cycles) on `wp/ios`, merged before Level 3 (ab4b9ef, doc follow-ups 4f83dd5 and ae61db9; tag `l3-base`). `docs/adr-drafts/ios.md` is normative detail for this ADR and stays in place: code and documents cite it by path.
- **Changes** (commits on `48271e7`). (1) `b9f2752`, the owner's own edits verbatim: `iOS 26.0` beside `macOS 26.0` on every Apple-tier test and on `AppleTierFixture`. (2) `7b0ead0`, availability only: `iOS 18.0` beside every `macOS 15.0` test gate (479 attributes in 63 test files, the orchestrator-owned `*ContractTests.swift` included, attributes only) and the three doc comments that state the convention (`FakeFileSystem.swift`, `ManualTimeSource.swift`, `CoreHarness.swift`); no test body, assertion, trait, time limit or floor changed. (3) `b671ea2`, the loopback listener no longer sets `acceptLocalOnly`, on every platform: a probe of six listener parameter sets on macOS 27 and in the iOS 27.0 Simulator (table in `ios.md` item 3) showed that `acceptLocalOnly` alone makes the simulator reset every 127.0.0.1 connection (64 failing tests before the fix); the listener stays bound to 127.0.0.1 (`requiredLocalEndpoint`) with `requiredInterfaceType = .loopback`, which keeps every other host out. (4) `b6e23a2`, pool-starvation yields: the crash enumerations (`StoreCrashTests`, `IndexCommitStateSpaceTests`, `FakeFileSystemCrashTests`, the last made `async` for this only) `await Task.yield()` at the top of each case. Their awaits into a store over `InlineBlockingExecutor` need not suspend, so a few cases held every pool thread while `R-STO-9: the live lock rejects a second opener in the same process and frees on close`, whose store resumes by continuation, waited for one and overran its one-minute limit under load (73.3 s at load about 88; evidence table in `ios.md` item 4). No assertion, trait, time limit or case count changed. Rejected: raising the limit or excluding the test (G3), and a shared gate on heavy cases (the waiting cases' own limits keep running). (5) `6a51450`, the parameters move into the internal `LoopbackHTTPServer.listenerParameters()`, pinned by `Test support: the listener is bound to 127.0.0.1 on the loopback interface and does not set acceptLocalOnly` and `Test support: a connection to the server's port on ::1 is refused and never reaches the server` (mutation check: without `requiredLocalEndpoint` both fail); `LoopbackRawClient` gains `init(host:port:)`.
- **Ratified.** `LoopbackHTTPServer.listenerParameters()` (internal static, in WP-H's TestSupport contract file; no public or package signature, no await, so no REENTRANCY row: its awaits stay under §10.3 h-5…h-9; docs gate unchanged at 1281/1281), `makeListener()`'s behaviour change, `LoopbackRawClient.init(host:port:)` (WP-H's test target), and the edits outside every work package's write set made under the owner's request: the availability attributes in 63 test files, and the per-case yields in WP-B's Core tests (`StoreCrashTests`, `IndexCommitStateSpaceTests`) and WP-H's TestSupport tests (`FakeFileSystemCrashTests`).
- **Decision: the gating convention**, normative for every later work package (`ios.md` item 2). (1) A test that uses a Mutex-backed TestSupport fake (`ManualTimeSource`, `FakeFileSystem`, `FakeLegacyDefaults`, `RecordingLogTap`, `FakeTransferHandle`, `FakeRemoteSource`, `FakeTransferBackend`, `FakeStagingSink`) carries `@available(macOS 15.0, iOS 18.0, *)` on each `@Test` function, or on the helper type or function that uses the fake, never on a `@Suite`. (2) An Apple-tier test carries `@available(macOS 26.0, iOS 26.0, *)`. (3) Every gate names both platforms: `@available(macOS N, *)` alone builds on macOS and fails on iOS. (4) In a test bundle (macOS 14.0 / iOS 17.0), APIs available at macOS 14 / iOS 17 or earlier need no gate, `.timeLimit` included (ADR-041 amendment). (5) `ZTronAssetTestSupport` builds at the package floors: both clauses on every newer API, no Swift Testing traits. (6) No new `#available`/`#unavailable` in `Sources/` (R-APL-5 allows only the one in `AppleHostedTier.swift`); test bodies may use `guard #available(…) else { return }`. (7) A work package that adds tests runs the simulator build (at least `build-for-testing`) before hand-off; `verify.sh` does not run it (`Scripts/` is frozen), so `VERIFY: PASS` alone does not show it. Every simulator test action also builds (but does not run) the benchmarks bundle, so new benchmark files and memory probes (WP-F's `BenchSync.swift`, WP-G's `BenchKit.swift`) build for iOS at the bundle's iOS 17.0 target under `-warnings-as-errors`, with the same two-platform gates. (8) A test that loops over many CPU-bound cases on the cooperative pool (crash points, fault points, seeds) awaits `Task.yield()` at the top of each case; awaits into a store over `InlineBlockingExecutor` do not count. iOS-only exclusions: none; no test is excluded, skipped or conditioned on the platform (the `#filePath` source scans, `Tests/Fixtures` and the live store directories under `.build-tests/storage-core/` work unchanged in a simulator process).
- **Commands** (`ios.md` "Commands"). From the worktree, with `TMPDIR` and `LLVM_PROFILE_FILE` in the agent's scratch path, under `caffeinate -i`: `xcodebuild -scheme ZTronAssetKit -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' -derivedDataPath <scratch>/xcode build OTHER_SWIFT_FLAGS='-warnings-as-errors' -quiet`, then `xcodebuild test -scheme ZTronAssetKit -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,id=<UDID>' -derivedDataPath <scratch>/xcode-sim -skip-testing:ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks -collect-test-diagnostics never OTHER_SWIFT_FLAGS='-warnings-as-errors'` and `xcrun xcresulttool get test-results summary --path <bundle>`. One named simulator per agent, never the owner's booted one, shut down afterwards (`xcrun simctl shutdown <UDID>`); never an `xcodebuild` without `-derivedDataPath`. `-collect-test-diagnostics never` spares a failing run up to 600 s of diagnostics collection and changes no outcome. A `.timeLimit` overrun makes `xcodebuild` kill and restart the test runner, and the tests in flight vanish from the totals (665 of 667 in one run): this cycle-2 explanation replaces cycle 1's leaked-state claim.
- **Consequences.** Package floors unchanged (ADR-005); test bundles build at iOS 17.0 / macOS 14.0. The device containers of the simulators agents run the tests on (under `~/Library/Developer/CoreSimulator`) are the one owner-accepted write outside the repository (ADR-006 amendment). Amended with this ADR: ADR-006, ADR-014, ADR-041; ARCHITECTURE header, §1.2, §2, §6.7, §13.1 issue 12, §14, §15; APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §0 Q3, §3 #21 and #24, §3.2. Two design-document claims are superseded and recorded here, because `docs/design/` is not amended (ARCHITECTURE and the ADRs supersede it): store.md (lines 416 and 1084) says Swift Testing rejects a two-platform `@available` on a `@Test` and that test targets are never compiled for iOS; sync.md (lines 749 and 1388) shows single-platform suite gates (already superseded by ADR-014).
- **Evidence** (`ios.md` "Evidence", cycle 2 on `6a51450`). Five consecutive full simulator runs (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0), each `** TEST SUCCEEDED **` with 669/669 (AppleHosted 104, Compatibility 3, Kit 12, Model 84, Storage 305, Sync 10, Transport 151), one under heavy load (peak 1-minute load average 262.7), none with a runner restart or time-limit line; the iOS library build exit 0; macOS `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS`; unsafe 0; docs 1281/1281. On the merged Level 3 head the orchestrator reports 804/804 on the iOS 27 simulator (ADR-052).
- **Not verified** (ARCHITECTURE §14). The tests run on the iOS 27.0 Simulator only: none below iOS 17, the Mutex-gated ones need iOS 18 and the Apple-tier ones iOS 26, and what they do on an iOS 17 simulator was not checked; iOS 15 runtime behaviour stays unverified (no iOS 15 simulator boots on this host, APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §2). The benchmarks bundle is built, not run, on the simulator. The loopback server's refusal of a LAN address is not probed: the `::1` probe stands in for it and assumes the host's `lo0` carries `::1` (a `getifaddrs` probe needs mutable pointers, C1). Whether a runner crash, as opposed to a time-limit overrun, also restarts the runner is taken from `xcodebuild`'s message. The ungated-`.timeLimit` rule holds for this Xcode only: a toolchain that builds test bundles at the package floor would need `iOS 16.0` beside `macOS 13.0` on each, so every toolchain change re-runs the simulator build.
- **Open** (outside the files this pass may edit). The orchestrator-owned simulator commands (`.claude/workflows/assetkit-stage2-level.js:88`, `assetkit-stage2-l3-close.js:33`, `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md`) lack `-collect-test-diagnostics never` and cite "the `docs/adr-drafts/ios.md` convention" (re-point to ADR-051), and the level script says "at the iOS 15 floor" (read as in the context above). CLAUDE.md's Commands do not mention the simulator run; agents may not edit CLAUDE.md, so adding it is the owner's choice.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 WP-I documentation merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-056).** The Consequences' "because `docs/design/` is not amended" now reads "not amended beyond the one-line status note at line 2 (WP-I, I-5; ADR-056)" (WPI-R4). The note replaced the blank line 2, so the lines cited here (store.md 416 and 1084, sync.md 749 and 1388) are unchanged.

## ADR-052 — Stage 2 level 3 merge: WP-C decisions · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (C-12, C-13, C-14, C-14 cycle 2, C-15)
- **Context.** WP-C (integrity, cleanup, eviction, legacy migration) was implemented on the merged WP-B store (`l3-base`) and independently verified: main run `wf_7bbebd31-575` (author, three verification rounds, two fix rounds; blocking 8 → 5 → 5), then close-out cycles 1–8 (`wf_23e43bcf-739`, `wf_6f1cfb40-9ad`, `wf_13af6d93-8ac`); the cycle-8 verification found 0 blocking issues in both lenses (18 non-blocking, recorded or routed). Merged into `feature/asset-kit` as 38d23f9 (traceability refreshed in 5455342). The orchestrator's checks on the merged head: `verify.sh --quick` `VERIFY: PASS`; release and iOS builds green; 804/804 tests on the iOS 27 simulator (ADR-051); unsafe 0; docs 1281/1281; trace: 7 MUSTs without a test, all WP-F's or WP-G's. The decision record is `docs/adr-drafts/c.md` (C-1…C-24, CCR-1…CCR-3, OI-1…OI-15, rows c-1…c-8 and the row amendments); that draft, with the orchestrator rulings R1–R9 and OI-15 recorded in it, is normative detail for this ADR. Where the draft and a ruling disagree, the ruling wins (see "Rulings over the draft").
- **Ratified contract changes** (checked against tag `l3-base`: no public or package signature changed anywhere in `Sources/`; the hook `applyEviction(_:)` keeps its signature). Edits of WP-B's files, ownership unchanged: (1) `doomOrphans(_:) -> [BlobKey]` in `StoreInternals.swift` (ruling R2 / CCR-1; internal like the other primitives, although the ruling calls it a package primitive): each listed key that is neither in the blob table nor in `publishing` becomes `doomed(unreferenced)` with its listed size and `eligibleAt` = now + `deletionGrace` on the awake clock (CCR-1 as made proposed `eligibleAt: uptimeNow`; the ruling's "eligible after the deletion grace" was built), leaves the launch orphans, and the state is marked `.lazy`. (2) `adoptKnownBlob` (`AssetStore+Apply.swift`) refuses `absent(.evicted)` records and binds a launch orphan only while `state.core.orphans[key]` still equals the size its re-stat confirmed (rulings R3, R1 / CCR-2; the former `withKnownIssue` test `R-CLN-2: orphan GC never sweeps a launch orphan that a running apply is about to adopt` now asserts I-2); `ApplyPlan.compute` no longer lists `absent(.evicted)` records (R3). (3) `struct ApplyEvictionChunks` (`ApplyPlan.swift`) and `private func applyEvictionInChunks(_:)` (`AssetStore+Apply.swift`): `apply` passes a plan larger than `EvictionState.chunk` (`AssetStore.evictionChunk`, 1,000) records to the hook one chunk of whole blobs per actor turn, behind its write fence, and reports the evicted ids in plan order (ruling R6 / CCR-3). WP-C's channel into WP-B (C-16 corrected): besides the primitives and WP-B's package API (`location`, `recordAccesses`, `freeSpace`, `nextDeadline`) it calls WP-B's internal `awaitOpen()`, `checkLive()`, `checkWritable()`, `runUnit(on:)` and `waitForKey(_:)` (a purge's wait for an in-flight deletion, C-5 cycle 8), reads `state.core.blobBytes` (the bytes a quarantine or a failure releases, ruling R7) and `state.core.commits.mutationSerial` (phase 3's guard, C-24, which relies on WP-B bumping it in `markDirty`), and writes `syncDue` (every purge, C-5); C-16's "reads only the fields above the bookkeeping mark" is superseded, and ARCHITECTURE §1.1 rule 1 and `StoreCoreState`'s docs now say so. WP-C's own internal state fields (§1.1 allows them): `EvictionState.chunk`, `releasedBytes`, `holderScans`; `CleanupState.chunk`, `collectingOrphans`, `orphans`, `leasedBytes`; `IntegrityState.verifiedInPass`; `MigrationState.root`, `queue`, `claimed`, `legacyIDs`, `manifestDigest`, `deferrals`, `kept`, `planCommitted`, `checkChunk`, `checkPasses`; only `migration.phase`, `migration.legacyStamp` and `cleanup.lastCleanupAt` are persisted (WP-B's codec), `kept` is in memory. Contract doc comments made true of the merged code in the L3 docs pass (comments only): `StorageReport.lastCleanupAt`, `CleanupResult.bytesFreed`/`filesRemoved`, `EvictionPlan.victims`/`bytes`, `LegacyMigrationProgress.dropped`, `StoreCoreState` (`degraded`, `records`, `stamp`, `syncDue`, `purgeEpoch`, `blobTable`, `commits`, `blobBytes`, `orphans`, the bookkeeping mark), `CleanupState.lastCleanupAt`, the `StoreInternals.swift` header and `LiveLegacyDefaults`.
- **Benchmarks** (C2, ruling R4; release, this Mac, `c.md` cycle-8 evidence, not re-run at the merge). `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/BenchCleanup.swift` (suite `CleanupBenchmarks`) carries no P-n budget, only the C2 rule's 10 ms. At 10,000 records every cleanup path holds the actor ≤ 10 ms: `purge(.evictable)` and `purge(.all)` of everything 1.90 and 1.94 ms (probe, best of three); per chunk of 1,000 (thread CPU) purge 1.05 ms, its reclaim pass 0.05 ms, `purge(.evictable)` 1.54 ms (5.99 ms after an alias was bound to each blob), `apply`'s hook 1.43 ms (worst 1.99; a whole-store plan held 13.9–20.4 ms before R6). Ruling R8's exception: evicting one blob that 10,000 ids share takes 16.2 ms in one turn in cycle 8 (15.4 ms when ruled; over 10 ms above about 6,000–6,500 holders, ~6,500 in the ruling). At `maxEntries` (100,000; recorded, OI-9; about 25–30 % higher under load): WP-B's grace start 42.3 ms and reaper queue 23.1 ms, the records copy 9.6 ms, quota eviction of half 23.6 ms, `purge(.all)` of the rest 23.2 ms, full-store purges 40.0 and 39.2 ms, orphan GC of 100,000 orphans 19.1 ms, a legacy batch of 500 17.5 ms.
- **Ratified decisions** (rulings R1–R9 and OI-15; normative detail in `c.md`).
  - *Deviations from SPEC R-STO-11 / store.md §7, ruling R5* · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**. **C-12**: a checksum-less legacy file with no valid (64 lowercase hex) local checksum is dropped and downloaded again instead of hashed inside `open()` (ADR-029 moved hashing out of `open()`; R-STO-11 adopts files "whose checksum verifies"). **C-13**: an undecodable or unusable legacy manifest degrades the launch and adopts nothing; its files are not kept as protected orphans (that needs a place-without-records primitive; R2's `doomOrphans` covers only `blobs/`) and stay in the legacy root, while phases 3–4 remove the prior art's metadata and the key with a sync due. **C-14**: a legacy root on another volume cannot be renamed into `blobs/` (EXDEV), so its files are dropped and downloaded again (store.md's copy needs a store copy primitive). **C-14 cycle-2 amendment (the legacy deferral bound)**: a legacy file whose read or move fails transiently three times in one lifetime is kept in place, counted dropped and downloaded again; attempts are counted per lifetime with no backoff, so an ENOSPC streak or an EIO burst can use all three, and phase 4 then removes `manifest.json` and the key, so the file stays unattributed for good. Its crash residual belongs to the same item (OI-11 (b)): the kept set lives in memory, so a crash after the durable "done" commit and before phase 4 finished lets the next lifetime's phase 4 remove a kept file the manifest attributes (its asset is downloaded again). A lifetime-based bound or a persisted kept set needs a STAMPS field in WP-B's codec (a CCR). **C-15**: phase 4 removes only what the prior art wrote, never recursively; files it cannot attribute and a non-empty root stay (R-STO-11 says "remove the legacy directory").
  - *Accepted without review tag (ruling R5).* **C-6**: eviction does not rank Apple-adopted assets after ordinary ones; records carry no tier and a store serves one tier at a time (ADR-027 amendment). **C-2**: eviction's budget counts the pending reclaim while `FreeSpace.budgetBytes` (WP-B, reported) does not, so the two differ for a fractional budget. **C-4**: `lastCleanupAt` is stamped only by a run that removed, doomed, evicted or purged something.
  - *Contract change requests.* CCR-1 approved (R2) and done (C-23: orphan GC dooms instead of unlinking). CCR-2 approved (R1, merge-gating) and done: the orphan entry is cleared inside `doomOrphans` in the turn that dooms the key, so R1's §1.1 rule-1 exemption was not used. CCR-3 approved (R6) and done (C-22 cycle 7 (1), row c-8; no engine cap and no exception to R4 were needed).
  - *OI-1 closed (R3).* `apply` and `admit` never re-adopt `absent(.evicted)`; only the launch adoption does (test `R-CLN-4: an apply or admit within the deletion grace never adopts an evicted asset again; only a relaunch does`). *OI-15 decided at merge:* R3 stays literal for Level 3 (`c.md`'s option (a)): an evicted record whose new content is already live in another blob is downloaded again (it dedupes at publication), a redundant download in a rare case, never a correctness issue; narrowing the refusal (option (b), which `c.md` recommends) is assigned to Stage 3.
  - *C2 (R4, R6, R7, R8).* Every cleanup path holds the actor ≤ 10 ms at 10,000 records (C-5, C-8, C-11, C-21, C-22, C-23, C-24), `apply`'s plan included (R6).
