R7: a quarantine, a recorded failure or a record's last integrity failure counts the bytes of the live blob it dooms toward a running maintenance eviction's released bytes (`EvictionState.releasedBytes`, C-2 cycle 7); the residual is WP-B's own release path, `apply`'s prune (OI-14). R8: the single-blob exception above, assigned to Stage 3.
  - *R9.* Legacy phase 3 may leave its decision to a later call (`remaining == 0 && !finished` after three interrupted passes of its chunked check); the caller calls `migrateLegacy` again while `!progress.finished` and backs off on such a return (ARCHITECTURE §4.7, RE-S29).
  - *Recorded by reference* (normative detail in `c.md`): C-1 (eviction candidates are blobs, evicted with all their records), C-3 (eviction reports bytes released to the reaper), C-5 with its amendments (purge: epoch bump and stamp clear before its first own await and again in the last chunk's turn when it purged something; chunks end at blob boundaries; the reclaim pass is chunked and waits for in-flight deletions; a purge during legacy migration leaves records still waiting for a legacy file to later batches, recorded here because store.md §6.5 is not amended), C-7 (integrity bookkeeping), C-8 (quarantine and deep verification share one path), C-9 (staging GC keeps only the caller's claims and `stagingClaimGrace`; no store-side reservation; ADR-028 amendment), C-10 (superseded by C-23), C-11 (storage report), C-16 (as corrected above), C-17 (B-16 seams), C-18 (the `legacy-v0` fixture, produced by the prior art itself; `python3 Tests/Fixtures/generate_fixtures.py --check` lists it, like WP-B's `index/v1/`, as not produced by the generator, and no gate runs that check), C-19 (signpost intervals), C-20 (the index state space with legacy phase 1: 392 initial disks), C-21 (candidates off the actor; holders from at most one scan of the records per turn), C-22 (the C2 measurements), C-23 (orphan GC dooms; its reap window), C-24 (legacy migration's actor stretches).
  - *ADR-050's WP-C assignments, done.* The B-36 test `R-STO-5: a legacy phase-1 commit before open()'s barrier with a pending promotion and a failed root sync never counts the surviving generation, and committedFile names a file that holds what it counts`; the S1-06, S2-01 (two), S2-04, S2-07 and S2-13 regression tests (ARCHITECTURE §12); the B-16 seams replaced by real APIs except `testExpect`, `testInsertRecord` and `testRaiseApplyFence` (C-17); the package initialisers of `AssetLease`, `EvictionVictim`, `EvictionPlan` and `VerificationReport` covered through real APIs (per `c.md`; the orchestrator's per-file coverage re-check is under "Open").
- **Authorised edits outside WP-C's write set** (ratified; `git diff --stat l3-base 5455342`). WP-B sources `Store/StoreInternals.swift` (R2), `Store/AssetStore+Apply.swift` (R1, R3, R6) and `Store/ApplyPlan.swift` (R3, R6). WP-B tests `Core/StoreApplyTests.swift`, `StoreDeletionTests.swift` and `StoreReentrancyTests.swift` (C-17, commit 5b36bb4: three files, not the "four" the ruling's text says). WP-A tests `Tests/ZTronAssetModelTests/Manifest/ManifestFootprintTests.swift` and `Support/DecodeMeasurement.swift` (OI-8 `2f07410` and its sibling `3ab4022`: deterministic on the iOS Simulator, bounds unchanged). WP-D tests `Tests/ZTronAssetTransportTests/TransportTestSupport.swift` and `ForegroundBackendTests.swift` (OI-10 `36dd17c`: `LoopbackRig.make(requestTimeout:)`, default 10 s, 50 s in the 500-download test, every assertion kept; no authorisation was recorded in the repository before, this records it). The new `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/BenchCleanup.swift` (R4, OI-7), nested in the benchmarks' parent suite.
- **C-9 ratified (orchestrator, Level 3 close).** The store keeps no live reservations of its own: staging GC keeps only the engine's claims, so WP-F must claim every transfer it still works on, those whose publication is in flight included (R14, RE-S39, §12.2 S2-11, ADR-028 amendment).
- **Rulings over the draft.** OI-15: option (a) as above, not the draft's (b). C-5's purge-during-migration decision is recorded here, not in store.md §6.5. C-16's lists are superseded (above). CCR-1's `eligibleAt: uptimeNow` is `uptimeNow + deletionGrace` (R2). C-21/C-22 cycle 6's "inherent" single-blob residual is withdrawn (cycle 7; R8 records it as an exception). Stale citations in the draft: C-2's round-3 amendment cites `EvictionOrder.swift:129`, now `:356` (`bytesToFree`); C-8 cites `R-INT-4: deep verification discards a hash whose blob name was replaced meanwhile…`, the test is `R-INT-4: deep verification discards a hash whose blob was quarantined or replaced meanwhile and never quarantines the new bytes`; C-10 cites `R-CLN-2: an orphan whose removal keeps failing…`, the test is `R-CLN-2: an orphan whose unlink keeps failing stays doomed with backoff and does not stop the others from being collected`. Corrections to the rulings' text: `holders(of:known:)`, named as an example addition in WP-B files, is WP-C's own internal `holders(of:known:scan:)` (`Eviction/QuotaEviction.swift`, with `HolderScan` in `Eviction/HolderScan.swift`), so there is nothing to ratify for WP-B; `doomOrphans(_:)` is internal, not package; the rulings (and `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md`) file these ratifications and the R5 tags under "ADR-051", which became the iOS Simulator build, so they are recorded here in ADR-052. The draft's "the orchestrator numbers these" for REENTRANCY rows: the identifiers are final.
- **Assignments to later work packages** (ARCHITECTURE §7.10, §11, §14 set accordingly).
  - *WP-F:* call `migrateLegacy(maxFiles:)` again while `!progress.finished`, backing off (for example to the next maintenance window) when a call returns `remaining == 0 && !finished` (R9; RE-S29; under continuous access stamps or pins only the stamp migration and the tree and key removal wait, the files are already adopted). Claim every transfer still worked on, those with a publication in flight included, in `collectStaging(keeping:)` (C-9; R14, RE-S39). Read quota eviction's and orphan GC's `bytesFreed`/`filesRemoved` as bytes and blobs released to the reaper (`runDueWork()`'s `reapedBytes` reports the unlinks); a concurrent `collectGarbage` returns an empty result at once; a run whose listing outlasts `deletionGrace` while an install is acknowledged dooms nothing and logs that the next run collects (OI-12); a purge waits in the caller's task, a caller cancelled during its waits gets `.cancelled` although the purge is committed, bytes held back by a non-acknowledging sink are in neither `bytesFreed` nor `bytesDeferred`, and each purge bumps `purgeEpoch` (twice when it purged something) (C-3, C-5, C-23). Notes: OI-15 (above); OI-14 (a maintenance eviction overlapping a pruning `apply` can free up to the pruned bytes more than needed; never met when maintenance runs apart from a sync's `apply`); OI-4 (the synchronous dry run `evictionPlan(toFree:protecting:)` holds the actor about 2.5 ms at 10,000 records and about 38 ms at `maxEntries`; moving it off the actor needs an `async` signature, a contract change with WP-F in Stage 3); OI-2 (`FakeFileSystem` makes `.barrier` and `.full` identical, ADR-049, so phase 3's durable commit before the `UserDefaults` key removal is shown by code reading only: "Not verified", and WP-F must not rely on a fake test to prove it). The engine half of R-CLN-8 carries the distinct name `R-CLN-8: the engine re-downloads an asset reportUnreadable quarantined` (§11).
  - *WP-F, WP-G, WP-K and Stage 3:* ADR-051's gating convention and simulator run for every new test.
  - *Stage 3 hardening (WP-B with the orchestrator).* (1) C-22 / OI-9: the actor stretches at `maxEntries` (records copy-on-write under a live snapshot about 9.6 ms, WP-B's present-table copy, `startGrace` over the blob table and the reaper's filter and sort, `freeSpace()` sums, the blob-table growth rehash inside an orphan doom chunk; the longest eviction and `purge(.all)` holds about 22–44 ms at 100,000, rising 25–30 % under load): a doomed-key index or a chunked grace start and reap queue, and releasing a unit's closure before the actor resumes. (2) R8: chunk one blob's holders (with C-3's `.stillReferenced`). (3) OI-15: narrow R3 to `adoptKnownBlob`'s `.doomed` and orphan branches, add probe ZP7-OI1b as a test, keep the R3 test passing. (4) The TSan headroom of WP-H's `start racing stop` test (TSan on an idle host, one agent at a time). (5) P-13, pending the owner's ruling on ADR-050's proposal. Candidates `c.md` also records: OI-14 (count `apply`'s prune releases), OI-12 (a per-key reap exclusion for orphan GC), C-24's alternative CCR (a serial bumped only by presence-reducing mutations), OI-11 (a persisted per-file attempt count and kept set, a STAMPS field), C-14's EXDEV copy primitive, C-13's place-without-records primitive, OI-4; any change to WP-B's grace start or doom rules revisits orphan GC's reap window (C-23).
  - *Environment items (WP-A, WP-D, WP-E owners).* OI-13: `R-MAN-3: seeded fuzz … in linear time` (`ManifestFuzzTests.swift:107`) failed once when one of its two decodes ran on an efficiency core (6.08 s against the 5.86 s limit); compare the lowest of a few measurements or measure at a QoS that keeps both on performance cores, without loosening the 64× bound. OI-8 audit: `ManifestAllocationTests` and the fuzz test's heap peaks still read process-wide figures (kept). OI-10: loopback tests time out under host load; whether their request timeouts should be injected or scale with load, without loosening an assertion; simulator verification on an idle host, one simulator at a time.
- **REENTRANCY rows.** The proposed rows c-1…c-8 keep their identifiers as final numbers in ARCHITECTURE §10.1 (the code cites "c-n (proposed)"; the word is historical, as for h-n and b-n). The amendments `c.md` proposed to R8, R9 (cycles 2 and 8), R10, R13 (cycles 6 and 7), R16 and c-4 are folded into those rows; the code cites the base rows, except "R10 amendment" (`OrphanCollection.swift`) and "R9 amendment (cycle 8)" (`StorePurge.swift`), which name text now in R10 and R9. R14 and RE-S39 are corrected (C-9: the store adds no reservations), R17 gains `purge`, RE-S29 takes R9. Every await in WP-C's four directories, `StoreInternals.swift` and `AssetStore+Apply.swift` has a `// REENTRANCY:` line (`c.md` cycle 8, scripted: 150 awaits, 0 missing). Two citations name a row other than the one whose text describes them: legacy phase 1's bulk unit cites "R1, R11" and phase 1's key removal without a tree "R1", both described by row c-4; recorded in §10.1, no code change.
- **Not verified** (carried to `docs/VERIFICATION.md` at Stage 5): `c.md` "Not verified here" by reference. Notably: the legacy rename on a device (Caches → Application Support on one APFS volume) and a root on another volume; `LiveLegacyDefaults`; Unicode normalisation or case differences between legacy path components and on-disk names (a mismatch drops the file); phase 3's ordered versus durable commit on a device (OI-2); the overlap guard's identity comparisons, resolved-identity walks and error mapping on a device (`/var` → `/private/var`, App Group containers, a sandbox refusing a stat above the container); deep verification's incarnation check against a real rename-over on APFS (inode reuse could hide a same-size replacement); the kept set across a crash (in memory by design, OI-11 (b)); phase 3's mutation-serial guard relies on WP-B bumping `commits.mutationSerial` in every persisted change (read, not audited across every WP-B write; tested with quarantine and pin only); OI-8's allocation measurements (Array and Dictionary ABI layout, `malloc_size` of a reused block; macOS 27 and the iOS 27 simulator only); C-22's actor holds, `apply`'s chunked eviction (c-8) and a purge's wait for another reaper's deletion on a device; the holders-scan prefilter's assumption that the first hash word spreads evenly (skew costs time only).
- **Open.** The orchestrator's merged-head runs before tag `l3-merged` (runs, not document items): `--perf` (P-13), the `CleanupBenchmarks` and whole-package TSan on an idle host, and the per-file Storage coverage export (ADR-050's re-check; `c.md` cycle 8 measured Storage 97.36 % and `LiveLegacyDefaults.swift` 3 of 14 lines covered, ARCHITECTURE §14).
- **Process.** `verify.sh` writes `.verify-logs/` inside the worktree, so two verifiers running `--quick` in one worktree overwrite each other's logs (verdicts come from their own exit status; `Scripts/` is frozen): later levels give verifiers separate worktrees or stagger `--quick`. The usage gate runs before every agent (agents in flight overshoot by up to one point). Simulator runs under heavy host load (two verifiers building plus simulators, load 50–110) expose timing flakes: agents use their own simulators under `caffeinate -i`, and sanitizer and benchmark runs go one agent at a time on an idle host.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L4 merge, 2026-10-17, ADR-053).** The WP-F assignments are done (`f.md` F-7, F-8, F-14): `migrateLegacy(maxFiles:)` is called again while `!progress.finished` and backs off to the next maintenance window on a `remaining == 0 && !finished` return (R9, RE-S29; test `R-STO-11: a migration call that leaves nothing remaining but cannot finish defers to the next maintenance window, which finishes it`); every transfer still worked on, a publication in flight included, is claimed in `collectStaging(keeping:)` (C-9, R14, RE-S39; test `R-CLN-3: staging GC during maintenance keeps the file of a publication in flight, however old`); the engine half of R-CLN-8 exists under its distinct name `R-CLN-8: the engine re-downloads an asset reportUnreadable quarantined`; OI-2 is under "Not verified" (F-14: the engine's durability ordering is verified against `FakeFileSystem`'s barrier only).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 WP-K merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-055).** WP-K's part of the ADR-051 assignment is done: every test WP-K added follows the gating convention (no macOS-only gate, none on a `@Suite`) and ran on the iOS 27 simulator (1,305/1,305 on the merged head). Under L5K-R13, L5K-R14, L5K-R17, L5K-R18, L5K-R20 and L5K-R23 WP-K edited WP-C's `Migration/LegacyPhases.swift`, `MigrationState.swift` and `StoreMigration.swift` (ownership unchanged; ARCHITECTURE §1.1): adopt-on-demand runs one adoption per legacy file whoever asks, a batch waits for an adopt-on-demand of the last claimed files and finishes in the same call, unless an adoption it waited for put its file back in the queue (which the next call processes), and a batch returns right after a coalesced publication while files remain (ARCHITECTURE §4.7).
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).**
  - OI-13 is closed by S3AH-4: each side of `R-MAN-3: seeded fuzz … in linear time` keeps the lowest of up to three CPU-time samples (`lowestDenseCPU`), the 64x ratio unchanged; a QoS pin was rejected (QoS steers a thread but does not bind it to a performance core). The test is at `ManifestFuzzTests.swift:61` and the check at `:103-109`, not `:107`. Merged-head `--full`, three runs (1 MiB samples vs limit sample, s): [0.0921, 0.0931, 0.0902] vs [2.998]; [0.0910, 0.0909, 0.0906] vs [2.955]; [0.0912, 0.0905, 0.0905] vs [2.990] (about 33x). Stage 3 hardening (4), the TSan headroom of WP-H's `start racing stop`, is closed under ruling S3-R4 (a three-minute guard with measurements, S3AH-5), not by an idle-host measurement.
  - OI-14 is resolved for `apply` and `admit` by S3B-9, with a new residual: dooms WP-C counted are not taken back when a publication rescues them (a CCR for WP-C). OI-12 is recorded by S3B-10 with a CCR for WP-C. Stage 3 assignment (1), C-22/OI-9: the reap queue's re-insertion is now a cursor (S3B-5); the chunked grace start was not done (S3B-14, beyond the reference scale), and releasing a unit's closure before the actor resumes was not addressed. Assignment (3), OI-15, is superseded by S3B-11: R3 stands, with a CCR for WP-F, so "R3 stays literal for Level 3" holds through Stage 3. Assignment (5), P-13, was ruled by the owner (ADR-050). No revisit of orphan GC's reap window is needed: S3B changed neither the grace start nor the doom rules, and `reapWindowClosed` is independent of the reaper's queue (`OrphanCollection.swift:106-109`).
  - R8's single-blob exception (16.2 ms in cycle 8) ended for quota eviction and both purges (S3C-2: cycle-2 best 4.28, 4.69 and 1.20 ms; merged-head best 4.295042, 4.733625 and 1.196541 ms) and remains only for `apply`'s hook (routed to WP-B); Stage 3 hardening (2) is done except that hook, and of its candidates C-24's alternative CCR (S3-B-027) and OI-11 (S3-B-031, a STAMPS field) were not made and OI-4 is declined for Stage 3 (S3-B-026; the dry run holds 2.56 ms at 10,000 records). C-1 ("candidates are blobs, evicted with all their records") is qualified by Decision S3C-2. The serial's reliance on WP-B's bumps is audited by reading (S3-B-035): every writer bumps in its own turn except `apply`'s fenced writes, which the manifest digest covers.
  - OI-10 is closed for the transport loopback tests only (S3D-5, `70f4059`, tests only): `LoopbackRig.heldStallTimeout` is 600 s, longer than any hang guard, so a stall that waits for the test's own action cannot end in a spurious timeout and a missed event still fails at the guard; the three tests where a firing timeout is the failure keep 10 s and `connectionReuse` 50 s. WP-F's `EngineLiveTransportTests` (the same class, `other-wp:f`) and OI-10's process half (simulator verification on an idle host, one simulator at a time) stay open.

## ADR-053 — Stage 2 level 4 merge: WP-F sync engine decisions
- **Context.** WP-F (planner and sync engine) was implemented on the merged Level 3 head and independently verified: main run `wf_3286fe9b-a79` (author, three verification rounds, two fix rounds; blocking 13 → 11 → 8), then 22 close-out cycles under the owner's strict bar (every cycle hunts for new bugs; any proven bug blocks): `wf_0989cd6c-62c` cycles 1–10, `wf_d8affeb1-4b4` 11–19, `wf_7e52a3a2-ebc` 20–22, with blocking issues per cycle 1, 3, 1, 3, 1, 1, 1, 5, 3, 2; 1, 1, 1, 4, 3, 1, 3, 2, 3; 1, 1, **0**. Cycle 22's verification (both lenses, HEAD `8a3f5ad`) found no blocking issue: `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS`, release and iOS builds green, Sync three times and under TSan clean, Sync coverage 93.22–93.33 %, 1,100/1,100 on the iOS 27 simulator, the gate model over 2,000 fresh seeds, every WP-F benchmark passed. Merged into `feature/asset-kit` as 331653b (traceability refreshed in f4d78ec). Weekly usage 13 % → about 64 %. The decision record is `docs/adr-drafts/f.md` (F-1…F-42, rows f-1…f-14, CCR-1 and the "Orchestrator, at merge" paragraph); that draft, with the orchestrator rulings L4-R1…L4-R25 (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md`, which is not in the repository; L4-R1…L4-R21 are quoted in the draft's "Orchestrator rulings (close-out)", and L4-R22…L4-R25, the merge rulings, are recorded in this ADR), is normative detail for this ADR. Where the draft and a ruling disagree, the ruling wins (see "Rulings over the draft"). No new NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW item arises from Level 4 (L4-R25): L4-R1 adds an internal test-only dependency, not a third-party one, and no public API, storage location, on-disk format or platform floor changed.
- **Ratified contract changes** (`git diff 64e41d9 331653b`: no public or package signature changed in `Sources/`; `SyncContractTests.swift` untouched). (1) `Planning/SyncPlan.swift`: `SyncPlanner.plan`'s body calls `PlanBuilder`; the doc of `SpaceAssessment.insufficient` states F-22's convention (`required` the plan's known bytes, `available` the room it had). (2) `Engine/SyncEngine.swift`: the stored `store`, `environment` and `planner` go from private to internal; new internal `tuning`, `log` and `var state`; the initialiser clamps the tuning; the entry points forward to `perform…` bodies; `diagnostics()`'s doc states L4-R9. (3) `Engine/EngineSupport.swift`: the package struct `CircuitBreaker` gains the internal `admission(of:at:)`, `probe` and `publicState` and private stored fields, and `admit` applies `admission`; its package API (`admit`, `record`, `nextDeadline`, `Admission`) is unchanged. So the draft's "no member was added to a contract type" (its CCR paragraph) is stale: internal members were added, and no signature changed. `JobState.swift` and `Refresh/RefreshDecision.swift` are unchanged in code; this pass amended `JobStateKind.allows`' doc comment (§7.1's new triggers).
- **Authorised edits outside WP-F's write set** (ratified; ownership unchanged). `Package.swift`: `ZTronAssetSyncTests` gains a test-only dependency on `ZTronAssetAppleHosted` beside its test-only Transport one (L4-R1, orchestrator commit `7cf647c`); Sync's production dependencies stay Model and Storage, and `ZTronAssetTestSupport` gains none. WP-C's `Sources/ZTronAssetStorage/Integrity/StoreIntegrity.swift`: the `recordFailure(_:identity:reason:)` guard, body and doc only (L4-R5, L4-R16). WP-C's tests: the `failure` case of `R-CLN-4: bytes a quarantine or a recorded failure dooms while quota eviction computes its candidates count toward its need` (`Cleanup/EvictionOverlapTests.swift`) retargeted to a stale record, its assertions on the doomed bytes unchanged; the new `R-NET-6: recordFailure leaves a record present at the failed content unchanged, and still fails a stale one` (`Cleanup/IntegrityTests.swift`), failing first at `7cc8e94`, fix `fa7f160`. None is a pre-existing CDN test, so `MIGRATION.md` does not apply. WP-F's own `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/FakePackBackend.swift` is a subset of WP-E's fake with a header naming its source and ADR-040 (L4-R1); WP-E's files are untouched.
- **Benchmarks** (release, this Mac; `f.md` F-42's figures at the last code commit `f54417c`, 1-minute load 23.6 at the start and 2.8 at the end; not re-run at the merge). P-4 11.41 ms (budget 20; doubling ratio 2.019, max 2.5); P-7 2.17 and 0.22 MiB (48); P-11 speedup 3.617 (min 3.0; a lower bound of the steady state, because its timed sync overlaps the launch run, F-12); P-14 0 timers, 0 tasks. `BenchSync.swift` (suite `SyncBenchmarks`) also holds ten C2 benchmarks of WP-F's actor turns at 10,000 members (the probe chain at 1,000), asserted on the best of three against the C2 rule's 10 ms (`c2Budget`, a literal, the level-3 ruling R4 precedent of `BenchCleanup` (ADR-052) applied to Sync; some also against P-9's `heartbeat_gap_ms_p99_max` on the median), and one ratio benchmark of a 10,000-id prune announced to a stalled subscriber (1.425, max 2). C2 figures: space parking 3.19 and 2.52 ms, space re-admission 3.75 ms, network edges 1.00 and 3.57 ms, handle releases 3.67 and 3.74 ms, re-joins 4.36 and 4.14 ms, a cancelled resolve 0.017 ms, a cooldown's end 4.637 ms, the probe chain 2.093 ms per turn (totals 2.59–2.69 s for 1,000 parked; L4-R22), a throttle extension 6.390 ms. In cycle 21 one run of the throttle extension missed 10 ms at a 1-minute load of 60 (best 10.88 ms); low-load re-runs passed at 6.3–6.8 ms, a miss caused only by load (rulings, "Process"). P-13 (WP-B) still fails and awaits the owner (ADR-050, L4-R11).
- **Ratified decisions** (rulings L4-R1…L4-R25; normative detail in `f.md`).
  - *Approved as decided.* **F-15 / L4-R2**: a run whose manifest fetch passed `manifestFetchDeadline` falls back to the applied manifest, ends `.complete` with `manifestFresh == false`, writes no stamp and leaves the sync due (§7.10). **F-16 / L4-R3**: an offline run without a network edge is retried once after `pendingRunDebounce`, then only on a satisfied-path edge (`PendingRun.awaitsNetworkEdge`); one bounded retry, not polling, so R-SYN-13 holds; the one-retry-per-edge bound is pinned by a test that fails under mutant M-EDGESTART (L4-R18; §7.6, §7.10). **F-20 / L4-R4**: an ENOSPC whose fresh free-space read shows that the volume holds the job is retried with backoff within the lane's retry budget (with no pending reclaim; see Acknowledged at merge (3)), so a volume that keeps answering ENOSPC while reading roomy ends in a typed `insufficientSpace` (`R-SYN-11: a volume that keeps answering ENOSPC while it reads roomy ends in insufficientSpace once the retry budget is spent`; §7.6).
  - *F-30's downloading → queued triggers* (L4-R18): a policy pause that arrives when its lane is admitted again is re-queued at the front; an `.offline` failure that arrives after a satisfied-path edge was applied while the attempt ran, with the path satisfied now, is re-queued at once, at most once per edge (the attempt records `networkEdgeSerial` when it starts) (§7.1, §7.6).
  - *The gate design of F-40, F-41 and F-42* (L4-R20, the stale-gate race family as a class). One pure evaluation of the origin's gates at `now` (`OriginGate`, `originGate(at:)`: the throttle's end against `now` and `CircuitBreaker.admission(of:at:)`, shared with the dispatch gate) is read by every decision site: a run's settling and the end it reports, a pending run's start, a joiner's fail-fast answer, a promotion and a user's backoff skip; a claim parked by a gate that admits is re-admitted in that turn (`readmitOpenedOriginGates`). Every probe exit without a verdict re-admits in that turn the jobs the breaker parked and owes a pump; a pending run that waits for a breaker admitting only a probe starts after the pump that dispatches those jobs (`dispatchOwed`); a run that starts while the breaker admits only a probe holds that probe for its manifest fetch from its start turn (§7.7, §7.10; ADR-031 amendment). The model test L4-R20 item 4 asked for exists (`R-SYN-6: seeded walks over clock moves, held alarm turns, probe outcomes, 429s and space events never settle, start or park against a gate that admits at now, and never repeat runs without the clock moving`, 160 seeded walks, `GATE_MODEL_SEEDS`; and its one-reserved-slot variant, F-42; invariants I1–I6, §14). It fails on the unfixed engine for both members L4-R20 named: B at seed 2 on `d5191e2`, A at seed 17 on MUT-BONLY (the unfixed engine plus only the probe-exit fix), as F-40 records.
  - *Presence, space and catch-up (L4-R5, L4-R6, L4-R7, L4-R10, L4-R16; see Acknowledged at merge (1), (2)).* The engine never downloads an id the store holds present at the entry's current content: runs, `ensureAvailable`, `followApplied`/`reroute` and joins consult a binding ledger fed by `report.reused` of an apply, the locations of commits and presence reads, and a job's first attempt asks the store before any bytes (`answeredFromStore`, f-11). A failed transfer never marks a present record failed: the engine re-reads presence (f-10) and the store's guard leaves a record bound to a live blob at the failed identity unchanged (L4-R16; ADR-032 amendment). `state.freeSpace` keeps the measured reading; only the planner and `ensureAvailable`'s own space decision see the important-for-opportunistic copy; an attempt that fails with ENOSPC discards its partial or keeps it for resume through a sidecar (L4-R6). A free-space read is adopted in the turn it returns, with the bytes committed since it began kept in the ledger; catch-up announces the ids the store's view dropped (`assetRemoved(.pruned)`), takes the view's generation and sends `snapshotChanged` (L4-R7). A store manifest ahead of the engine after a failed index commit is by design (ADR-050); the engine follows it through catch-up (L4-R10). Space decisions: §7.6's `insufficientSpace` row and §7.10's Space bullet (F-20, F-22, F-25…F-29, F-37, F-38).
  - *The engine's own apply turn (F-25).* The engine sends one `apply` at a time to the store and the next only after it followed the previous one (`takeApplyTurn`/`passApplyTurn`, f-13), so it follows its applies in the store's commit order whatever the task priorities (§7.10).
  - *Throttle answers (F-36…F-39, L4-R23).* A user-initiated request waits only while the throttle ends within `interactiveMaxWait`, the bound covering its whole wait; a later 429 that extends the throttle beyond it fails those interests fast with `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)`, at most `parksPerPump` jobs per turn; every answer to a refusal by the throttle reports the origin's end as read when it answers; each extension moves a throttle-gated pending run to the new end. **L4-R23 (decided: (a), by design):** an `ensureAvailable` whose manifest is in hand reports its scope, and a member a throttle extension answered counts as missing, as members the gate fails fast at admission always did; a fresh call whose manifest fetch the throttle refuses, and whose scope the store cannot answer, fails at once with `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)` carrying the time left. This closes F-37's option (b) and F-38 (a)/(b); SPEC R-SYN-10 does not decide between them (§7.6).
  - *Lane queues (F-30, F-31; L4-R18).* Each lane-queue entry keeps its place; a scan sets aside, once, the entry of a job only released groups want; an entry that regains an interest returns to its place, `restoreAside` merges by place, members re-joined in one turn keep their order and never pass earlier queued work beyond R-SYN-3's lane rules, and compaction also runs when jobs leave (§7.4). A re-join that changes lane, or comes from user work, still goes to the back through `relane` (L4-R24(4), Stage 3).
  - *Engine invariants (F-1, F-31, F-33, F-34, F-35, F-42).* `invariantViolations()`, which the model tests assert after every operation, includes the reachability rule (every queued job can still be dispatched) and "no probe stays held for a manifest fetch that is neither in flight nor about to begin"; the full list is in §7.1.
  - *Diagnostics while degraded (L4-R9).* `SyncEngine.diagnostics()` answers while the engine is degraded or its store cannot answer, with the error ring, the last-known generation (never older than one the engine announced; 0 if none was ever read) and the last-known stamp or `nil`; after `shutdown()` it fails `.shutDown` (ADR-042); `metrics()` is unchanged; no signature changed (test `R-OBS-3: diagnostics answer while the engine is degraded, with its error ring and the last-known generation and stamp`; §7.10).
  - *Tests (L4-R8, L4-R12, L4-R14, L4-R15, L4-R17).* L4-R8: `R-SYN-12: a stalled subscriber's backlog never exceeds one slot per progress key and subject during a 10,000-asset sync` went from a 3-minute to a 4-minute `.timeLimit` together with a 15 % sanitizer cost cut (F-21); after F-33's priority floor it measured 18–19 % of the limit in-suite under TSan at a load about 25. Before that fix: the 103.8 % run at median load 125.6 in cycle 5, the 52.1 % run at load about 23 in cycle 7, and the in-suite TSan overruns F-33 was written to fix, 257.9 s and 258.1 s against the 240 s limit at a load about 25 (cycle 13, F-17), reproduced at 258.1 s (F-33); the gate model runs under `.timeLimit(.minutes(3))`. L4-R12: the coverage gate is per target (Sync ≥ 90 %, measured 93.22–93.35 % in cycle 22's runs); per-file margins are not a gate. L4-R14: the two tests written after their fixes (the F-15 assertion and `R-SYN-4: a resolve that joins a job waiting to be online is answered offline at once`) are not rewritten; the requirements verifier's check is to re-apply each old defect as a mutant and show the test failing against it. L4-R15: this level's own tests that changed without weakening an assertion are listed in F-20 (three), F-21 (six), F-22 (one expected verdict), F-23 (a retag), F-24 (two), F-25 (three), F-26 (one), F-29 (four), F-31's table and F-40 (two cycle-19 tests); none is a CDN test, so `MIGRATION.md` does not apply. L4-R17: every scheduling-dependent WP-F test and helper was enumerated and converted to condition waits that end on cancellation (F-31's table; the §14 test rules).
  - *Stops and scope (L4-R11, L4-R19, L4-R21).* The weekly-usage gates of the close-out (60 %, then 65 % under the owner's 70 %); P-13, the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage stub and the P-1/P-8/P-9/P-12 budgets are outside WP-F (`outside-wp`).
  - *L4-R13.* Rows f-1…f-14 keep their numbers in ARCHITECTURE §10.2 (below).
  - *Recorded by reference* (normative detail in `f.md`): F-1 (one state value, owned tasks keyed by spawn id, token-keyed joins), F-2 (the planner mirrors `apply`; its space verdict as corrected by F-22), F-3 and F-4 (as superseded by F-40…F-42), F-5 (store deadlines after a publication), F-6 (unknown identifiers: one point fetch per refetch interval, never a wrong `notFound`), F-7 (launch reconciliation and staging claims, C-9), F-8 (legacy migration loop, R9), F-9 (Apple-tier gating on the engine's own conditions, E-17; the engine half of R-NET-8), F-10 (an engine without a remote source fails fetches at once), F-11 (log lines carry no identifiers or error details, R-OBS-1), F-12 (benchmarks), F-13 (the test harness drives the clock without timing out a fetch), F-14 (not verified), F-17…F-19, F-21…F-29, F-31…F-39 (the close-out cycles' fixes, each with its failing test first, and their dispositions).
- **Acknowledged at merge** (the draft asks for each; no ruling covered them; the code was checked against each statement; (1)–(4) bend the letter of L4-R5, L4-R4 and L4-R14; the orchestrator confirmed all four at merge, ruling L4-R26). (1) *L4-R5's "never creates a job" holds as "never downloads"* (F-23): after the binding ledger is dropped (a store mutation that threw, a maintenance pass, a quarantine), a job may exist for a present id for one store read per id before its first attempt asks for bytes (`answeredFromStore`), and it downloads nothing; the resolve-registration and prefetch-join ledger checks are then optimisations (mutants M-RESOLVEREG and M-JOIN cost one job and one read per id, never a download). (2) *Relocations do not feed the ledger* (F-21), against L4-R5's literal feed list (`report.reused ∪ report.relocated`): `AssetStore+Apply` relocates absent records too (`diff.metadataChanged`), so feeding relocations would make a run skip fetching an absent asset; a relocation keeps the content, so no job moves and nothing is downloaded twice (`EngineRuns.swift`, the comment where only `report.reused` is fed). (3) *ENOSPC with pending reclaim waits `awaitingSpace`, counted against the retry budget, without a timed backoff* (F-27…F-30): this follows F-20's approved order (pending reclaim → `awaitingSpace`), and L4-R4's condition holds (the attempt counts, so a volume that keeps answering ENOSPC ends in a typed `insufficientSpace`: two starts for a resolve or an ensure, four for a run); when nothing reclaims on time the settlement's fresh read re-queues the job at once, so those attempts run back to back (PROBE-C6R-1: 2 starts in 0.0 s). F-27's option (c), a backoff deadline for that re-queue, needs a new transition or deadline kind (`blocked → failed` is not in the table) and is not taken. (4) *L4-R14's treatment extends to the other pins written after their fixes*: F-42's three (`eb936d2`: the throttled manifest probe's release, the cooldown-turn retry, the joined pre-trip fetch), F-22's `0fde299`, F-23's `9111c5d` and F-28's `0ffc058`; each fails under a named mutant that re-applies its defect, which is L4-R14's own test (CLAUDE.md P3). (5) *Room an admitted-ahead job leaves when it ends without committing* (F-28, corrected in F-38): a job that fits alone but not behind the jobs admitted ahead of it stays parked `insufficientSpace` until a later free-space read; for runs, `checkRunSettled` re-checks the space-parked jobs before it settles a run whose claim the ledger admits (F-38), while other parked jobs wait for the next read.
- **Rulings over the draft** (stale text; ADR-053 and ARCHITECTURE hold what is true). F-2's `insufficient(required: need + reserve, available: volumeOpportunistic)` is superseded by F-22 (`required: need, available: room`, `PlanBuilder.swift`). F-4 ("the alarm's breaker deadline only unblocks jobs and fulfils the pending run"; "the first transfer after the cooldown is the probe") carries no correction although F-40 says "F-3, F-4 and F-39 corrected in place": a pending run starts only through `reconsiderPendingRun`, and a starting run reserves the probe (F-42). The CCR paragraph's "no member was added to a contract type" is stale (above), and its request to list R-APL-3 as deferred or not verified is obsolete (L4-R1 resolved it). The merge paragraph's and F-39's "stays unsettled until the alarm's turn" is replaced by F-40's re-admission in the settling turn. F-14 places the settlement's `continue` in `EngineTimers.swift`; it is in `EngineJobs.swift` (`sweepSpaceParked`). F-31 calls `SyncEngine.clockFence()` a test seam; it is a test-target extension (`EngineHarness.swift`), not production code. F-21, F-22 and F-23 say the store guard was not added; L4-R16 added it in cycle 11 (F-31). F-29's benchmark names predate the C2 suffix; `BenchSync.swift`'s display names are current. The merge paragraph's requests to note P-11 and two benchmarks in `TRACEABILITY.md` are re-targeted: that file is generated and holds per-ID counts only (the benchmarks are counted under R-SYN-5), so P-11's note is in §11's P-11 row and the C2 benchmarks in §1.2 and §11. The draft's "the orchestrator numbers these" for REENTRANCY rows: the identifiers are final.
- **Design text superseded** (`docs/design/` is not amended; ARCHITECTURE and the ADRs supersede it, ADR-051). sync.md §8.2 (line 656), "a `userInitiated` request waits if `until − now ≤ interactiveMaxWait`": the bound covers the request's whole wait, and a later extension fails it fast (F-36). sync.md §8.3 (line 674), "the half-open probe is the next run's manifest fetch": it now holds whenever a run starts while the breaker admits only a probe, not only when no re-admitted job takes the probe first (F-41, F-42). F-36 and F-37 cite "sync.md §7.6" for the wait rule; the rule is in §8.2.
- **Assignments to later work packages** (ARCHITECTURE §8, §11, §12, §14 set accordingly).
  - *Stage 3 hardening.* (L4-R22) Lazy re-admission of probe-chain exits with an ordered index of the parked set, O(log n) per exit, and a new bound on the chain's total under SPEC goal 4 (battery); baseline `R-SYN-5: a chain of half-open probes ending without a verdict over 1,000 prefetch members parked by the breaker holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn, and reports the chain's total time` (1,000 parked 2.6–3.6 s, 10,000 about 161 s; longest turn 2.1–3.0 ms; the quadratic total is accepted for Level 4). (L4-R24(1)) Move `reserveManifestProbe()` after `state.run = run` and before `spawn`, and add an observer-side `invariantViolations()` assertion to the start-turn test; F-42's caution: a first draft that reserved after `spawn` failed the start-turn test, because `spawn` already notifies with the run in place, so the reservation must stay before `spawn`. (L4-R24(2)) `ManualTimeSource.advance(to:)` lands 1 ns short of a boundary in about 2.9 % of gaps (`Double` truncation in `MonotonicInstant.adding(seconds:)`; WP-H's file; WP-F's tests use exact moves meanwhile). (L4-R24(3)) a presence guard for `recordIntegrityFailure`. (L4-R24(4)) a re-join that changes lane, or comes from user work, goes to the back through `relane`. (L4-R24(5)) L4-V3-12, the shared-content late joiner's second download, with OI-15 (ADR-020 amendment). (L4-R24(6)) whether release builds copy WP-B's blob table per publication in `gainReferenceInPlace`. Routed here (the draft raises them, no ruling assigns them): WP-B's `FreeSpace.pendingReclaim` counts doomed bytes for which `nextDeadline()` schedules nothing (shown for bytes pruned behind an unacknowledged install: `pendingReclaim` 300, `nextDeadline()` nil; seen after an `apply` that threw, log `single3/r8-7.log`), so WP-B may want `nextDeadline()`/`runDueWork()` to schedule that work (the engine works around it with a fresh read, ADR-022 amendment); the C2 turns still linear and unmeasured at 10,000 and the prune announcement's ~35 ms estimate at 100,000 ("Not verified"); an engine-side assertion for R-SYN-15's join-before-close order (store-1 S1-09; mutants M5/M6 pass because `close()` drains) and a liveness phase for the model tests (ALARM-STUCK passes the model at its seed, F-34); the §12 regression names not written at this merge (§12.3 S1-01 (two), S1-07, S1-11, S1-12, S1-15's run bound, and the unwritten halves of S1-09 and S2-01; §12.4 S2-09, S2-10, S2-19; §12.5 T2-06's cross-launch collision case).
  - *WP-G (Level 5).* L4-R23: keep the facade's `ensureAvailable` result faithful, so that a throttled-out member surfaces as missing with the report's `manifestFresh` and a fresh call the throttle refuses throws `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)` (§8). `diagnosticReport()` can rely on `SyncEngine.diagnostics()` answering while degraded (L4-R9). Still WP-G's under L4-R11: the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage stub (37.82 %) and the P-1/P-8/P-9/P-12 benchmarks. Three §12 regression names whose findings are the facade's: §12.3 S1-05's `R-CAR-3: a neighbour that becomes current is promoted without a second transfer`, §12.5 T2-11's `R-NET-3: handling background events before start opens the store first and runs no sync` and §12.4 S2-13's `P-8: the fallback chain's bundle step does no work on the main thread` (§11 rows R-CAR-3, R-NET-3 and P-8; the last added to the P-8 row at this merge, so the Level-5 script picks it up); the other benchmark names of §12.4 (S2-12, S2-14, S2-18) are already §11's P-8, P-9 and P-12 rows.
  - *WP-K.* Revisit L4-R23 only if the compatibility shims need a retry-after for the prior art's behaviour.
  - *Environment item (WP-D's owner).* Two loopback cancellation tests failed once on the iOS Simulator at a load up to 468.9 (`ForegroundBackendTests.swift:162,164` and `:200,202`), like OI-10.
  - *Owner and orchestrator.* P-13 (WP-B) awaits the owner's ruling on ADR-050's proposal (L4-R11). If P-11 keeps missing its minimum on idle runs, its measurement needs an orchestrator ruling, never a lower budget (G1; F-37).
- **REENTRANCY rows.** The proposed rows f-1…f-14 keep their identifiers as final numbers in ARCHITECTURE §10.2 (L4-R13; the code cites "f-n (proposed)"; the word is historical, as for h-n, b-n and c-n). f-2's await cell also names launch reconciliation's sidecar read, which the code cites as f-2. RE-S16's await cell is widened to the alarm-turn test seam and `fireDue()`, and RE-S21's to an owned task's single hop onto the actor (about 26 of its 28 citations), as the code cites them; RE-S35's re-validated cell names the store's guard (L4-R16). A scripted count over `Sources/ZTronAssetSync` at the merge: every f-n is cited at least once, no f-n is cited that the draft lacks, and every other citation names an RE-S row.
- **Not verified** (carried to `docs/VERIFICATION.md` at Stage 5): `f.md` F-14 and the residue bullets of F-20…F-42 by reference (ARCHITECTURE §14). Notably: OI-2, `FakeFileSystem`'s barrier equals a full sync, so the engine's durability ordering (a publication acknowledged only after the store's commit; reconcile committed before the first transfer) is verified against that model only; device-only behaviour: real background `URLSession` relaunches, real Apple-hosted packs (R-APL-3 runs end to end over WP-F's `FakePackBackend` only), real `NWPathMonitor`, thermal and Low Power Mode signals; P-11's speedup as a lower bound of the steady state (F-12). The binding-ledger windows: the resolve-registration and prefetch-join checks have no deterministic probe; the one-await window after the last presence read stays open for `recordIntegrityFailure` and the pre-transfer read (only a legacy adoption or an apply's adoption of an orphaned blob of the same content can bind the id in it); a 404 job's waiters for an id bound during `recordFailure`'s await are answered `notFound` while the store serves it (F-14, F-32); the ledger's invalidation after a store mutation that throws is shown only for a failed index commit (PROBE-C1-N1); the `followStoreView` and `ledgerForgets` guards are pinned at function level only; a holder gained between `blobHolders` and a quarantine is not announced or healed (F-23, f-12). The space interleavings: the real-priority form of two committed applies followed out of order (a seam stands in); WP-B's unscheduled reclaim after an `apply` that threw, not reproduced; a purge's reaper against the engine's alarm on a busy actor (a store clock running ahead stands in); two alarm runs whose store work overlaps (`storeWorkRuns`, unpinned); bytes reclaimed by maintenance are re-checked at the next deadline or read, by design; a settlement read racing a newly armed store deadline (the `continue` in `EngineJobs.swift`, unpinned); a manifest copy changing between two join batches of a prefetch group (branch uncovered). C2 at scale: the first turn of each re-admission event (collect and sort, a few ms at 10,000), `registerGroup`'s de-duplication and sort (about 3 ms), retries that fall due together in one alarm turn and shutdown are linear in one turn and not measured at 10,000; the 10,000-id prune announcement takes about 3.5 ms in one turn and is estimated, not measured, at about 35 ms at `maxEntries` (F-20). Test fidelity: R-SYN-15 S1-09's join-before-close order has no engine-side assertion; a completion racing the engine's cancel when a kept drained file is discarded is shown by reading only; a 404 whose presence reads overlap shutdown and a transfer request after shutdown began are hardened, not produced; the harness priority floor means the engine harnesses no longer reproduce production's utility/default QoS gap (the benchmarks keep production priorities, F-33); the model test has no liveness phase (F-34); the gate model's outcome kinds were not extended, and six F-40 site changes have no reachable difference and no test (F-41; §14); condition observers can see a half-started run inside `startRun` (L4-R24(1)); release builds' copy of the blob table in `gainReferenceInPlace` was not checked (L4-R24(6)).
- **Open.** Nothing. The orchestrator confirmed "Acknowledged at merge" (1)–(4) at merge (ruling L4-R26: each matches the intent of L4-R5, L4-R4 and L4-R14 and is backed by the code and a named mutant); F-27's option (c), a backoff deadline for the `awaitingSpace` re-queue, is not taken and may be revisited in Stage 3 if a device test shows churn. The test-file doc comment of `EngineGateModelTests.swift` and the code comment at `EngineRuns.swift` that cite "ARCHITECTURE §7.6/§7.7: the half-open probe is the run's manifest fetch", and `SyncPlan.swift`'s and `EngineFailures.swift`'s citations of §7.10 for the `insufficientSpace` figures, became true with this merge's §7.7 and §7.10 amendments.
- **Process.** Concurrent verifier runs, each with its own simulator, pushed the host load past 100 (up to about 470) and shrank the 10,000-asset test's margin to about 1.6×: serialise the verifiers' simulator runs (F-17). Idle `swiftpm-testing-helper` processes 27626, 52290 and 53148 from earlier verifiers' scratch paths were left for the orchestrator (agents never run an unscoped `pkill`). About 120 `await task.value` joins in WP-F's tests were not converted to cancelling joins; only one outlived a cancellation, and the rest were noted for verifiers (F-31). F-32's flake fix had no failing test committed first, because splitting the loopback server's writes needs WP-H's `LoopbackHTTPServer`; its evidence is the scratch probes SPLITSTALL3 and SPLITSTALLN (a recorded exception to CLAUDE.md P3). A strict close-out bar converged only once a recurring bug family was demanded as a class (L4-R17 for scheduling-dependent tests, L4-R20 for stale gates): demand the enumeration as soon as a class repeats, as ADR-050's process note says. Two verifiers sharing a worktree take `--quick` verdicts from their own exit status; benchmarks and TSan on a loaded host report the load average, and a budget miss caused only by load is not blocking, while a time-limit overrun is (rulings, "Process").
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 merge, 2026-10-22, ADR-054).** The WP-G assignments are done: L4-R23 holds in the facade (G-5; tests `R-SYN-10: the facade's ensureAvailable throws serverUnavailable with the time left when the origin's throttle refuses its manifest fetch` and `R-SYN-10: the facade's ensureAvailable reports a member a throttle answered as missing with a fresh manifest`); `diagnosticReport()` relies on L4-R9 (`R-OBS-3: diagnosticReport answers while the store cannot be opened, with the degraded lifecycle and empty storage figures`); the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage stub (37.82 %) is now 97.83 %; P-1, P-8 (both names), P-9 and P-12 pass; the three §12 names exist, `R-CAR-3: a neighbour that becomes current is promoted without a second transfer` with the engine's pending claims (L5-R1). Its "Not verified" C2 item `registerGroup`'s de-duplication and sort is measured now: 3.44–3.51 ms of a 4.27–4.40 ms registering turn at 10,000 members (a release-mode probe on macOS 27 at a load of about 4.9; `g.md` L5-R1 figures).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 WP-K merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-055).** The WP-K note is closed: the shims never call `ensureAvailable` (MIGRATION.md only recommends `client.availability(scope:)` to apps, K-5), so L4-R23 was not revisited. Under L5K-R1, L5K-R14, L5K-R16, L5K-R17, L5K-R18, L5K-R21, L5K-R23 and L5K-R25 WP-K edited WP-F's engine (ownership unchanged; ARCHITECTURE §1.2): the pre-transfer read adopts while legacy migration is pending (rows f-11 and k-4), the parked launch run is settled (a run that starts while it waits takes its place), every store call that can leave an install due is followed by the RE-S40 re-read, and `drive` sends no request for an attempt the reads left no longer current (ARCHITECTURE §7.1, §7.10).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 WP-I documentation merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-056).** "Design text superseded"'s "`docs/design/` is not amended" now reads "not amended beyond the one-line status note at line 2 (WP-I, I-5; ADR-056)" (WPI-R4). The note replaced the blank line 2, so sync.md lines 656 and 674 are unchanged.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).**
  - L4-R24(2) is closed by S3AH-3: `ManualTimeSource.advance(to:)` moves in nanoseconds (WP-H) and `MonotonicInstant.adding(seconds:)` rounds to the nearest nanosecond (WP-A, the authorised body of `Environment/Time.swift`). Before the fix 651 of 20,005 seeded gaps missed and the `ManualTimeSource` test had 450 issues over 2,004 gaps; after it the whole suite passes, so no test relied on the shortfall, and "WP-F's tests use exact moves meanwhile" is historical.
  - §12.5 T2-06's cross-launch collision case is written (S3B-8, `1d5bf8c`). L4-R24(6) is answered: release builds do not copy the blob table (S3-B-016: 0.103 ms for 1,000 gains at 100,000 blobs). The routed item on WP-B's `FreeSpace.pendingReclaim` is answered by S3-B-015: not reproduced, and only the documented states apply. L4-R24(5), the late joiner with OI-15, stays with S3B-11's CCR.
  - L4-R24(3), a presence guard for `recordIntegrityFailure`, is done (S3C-9; failing test `70e1dba`, fix `100d1c2`); in "Not verified" the one-await window is closed store-side for `recordIntegrityFailure` and stays open for the pre-transfer read.
  - The environment item (WP-D's loopback cancellation tests at load up to 468.9) is resolved by S3D-5. §12.4 S2-19's regression test is written (WP-D, `c69327e`; its race fixed by `22d1e78`).
  - WP-F's Stage 3 close-out (S3F-12 to S3F-15): in *Stage 3 hardening*, L4-R22 is done by S3-B-063 (`e575681`: lazy re-admission through `CircuitParkedOrder`; a breaker that admits only a probe re-admits one parked job in the pump's order and the others wait while it takes the probe, and a closed breaker re-admits all of them, `readmissionsPerTurn` a turn), its baseline now the 10,000-member probe chain ("R-SYN-5: a chain of half-open probes ending without a verdict over 10,000 prefetch members parked by the breaker holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn, and reports the chain's total time", about 1.1 s); L4-R24(1) is done by S3-B-064 (`e21472b`); L4-R24(4) is changed by S3-B-065 (`6bb4c1e`) in its scoped form: a job only released groups wanted that a new interest joins is reinstated at its place in the queue its lane now selects, whatever lane the interest brings, and `relane`'s back-of-queue re-queue stays for jobs that waited in their queue all along. *Routed here:* the C2 turns are measured at 10,000 (`dueRetriesTurns`, `shutdownTurns`, `pruneAnnouncementTurns` and the re-admission first turns; S3F-15), and only the ~35 ms estimate at `maxEntries` (100,000) stays unmeasured; R-SYN-15's engine-side join-before-close assertion is done (S3F-14, `closingStore`; `EngineSync1HalvesTests.swift:177`); WP-F's §12 regression names are written (S3F-14; S2-19's is WP-D's `ForegroundBackendTests.swift:239`, S3D-4, and T2-06's cross-launch case WP-B's, S3B-8); the model tests' liveness phase (S3-B-079) stays open (§14). Stale in this ADR: *Benchmarks*' "ten C2 benchmarks … (the probe chain at 1,000)" (`BenchSync.swift` has 37 `@Test` and the eight Stage 3 files 28 more, the probe chain at 10,000); the F-40 design's "Every probe exit without a verdict re-admits in that turn the jobs the breaker parked and owes a pump" (superseded by S3-B-063, above); the lane queues' "A re-join that changes lane, or comes from user work, still goes to the back through `relane` (L4-R24(4), Stage 3)" (superseded by S3-B-065's scoped form); and *Not verified*'s "condition observers can see a half-started run inside `startRun` (L4-R24(1))" (fixed by S3-B-064).

## ADR-054 — Stage 2 level 5 merge: WP-G facade decisions
- **Context.** WP-G (the facade) was implemented on `l4-merged` and independently verified: main run `wf_6593085d-170` (author, three verification rounds, two fix rounds; blocking 2 → 3 → 4, out of 16, 12 and 9 issues; 4.7 h; weekly usage 0 → 5 %), then three close-out cycles under the owner's strict bar (every cycle hunts for new bugs; any proven bug blocks): `wf_a049b89c-afb` cycle 1 (5 → 8 %), `wf_0ec4322f-bdb` cycle 2 (8 → 12 %) and `wf_b17021d0-91c` cycle 3 (13 → 15 %), with blocking issues 4 → 1 → **0**. Cycle 3's verification (both lenses, HEAD `1dca923`) found no blocking issue: the requirements lens put each fixed defect back as a mutant (MC00, MP5, the release-gate guard removed, the old `unavailableReason`-only path, the value-only R-STO-9 match) and the tests fail against every one; the correctness lens ran probes V3-1…V3-10 (an unread or lagging stream across three reopens; `sync` and `resolve` on a replaced root no commit had detected). Evidence (fixer and verifiers): `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS`; Kit 85, Sync 323 and Storage 445 tests; TSan clean; 1,195/1,195 on the iOS 27 simulator; the event model at 5,000 seeds; Kit coverage ≥ 97.8 %; WP-G's five benchmarks within budget. Merged into `feature/asset-kit` as `3de588e` (traceability refreshed in `6d1e0cc`). The orchestrator's checks on the merged head: `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS` (Model 151, Sync 323, Storage 445, Transport 84, Kit 85, Compatibility 3, AppleHosted 104 tests), `COVERAGE: PASS` (Kit 97.83 %, Sync 93.54 %, Storage 97.41 %), `--gates-only` `VERIFY: PASS` (TRACE passes now that P-1, P-8 and P-12 have benchmarks), release and iOS builds green, 1,195/1,195 on the iOS 27 simulator; this pass re-ran unsafe (0) and docs (1281/1281). The decision record is `docs/adr-drafts/g.md` (G-1…G-18, rows g-1…g-12, CCR-G1…CCR-G3, the authorised-edits table, the L5-R2 spawn-site table, the C2 figures, the L5-R14 table and the dispositions); that draft, with the orchestrator rulings L5-R1…L5-R23 (`.build-orchestrator/l5-rulings.md`, which is not in the repository; L5-R1…L5-R21 are quoted in the draft's "Orchestrator rulings (close-out)", and L5-R22 and L5-R23, the merge rulings, are recorded in this ADR), is normative detail for this ADR. Where the draft and a ruling disagree, the ruling wins (see "Rulings over the draft"). No new NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW item arises from Level 5 (L5-R23): the two `AssetStore` additions are package API, and no public signature, storage location, on-disk format, platform floor or dependency changed.
- **Merge rulings.** *L5-R22:* the remaining non-blocking items of cycle 3 need no new close-out cycle; each goes to the docs level or to the Stage 3 backlog: (1) `next()`'s exit for a consumer that stopped during its `events` registration runs but is not pinned (mutant M05 survives; Stage 3; the docs state the gap); (2) the model's tolerance table lacks two tolerances, confirmed as measurement limits of the model (below), its tightening Stage 3's; (3) `sync`'s L5-R20 trigger list is pinned only for `.storageUnavailable` (mutant M01s survives; Stage 3); (4) `invariantViolations()` reports a large give-up's leftover claims as "released and registered" (Stage 3); (5) the `current()` wording (below). *L5-R23:* this ADR records L5-R1…L5-R23 and the ratified G-1…G-18; the authorised edits outside WP-G go into ARCHITECTURE §1.1/§1.2; the ARCHITECTURE amendments (§2's sleeping sentence, L5-R7; §7, §8, §10, §11); "Not verified" by reference to `g.md`; the Stage 3 backlog; no new NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW item.
- **Owner information** (L5-R8; not NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW, no public signature changed). G-1 and G-5 add facade behaviour ARCHITECTURE §8 left open: how the reopen after a replaced root is triggered, which failures repeat a call (at most three reopens per call), and what survives a reopen (prefetch groups, the memory-pressure observer, the run-time retention scope); pins live in the vanished root's records and are lost with it. R-NET-3's run-time half is not detected: an app that attests the background-events hook (`BackgroundTransferPolicy.enabled`) without wiring it is not caught (L5-R5; ADR-026 amendment).
- **Ratified contract changes** (`git diff l4-merged 6d1e0cc -- Sources/`: no public signature changed; the docs gate stays at 1281/1281). (1) WP-B's contract file `Store/AssetStore.swift` gains two package members, each with a `///` comment naming the facade's B-15 reopen: `unavailableReason: StorageUnavailableReason?` (L5-R4 / CCR-G3: the recorded `state.core.unavailable`, no I/O: `.rootVanished` once a replaced root was detected, B-34's `.ioFailure` for a read-only launch) and `verifyRootNow() async -> StorageUnavailableReason?` (L5-R20, G-18: the commit's own `StoreLock.verifyRoot` on demand, one `itemInfo` of the lock file in one unit on the metadata executor; at once and with no I/O when a reason is recorded or the store is not open; a root that no longer verifies is recorded as `.rootVanished`, the state a commit's detection leaves, with one store error line carrying no identifier or path; it never writes and counts no commit; a stat that itself fails records nothing). Package, not public: not NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW (L5-R4, L5-R23). (2) Kit's contract file `Facade/AssetClient.swift`: no public or package signature changed; its private `store` and `engine` are replaced by the internal `lifecycle: ClientLifecycle`, `mirror` is internal (was private), and it gains the internal `currentEngine()` (a test seam), `static let reopenAttempts = 3` and `static func mayStemFromFileSystem(_:)`, and the private `perform`, `performOnCore` and `reportFigures(of:)`; the new internal types are `ClientCore`, `LifecycleSeamPoint`, `ClientLifecycle` (which conforms to the package protocol `PrefetchRegistry`), `EventForwarder` and `DiagnosticReportBuilder`. (3) WP-F: no contract file changed in code (`SyncEngine.swift`, `EngineSupport.swift`, `JobState.swift`, `SyncPlan.swift`, `RefreshDecision.swift`); internal and private additions in WP-F's files: `EngineState.claims`, `claimHeld` and `unlistedClaims`; `Group.members`, `claimed`, `claimCursor` and `unlistedClaims`; `Release.claims`; `Job.heldLane` (`Job.lane` falls back to it); `ClaimKey`; `recordGroup`, `spawnJoin`, `endClaims(of:)` (internal), `claimNewlyListed`, `dropClaim`; `EventSlot.clientEnd`; `isReleasedOnly`'s guard. (4) Doc comments made true of the merged code in this pass, comments only: the contract files' `SyncEngine` (the removal funnel and the pending claims; `registerGroup`; who uses `makeEventStream()` and `subscribe()`), `AssetEvent` (client lifecycle events never coalesce; streams across a reopen; `clientShutDown`), `StorageUnavailableReason.rootVanished` (the facade reopens and repeats), `StoreCoreState.unavailable` (its three writers), `PrefetchRegistry` (the facade's registry) and `PurgeableCacheRelay` (changes reach the engine through `ClientLifecycle.observeMemoryPressure`); outside them, `performShutdown`'s count of spawn sites reachable in `created` (three, not two) and `ClientLifecycle.current()` (L5-R22 (5)).

WP-F sources, internal state and bodies only: `Engine/EngineState.swift`, `EngineCommands.swift` and `EngineJobs.swift` (the pending claims, L5-R1; the bounded give-up, L5-R15), `EngineLifecycle.swift` (the shutdown from `created`, L5-R2; claims cleared at shutdown, L5-R1, L5-R15), `EngineDispatch.swift` (`isReleasedOnly`'s guard only, L5-R21) and `SubscriberQueue.swift` (`EventSlot.clientEnd`, L5-R19). New tests in WP-F's target: `Engine/EngineGroupClaimTests.swift` (14; L5-R1, L5-R15, L5-R21), `EngineCreatedShutdownTests.swift` (3; L5-R2) and `EngineClientSlotTests.swift` (1; L5-R19). WP-B: `Store/AssetStore.swift` (the two package members above) and the new `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Core/StoreUnavailableReasonTests.swift` (4; L5-R4, L5-R20). The orchestrator-owned `Tests/ZTronAssetKitTests/KitContractTests.swift`: `GatedRegistry.waitUntil` registers its waiters by id inside the continuation body, and a cancellation handler resumes them (G-8, `585807a`; assertions unchanged), authorised by ADR-050's Level 2 close and missing from the draft's table (see "Acknowledged at merge" (4)). None is a pre-existing CDN test, so `MIGRATION.md` does not apply (L5-R4, L5-R19).
- **Benchmarks** (release, this Mac; WP-G's five only, L5-R18: `--filter "ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/KitBenchmarks/"`, 5 tests in 2 suites, on cycle 3's final tree at a 1-minute load of 6.68 → 7.83; not re-run at the merge). P-1 lookup p99 0.417 µs at 10,000 assets (budget 25 µs; scale ratio 1.25, max 3.0); P-8 UI calls p99 at most 0.064 ms (1.0 ms), no main-thread file-system call, the bundle step off the main thread; P-9 heartbeat gap p99 15.136 ms (50 ms); P-12 init p99 0.0038 ms over fakes and 0.0033 ms over `.live()` (1.0 ms), no file-system call. C2 of the engine's pending claims at 10,000 members (temporary release-mode probes on macOS 27, load about 4.9–5.5, never committed): the registering turn 4.27–4.40 ms (the existing de-duplication and sort 3.44–3.51 ms of it, the claims about 0.85 ms), a release turn 0.14–0.16 ms, the first turn of 512 claimed-only retirements 4.34 ms (the existing chunked release 4.30–4.60 ms), `claimNewlyListed` 1.01–1.05 ms, a large give-up's turn 3.53–3.70 ms (about 85 ms in one turn before L5-R15), a degraded engine's give-up 6.0–7.2 µs. The whole 35-test suite (P-5, P-11, P-13, R-SYN-5's C2) is the orchestrator's near-idle run after the merge (L5-R6, L5-R18); its results are under Open. P-13 (WP-B) awaits the owner (ADR-050).
- **Ratified decisions** (rulings L5-R1…L5-R23; normative detail in `g.md`).
  - *Approved as decided (L5-R8; owner information above).* **G-1**: an internal actor, `ClientLifecycle`, owns the current store and engine pair and the single-flight reopen after a replaced root (B-15); prefetch groups, the memory-pressure observer and the run-time retention scope survive it, pins are lost with the vanished root, and `lookup` returns `nil` between the mirror reset and the replacement's first install. **G-5**, with its L5-R4 and L5-R20 amendments: a call is repeated on the replacement after `.storageUnavailable(.rootVanished)`, or after a failure that can stem from the file system (`.storageUnavailable(_)`, `.serverUnavailable(_)`, `.insufficientSpace`, `.integrityFailure`) when `verifyRootNow()` answers `.rootVanished`, and after a `.shutDown` from an engine a reopen retired; `withAsset` reopens first when `unavailableReason` reports `.rootVanished`; at most `reopenAttempts` (3) reopens per call; every other result is returned as given (L4-R23) (ARCHITECTURE §8).
  - *Class rulings.* **G-13** (L5-R1, CCR-G2 approved and widened): pending claims make true that, once `registerGroup` returned for a group naming x and until that group is released, no other group's release, waiter withdrawal or caller cancellation retires x's job or cancels its transfer, in both windows of the ruling; L5-R15 bounds a join's give-up by the release follow-up; L5-R16 accepts a claimed-only job's failure exit (below); L5-R21 makes the release gate never read a registered token as released; L5-R17 accepts G-15's deviations (the pure split `recordGroup`/`spawnJoin` instead of a `planningView()` gate, the 50-run series filtered by function name, the claim check in `removeInterest` rather than `retire(_:)`). **G-14** (L5-R2): `shutdown()` from every state, `created` included, cancels and joins every owned task and resumes every continuation before it returns; the three spawn sites reachable in `created` each have a test. **G-16** (L5-R13): followership precedes registration; the five-path enumeration; the seeded model (500 seeds in the suite, 5,000 by hand). **G-17** (L5-R19): `clientShutDown` has a queue slot of its own, so client lifecycle events never coalesce with each other; the model's oracle tightened and every tolerance audited (E1…E10). **G-18** (L5-R20): `verifyRootNow()` and the facade's root check after a failure that can stem from the file system. L5-R14: every scheduling-dependent facade test converted to a condition wait or a defined baseline (the draft's table; ARCHITECTURE §14).
  - *The model's measurement limits (L5-R22 (2)).* Besides the tolerances of G-17's audit, two rows are **measurement limits of the model, not product promises**: (a) I1 does not owe the registration generation's own `clientStarted`, nor the terminal events of calls (other than the markers) that began after the registration; (b) I2 accepts one extra `clientStarted` in the first gap, the registration engine's own. L5-R13/L5-R19's promise stands: a stream is owed every event of its registration's generation from its registration on. Mutant MC00 is still caught, and no loss is proven. Tightening the model is Stage 3's (Assignments).
  - *`current()` (L5-R22 (5)), as the code does it.* `ClientLifecycle.current()` waits for a reopen that is running when it is called until that reopen's task ends, which is after the reopen's tail (the newest memory-pressure request, the group re-registration and the replacement's `start()`); the reopen clears its single-flight entry in the turn that installs its replacement, so a call made during the tail returns the installed replacement at once, and a caller's `start()` joins the replacement's single-flight start. The ruling's first clause, that `current()` waits for a running reopen only until it installs its replacement, holds for a call made after the installation (it does not wait at all); a call already waiting at the installation resumes only when the reopen's task ends. So a `subscribe()` made while a reopen is parked before its installation registers with the started replacement, as G-16 and the comment at `FacadeReopenTests.swift:533-535` say; that comment is accurate as written. ARCHITECTURE §8, G-2, G-16 and `current()`'s doc comment now state these semantics.
  - *L5-R16 (accepted for Level 5).* A job only a claim keeps ends on its first failed attempt as `failed(.nextRun)` with `assetFailed`, and the successor's join starts a fresh transfer: L5-R1's unchanged failure exit, not a violation; G-13's "behaves as if the released interest were still there" holds except on failure. A claim that inherits the held lane's retry policy is in the Stage 3 backlog; `EngineFailures.swift` is unchanged.
  - *Contract change requests.* CCR-G1 declined (L5-R5): under ADR-026 (c) `BackgroundTransferPolicy.enabled` is the app's attestation that it forwards the hook, so the attestation is what "wired" means in Stage 2 (G-6's test covers it; ADR-026 amendment); no WP-D change. CCR-G2 approved and widened to the whole class (L5-R1, G-13). CCR-G3 approved (L5-R4), with L5-R20's on-demand root check after it.
  - *L5-R3 (G-2).* The replacement's `clientStarted(generation:)` is forwarded, so consumers learn that a new generation started, matching the mirror's reset; a retired engine's `clientShutDown` stays hidden. *L5-R9.* Interleavings no existing seam point reaches cheaply stay untested; a seam point is added only with the fix for a bug proven there (G-10's fifth point came with L5-R2's fix, its sixth with L5-R13's model); `bind`'s unreachable early return stays as an invariant guard (ARCHITECTURE §14). *L5-R10.* WP-G merged only after L5-R1 landed and both R-CAR-3 facade tests ran free of `withKnownIssue`; CCR-G2's fallback ("record R-CAR-3 and S1-05 as failing") is void. *L5-R11.* The files outside WP-G are the authorised ones above (`KitContractTests.swift` under ADR-050's earlier authorisation), and no contract signature changed apart from the two package members. *L5-R6, L5-R18.* Benchmarks as above.
  - *Recorded by reference* (normative detail in `g.md`): G-2 (the pull-through forwarder: at most one event waits in it, the pump runs off the actor, a `Consumer` token stops it on drop as well as on cancellation, two iterators of one stream are served in turn), G-3 (`setRetentionScope` and `subscribe()` await `start()`; the scope is recorded before it is applied), G-4 (`diagnosticReport()`: one engine's figures per report, the configuration summarised field by field, named counts, `moduleVersion` "1.0.0"), G-6 (R-NET-3's attestation half), G-7 (failing tests first where Stage 1 had left stubs), G-8 (above), G-9 (`KitHarness`), G-10 (the six `LifecycleSeamPoint`s, `nil` in production), G-11 (closed by L5-R1, L5-R4 and L5-R5), G-12 (`observeMemoryPressure` never waits for a reopen), G-15 (above).
- **Acknowledged at merge** (no ruling covered them explicitly; the code was checked against each statement; the orchestrator confirmed all five at merge, ruling L5-R24). (1) *Failing-first commits.* L5-R13 asked the model to fail on `2e828d1` and L5-R19 on `9619770`. The model cannot compile against `2e828d1` alone, so it failed on `666ec0d` (`2e828d1` plus the model and its behaviour-neutral seams; the one-step root replacement `0ae3004` failed at the same seeds), and the tightened model on `a8e3b1d` (`9619770`'s sources plus tests only). (2) *L5-R20's trigger list.* "Transfer or staging failures" is read as the failures that can involve the volume: `.serverUnavailable(_)` (a staging write that failed is classified transient), `.insufficientSpace` and `.integrityFailure` trigger the root check; `.offline`, `.authRejected` and every `.notFound` (the catalog's or the origin's) do not, besides the ruled `.cancelled` and `.shutDown`. (3) *`verifyRootNow()` is `async` and logs one store error line*, against the ruling's synchronous example: the stat is blocking work that must run on the metadata executor, not on the actor's cooperative thread (ADR-003, C2). (4) *`KitContractTests.swift`*: an orchestrator-owned file changed under ADR-050's earlier authorisation, outside L5-R11's list and the draft's table. (5) *`current()`*: L5-R22 (5)'s first clause holds only for a call made after the installation (above).
- **Rulings over the draft** (stale text; ADR-054 and ARCHITECTURE hold what is true). G-2's close-out paragraph: "a forwarder registered after the hand-over has no history to lose" is superseded by its cycle-2 paragraph and L5-R13, and "one slot per progress key and per subject" by L5-R19 (client lifecycle events have a slot each). CCR-G2's "Status for the merge … list both as failing" is void (L5-R10). CCR-G3's "Pinned for now by R-STO-9 … asserts the `.ioFailure`" is replaced by L5-R4's behaviour. G-11's open findings are closed (its own status line). The CCR header's "otherwise the contract files' bodies and private members only": internal members were added (above). The merge paragraph places g-12 in ARCHITECTURE §10.1 and writes the "Not verified" items into `VERIFICATION.md`; under L5-R23 g-12 is in §10.6 with WP-G's rows, and the items are recorded here by reference (`VERIFICATION.md` is compiled at Stage 5). The untested-lines paragraph counts `EventForwarder.swift:159-165` as covered since cycle 3: it runs, but no assertion depends on it (L5-R22 (1)). G-17's audit lists "every tolerance": two more are measurement limits (L5-R22 (2)). The L5-R11 bullet's "Files outside WP-G are exactly the ones below" omits `KitContractTests.swift`. The L5-R15 bullet's "`ClientLifecycle.swift`'s former `:337`, now `:416`": in the merged tree the guard is `:416` and its return `:417`. "Not verified here" lists `subscribe()`'s registration among the untested two-hop interleavings, although since cycle 2 that path goes through `register(_:)` and is tested (the model; `R-SYN-12: an events stream cancelled while its first registration waits for a reopen leaves no follower and no subscriber`; path 5 pinned by `R-SYN-12: an events stream whose first pull registers while a reopen is parked after the old engine's shutdown receives the replacement's clientStarted before its later events`). G-6's "ADR-026 option E" is ADR-026 (c) (transport.md §10.2's option E). The `current()` wording of G-2, G-16 and the draft's L5-R3 bullet was corrected in the draft in this pass (wording only, L5-R22 (5)). The draft's "the orchestrator numbers them at merge": decision and row identifiers are final.
- **Assignments to later work packages** (ARCHITECTURE §11, §14 set accordingly; §11 credits assigned names and ruled behaviour only. The Stage 1 behaviour tests WP-G added, three further R-CAR-1 names, eight more R-CAR-2 `FallbackChainTests` names, R-APL-5, R-SYN-9 and R-CLN-8, count in the trace gate but are not credited by name, as ADR-053 did not list every merged test).
  - *Stage 3 backlog (L5-R16, L5-R22).* (1) L5-R16: a pending claim inherits the held lane's retry policy, with its test. (2) L5-R22 (1): pin `next()`'s exit for a consumer that stopped during its `events` registration (a test read for a started pump, or a census of the replacement's registrations), so that mutant M05 fails. (3) L5-R22 (2): tighten the model: stamp `performMaintenance` and `sync(.force)` calls, and owe the registration engine's `clientStarted` when the follow turn preceded its start. (4) L5-R22 (3): a forced-sync test that ends `.insufficientSpace` or `.serverUnavailable` on a healthy root: one root check, the result returned as given, no reopen (mutant M01s must fail it). (5) L5-R22 (4): `invariantViolations()` flags a registered token in `releasing` only while its `Release` holds members or pending interests, with the E2-shape test. (6) CCR-G1's run-time detection of an attested but unwired hook, only with a detection that cannot misfire on an early finish, for example a finish that no hook call claims for the whole background wake (L5-R5; WP-D's files). (7) ADR-050's deferred, optional P-9 case of the sliced release survey at 100,000 entries (E-16): not written at Level 5; still optional.
  - *WP-F / Stage 3 notes* (the draft raises them; no ruling assigns them). `releasesPerTurn`'s doc estimates about 1 µs per removal, while WP-G's probe measured about 8.5 µs per retirement of a queued job (a 512-member turn 4.30–4.60 ms at a load of about 4.9) and the WP-F review about 15 ms for 10,000 removals in one turn; that one-turn figure predates the chunking, and every turn of the chunked release measured since stays within C2, so the doc is left until a re-measurement. The "no give-up after a running join's read" invariant (G-13) must be kept, or the leftovers-merge branch tested. A claim names the content its member had when claimed: a member whose entry changes is protected again only once its join reaches it.
  - *WP-K (Level 5, after G).* The facade the shims wrap: after a replaced root, calls are reopened and repeated (at most three reopens per call); `lookup` returns `nil` between the mirror reset and the replacement's first install; event streams continue across reopens with each new generation's `clientStarted` (values may repeat, for example `clientStarted(generation: 1)`, and snapshot generations restart, B-17) and never a retired engine's `clientShutDown`, while a real shutdown's `clientShutDown` is last; pins are lost with a vanished root; the facade's `ensureAvailable` stays faithful (L4-R23; revisit it only if the shims need a retry-after, ADR-053). The shims' tests use a real `AssetClient` over the fakes, as the facade's own tests do (orchestrator, at this merge); L5-R14's test rules apply (condition waits only, baselines for shared fake logs, one-rename root replacement); `KitHarness` lives in `ZTronAssetKitTests` and is not importable from `ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests`, which depends on Kit and TestSupport only; swift-testing's `--filter` matches function names, not display names (G-15).
  - *Orchestrator (before tagging `l5g-merged`).* Whole-package TSan and `verify.sh --perf` on a near-idle host, where P-5, P-11, P-13 and R-SYN-5's C2 are judged (L5-R6, L5-R18), with the figures recorded. Optional comment-only edits of test files, outside the docs writer's files: `CompositionTests.swift:39-44` cites "ADR-026 option E" and says the run-time half "needs a transport change (CCR-G1)", which L5-R5 declined; `FacadeReopenTests.swift:96-99` names `AssetStore.unavailableReason` where the facade now asks `verifyRootNow()` (which answers from the recorded reason); `FacadeReopenTests.swift:709-711` says `sync` hands only `.storageUnavailable` and `.shutDown` outcomes to the reopen path, while since L5-R20 it hands every failure `mayStemFromFileSystem` accepts. `FacadeReopenTests.swift:533-535` is accurate as written and needs no edit (above).
  - *Owner.* P-13 (WP-B) still awaits the ruling on ADR-050's proposal; it missed under load in every WP-G run and is not WP-G's.
- **REENTRANCY rows.** The proposed rows g-1…g-12 keep their identifiers as final numbers in the new ARCHITECTURE §10.6 `ZTronAssetKit` (L5-R12, L5-R23; the code cites "g-n (proposed)"; the word is historical, as for h-n, b-n, c-n and f-n). g-12's await is in WP-B's `AssetStore.verifyRootNow()` and is kept in §10.6 with WP-G's rows (L5-R23; §10.1 points to it), not in §10.1 as the draft's merge paragraph proposed. A scripted count over `Sources/` at the merge: every `// REENTRANCY:` in `ZTronAssetKit` cites a g-n row (g-1 ×3, g-2 ×3, g-3 ×3, g-4 ×5, g-5 ×2, g-6 ×2, g-7 ×9, g-8 ×2, g-9 ×5, g-10 ×2, g-11 ×3), g-12 is cited once in `AssetStore.swift`, every g-n of the draft is cited, and no other. No engine row is added (L5-R12 asked for rows for the engine awaits L5-R1 and L5-R2 add; they add none): the claims are synchronous bookkeeping, the release follow-up, a join's large give-up and the created shutdown reuse RE-S21's spawn and join loop, and `spawnJoin` keeps RE-S30 (the only changed engine `await` line is `joinGroup`'s argument). The awaits of the nonisolated statics `EventForwarder.subscribed`, `pump` and `forward` run off the actor and need no row (g-7 covers the pump's entry); the test-file actor `GatedRegistry` (`KitContractTests.swift`) is outside C3's scope (ARCHITECTURE §10).
- **Not verified** (carried to `docs/VERIFICATION.md` at Stage 5, L5-R23): `g.md` "Not verified here" by reference (ARCHITECTURE §14).
