Notably: R-NET-3's run-time half (L5-R5): a background-events hook the app attested but did not wire is neither detected nor tested, and goes into the device test plan; a real `rootVanished` on a device (iOS purging a Caches root while the app runs) and the reopen there; UIKit's background-events hook end to end (the facade test calls `handleBackgroundEvents` directly); the live `MainBundleLookup` against an app bundle with resources; P-8 and P-9 on a device (macOS host only); the benchmarks on the iOS Simulator (built, not run, ADR-051); the interleavings the draft lists as untested (L5-R9; ARCHITECTURE §14), among them a reopen landing between two hops of one facade call (the background-events hand-off, the diagnostic report's three reads) and a stream's registration overlapping the client's shutdown (shown by reading only). Also from the draft: a root that vanishes between `withAsset`'s read of `unavailableReason` and its lease leases from the vanished store (a read, accepted under L5-R4), and a call that succeeds on a replaced root no commit has detected yet does not reopen until a qualifying failure or a commit detects it; a root check whose stat itself fails records nothing (shown by reading only), and the check's cost on a device; a claimed-only job's failure path (shown by reading only); the claims' and L5-R15's C2 figures come from release-mode probes on macOS 27 at a load of about 4.9–5.5, not a device; the "no give-up after a running join's read" invariant is argued, not tested, and the merge of a large give-up into a later large release has no test; R-CAR-5's lost update was never reproduced (no seam reaches it; the record-first order removes the window by construction, G-3).
- **Open.** Nothing in the documents. The orchestrator confirmed "Acknowledged at merge" (1)–(5) (L5-R24). For (5), the wording here matches the code and `current()` needs no change. The planned edit of the `FacadeReopenTests.swift:533-535` comment was dropped, because that comment is accurate. Before the tag, the orchestrator ran the near-idle checks at `55a4799` (logs `.build-orch/l5/`). Whole-package TSan: every target passed (151, 323, 445, 84, 85, 3 and 104 tests) with 0 ThreadSanitizer reports, at a 1-minute load of 2.4 → 5.4. `verify.sh --perf` at a load of 5.4 → 3.1: 34 of 35 benchmarks passed. Among them: P-5 durable p95 30.9 ms (budget 40 ms, ordered p95 14.9 ms), P-11 speedup 3.43 (min 3.0), every R-SYN-3/R-SYN-4/R-SYN-5 C2 benchmark's longest turn ≤ 6.0 ms (10 ms), and WP-G's five within budget (P-1 p99 0.417 µs, P-9 heartbeat p99 15.1 ms, P-12 0.009 ms over `.live()`). The one miss is WP-B's P-13: the longest deletion unit took 8.89 ms wall and 1.04 ms on CPU, against the 5.0 ms per-batch budget. It awaits the owner (ADR-050), so `--perf` ends `VERIFY: FAIL (1 failed: benchmarks)`.
- **Process.** Class rulings converged where case fixes had not: event continuity took L5-R3 → L5-R13 → L5-R19, and the scheduling-dependent tests L5-R14, as at Level 4 (L4-R17, L4-R20): demand the enumeration and a model as soon as a class repeats. A ruling's test filter must name test functions: swift-testing's `--filter` matches function names, so L5-R1's display-name filter matched nothing (G-15). Verifiers and fixers run only the work package's own benchmarks, with a filter that matches only their suite, and check the run's test count; the whole suite is the orchestrator's near-idle run (L5-R18). Two verifiers sharing a worktree take their `--quick` verdicts from their own exit status (rulings, "Process"). A strict close-out cycle cost about 3 points of weekly usage and 2–3 hours (5 % → 15 % over three cycles, after the main run's 0 → 5 % in 4.7 h).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 L5 WP-K merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-055).** The WP-K notes are done: the shims' tests use a real `AssetClient` over the fakes; L5-R14's rules apply through the L5K-R2 table (patterns (a)–(g) plus the auto-advancing clock; (d) and (g) occur nowhere); `KitHarness` is not imported (K-1, K-10); the facade's `ensureAvailable` was not revisited (ADR-053's amendment). Not addressed: the facade's reopen after a replaced root as the shims see it (calls reopened and repeated, `lookup` answering `nil` between the mirror reset and the replacement's first install, event streams across reopens): no shim test or MIGRATION.md passage covers it, so ADR-055 records it as Not verified.
- **Amendment (Stage 2 WP-I documentation merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-056).** The Context's `--quick` counts name Model and Transport the wrong way round: at `6d1e0cc` Transport has the 151 tests (16 suites) and Model the 84 (18 suites), as the two targets' `@Test` and `@Suite` counts show; the other five names are right.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).**
  - L5-R22 (1) and backlog (2) are closed by the census test (mutant M05 fails it), (2) and backlog (3) by the model's tightening, and (3) and backlog (4) by the forced-sync pair (M01s fails both cases); S3-B-096, the reopen limit no test ran, is closed by S3G-3 (S3G-2, S3G-3). The model's rows (a) and (b) are replaced by two measurement limits, not product promises: (a′) a `subscribe()` stream whose registration engine was unstarted when the call began, or that was started while a reopen was parked before its install, is not owed that engine's `clientStarted` and may receive it once; (a″) calls begun while a reopen was parked before its install, and `sync(.force)` calls that returned `.failed` or `.notDue`, are not stamped. The registration generation's `clientStarted` is owed when the registration preceded its start, and so are its stamped calls' terminal events (35 such registrations over the 500 suite seeds; MT1 and MT2 fail all 10 cases on the tightened model and pass on the old one; MC00 is still caught 10 of 10; 5,000 seeds in 20.2 s at a load of about 30). Two gaps in that wording, found by reading: an `events` stream's start order also stays unknown when its engine started between the action and the registration turn, when the lifecycle wait ended without a registration, or when the walk had no oracle tap; and a `performMaintenance` call that threw, or any call made without the walk's oracle tap, is not stamped. "Rulings over the draft"'s sentence that `EventForwarder.swift:159-165` runs but is pinned by no assertion is superseded. Backlog (7), P-9 at 100,000 entries (E-16), stays optional and unwritten (S3-B-093).
  - WP-F's Stage 3 close-out (S3F-12): backlog (1), L5-R16, is done by S3-B-081 (`002844b`): a claim-only job's failed attempts count against its held lane's retry policy, so the qualification in *L5-R16 (accepted for Level 5)*, that G-13's "behaves as if the released interest were still there" holds except on failure, is lifted for retryable failures: a claim-only job now ends at the held lane's cap, not on its first retryable failure. Backlog (5), L5-R22 (4), is done by S3-B-082 (`3cf147d`): `invariantViolations()` flags a registered group in `releasing` only while its Release holds members, with the E2-shape test. Backlog (2) to (4) are WP-G's (above); (6) and (7) are not WP-F's.

## ADR-055 — Stage 2 level 5 merge: WP-K compatibility shims · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (K-2, K-3, K-5, K-6, K-15)
- **Context.** WP-K (R-CAR-6, the compatibility shims; a test-only work package) was implemented on `l5g-merged` and independently verified: main run `wf_5feaaf72-e33` (author, three verification rounds, two fix rounds; blocking 3 → 2 → 2, out of 18, 10 and 11 issues; weekly usage 18 % → about 25 %), whose two round-3 blockers were L5K-V3-05 (CCR-K1, an explicit sync during legacy migration downloading prior-art files R-STO-11 wants moved, needed a ruling) and L5K-V3-04 (the CCR-K1 tripwire test raced the end of migration under TSan); then five close-out cycles under the owner's strict bar: `wf_d5c59efd-6db` cycle 1 (25 → 28 %), `wf_d80dd07d-5cd` cycle 2 (28 → 30 %), `wf_cfb1c56d-e3f` cycle 3 (30 → 34 %), `wf_da30f4d9-921` cycle 4 (34 → 44 %) and `wf_02981dcc-c86` cycle 5 (44 → 57 %, including the owner's own use), with blocking issues 2 → 1 → 3 → 6 (4 distinct) → **0** (the rulings' count). The owner gave no stop at the start (the orchestrator's default, 45 %), then moved it to 65 % ("move usage target to 65%", 2026-10-24 01:25). Cycle 5's verification (both lenses, HEAD `2df51ab`) found no blocking issue: the requirements lens put each fixed defect back as a mutant (STALE, LASTBULK, PERREAD, LATEJOIN, LATEJOIN2, GUARD, NOANSWER, CHECKFIRST) and the new tests fail on the defect assertions; the correctness lens ran probes KC5-S1 and E1–E6 and the L5K-R21 pins, which pass on HEAD and fail on the cycle-4 sources. Evidence (fixer and verifiers): `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS`; Storage 459, Sync 351, Kit 85 and Compatibility 71 tests three times each; TSan clean (Compatibility, Sync, Storage); release and iOS builds green; unsafe 0; docs 1281/1281; 1,305/1,305 on the iOS 27 simulator; the shims 98.18 % in the compatibility bundle. Merged into `feature/asset-kit` as `23807ac` (traceability refreshed in `7d90719`). The orchestrator's checks on the merged head (`.build-orch/l5k/summary.txt`): `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS` (Model 151, Sync 351, Storage 459, Transport 84, Kit 85, Compatibility 71, AppleHosted 104 tests), `COVERAGE: PASS` (Kit 97.83 %, Sync 93.42 %, Storage 97.36 %, overall 96.51 %, device-only share 0.68 %), `--gates-only` `VERIFY: PASS` (1,181 tests with IDs, unsafe 0, docs 1281/1281), release and iOS builds green, 1,305/1,305 on the iOS 27 simulator; TSan and `--perf` were deferred (Open). The decision record is `docs/adr-drafts/k.md` (K-1…K-15, rows k-1…k-7, CCR-K1, the authorised edits, the enumerations of L5K-R1, L5K-R13, L5K-R16, L5K-R17 and L5K-R21, the C2 figures and the L5K-R2 table); that draft, with the orchestrator rulings L5K-R1…L5K-R28 (`.build-orchestrator/l5k-rulings.md`, which is not in the repository; L5K-R1…L5K-R26 are quoted in the draft's "Orchestrator rulings (close-out)", and L5K-R27 and L5K-R28, the merge rulings, are recorded here), is normative detail for this ADR. Where the draft and a ruling disagree, the ruling wins (see "Rulings over the draft").
- **Merge rulings.** *L5K-R27:* the remaining non-blocking items need no new cycle; each goes to the Stage 3 backlog or to this ADR: (1) the liveness robustness of `LegacyOnDemandRaceTests`' tracked-call reads; (2) a pre-existing TSan flake in a WP-C test, to make deterministic before Stage 5's `--full`; (3) L5K-R24's sites, ADR-016's lag (MIGRATION.md §4 records it; Stage 3 holds the optional `installIfDue()`); (4) the removal-only dooms install, the accepted exception to L5K-R20; (5) the L5K-R25 next-id fallback, defensive and unpinned (all below). *L5K-R28:* this ADR records L5K-R1…L5K-R28 and the ratified K-1…K-15; K-2, K-3, K-5, K-6 and K-15 are NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW, in this heading and on the owner review list; the authorised edits outside WP-K go into ARCHITECTURE §1.1/§1.2; the ARCHITECTURE amendments (§4, §5, §7, §10.7, §11, §14); "Not verified" by reference to `k.md`.
- **Owner decisions** (NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW; L5K-R8 approved each as the safest default; shim behaviour and API in a test target the owner copies into ZTronCarouselCore, ADR-033). **K-2:** `CDNAssetResolver.installed` is a `@MainActor` computed property over a write-once install point (`CDNInstallPoint.process`): the first installation wins, a later one returns the installed client, `installed = nil` changes nothing, and nothing re-installs or uninstalls (the prior art's `nonisolated(unsafe) static var` is global mutable state, which SPEC §4.2 forbids). **K-3:** the lookups (`CDNLocalStore.client`, `existingLocalURL`, `hasAsset`, `missingOrStaleAssets`, `isFullyAvailable`, `localURL(for:)`, `cacheManifest`, `cachedAsset(forIdentifier:)`, `reconcile(newManifest:)`) are `@MainActor`, with no I/O (the lookups read the client's snapshot); the carousel's call sites compile unchanged (`PriorArtCallSiteTests`), and a nonisolated caller needs `await MainActor.run`. **K-5:** `StaticManifestCDNProvider` and `BackgroundAssetsCDNProvider` describe a client tier and serve a manifest; `fetchManifest()` answers the validated manifest for `StaticManifestCDNProvider` (lowercase checksums, quarantined entries left out) and the app-supplied catalog for `BackgroundAssetsCDNProvider`; `downloadAsset` is deprecated and throws `CDNShimError.downloadsGoThroughTheClient` (the Apple provider still reports a malformed `remotePath` first). The provider's own fetch sends no app key: option (a), documentation and a manifest source that sends the key (or `client.availability(scope:)`), so `verifyFullyAvailable()` through the keyless provider fails with the origin's 403; option (b), a key-taking initialiser that would copy the secret outside `AppKey`'s redaction, is declined and stays available as an additive change. **K-6:** the deprecated initialisers share one client per install point, built on first use from the provider; `CDNLocalStore()` binds the process point; `CDNLocalStore(baseDirectory: X)` roots the store at `.custom(X's parent)`, `<parent>/ZTronAssetKit/v1` beside the legacy root `X` (**storage location**), so one client per resolved base directory (two directories with one parent share one root; a parent in Caches shares the root of a client at `StorageLocation.caches`) and one legacy root per process; the shared per-directory registry is declined as global mutable state; a prior-art provider at the configured origin keeps the configuration's key and HTTP settings, never across origins. **K-15:** on a launch on which legacy migration is still adopting the prior art's files (the first after adoption, and any later launch that resumes an interrupted migration; L5K-R4) the lookups miss a prior-art file until the snapshot holding its adoption is installed; a resolve adopts it on demand without a transfer; the shims cannot close the window without main-thread I/O (C2); the window's syncs download nothing migration can still adopt (L5K-R1).
- **Owner options and information** (not NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW, so not on the owner review list, which holds the tags only). K-13 (L5K-R9): a public read of the client's applied manifest (for example `AssetClient.manifestEntries(scope:)`, no network) would let `cachedAsset(forIdentifier:)` and `verifyFullyAvailable()` work without a provider and its keyless fetch; a way to await legacy migration (a start option or an awaitable migration report) would let MIGRATION.md step 3 hold carousel content until K-15's window has closed; neither is added in Level 5 (a public API addition no requirement needs). K-5's option (b), declined by L5K-R8 (above). L5K-R24's lag: the commit path, `ensureAvailable`'s present members, the resolve's fast hit and joins answered from the ledger may answer a user-initiated asker up to about two install intervals (0.2 s by default) before the readers' snapshot holds the asset; the location answered is valid at once (MIGRATION.md §4; the observed bound, the cycle-4 probes P21-COMMIT, P21-ENSURE-MIG and K4-COMMITPROMO on macOS 27, is in `k.md` "L5K-R24").
- **Ratified contract changes** (`git diff l5g-merged 7d90719 -- Sources/`: no public signature changed; the docs gate stays at 1281/1281). (1) WP-C's contract file `Migration/StoreMigration.swift`: the package signature `AssetStore.adoptLegacyNow(_ id: AssetID, urgency: AssetPriority = .userInitiated) async throws(StoreError) -> AssetLocation?` (was `adoptLegacyNow(_:)`; L5K-R14, L5K-C2-06; package and defaulted, so the resolve path and leases are unchanged; not NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW). (2) WP-C's contract sub-struct `MigrationState` gains two internal fields, `adopting` and `onDemand` (`[[String]: [CheckedContinuation<Void, Never>]]`: one adoption per legacy file, L5K-R13). (3) WP-F: no contract file changed; internal test seams in WP-F's other files: `holdNextStoreDeadlineRead()`, `releaseStoreDeadlineRead()` and `storeDeadlineReadHeld` (L5K-R16, ratified by L5K-R22), `MigrationProgress.batchCalls` (L5K-R17), `invariantViolations(storeDeadlineDue:)` (defaulted, so its other callers are unchanged) and `storeDeadlineSettled`; internal state `storeDeadlineUpdates` and `storeDeadlineReadHold`; the internal `storedLocation(of:lane:)` (its cycle-3 `in transfer:` parameter removed in cycle 4) and the private `releaseLease(_:)` and `unaskedIDs(of:epoch:attempt:asked:)`. (4) Doc comments made true of the merged code in this pass, comments only: `migrateLegacy(maxFiles:)` (a batch whose bulk publication left the trailing install due returns right after it while files remain once that publication returned; otherwise, and for the last batch, it installs at once; L5K-R20, L5K-R23; with the queue empty and every claimed file held by an adopt-on-demand the call waits for those adoptions instead of returning at once, finishes the migration once no file is left, and returns, with `remaining > 0`, for the next call when an adoption it waited for put its file back in the queue; L5K-R17) and `adoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)` (a `.userInitiated` call installs before it answers a present asset; a call that waited for another processing publishes at its own urgency; L5K-R14, L5K-R18) in `StoreMigration.swift`, `MigrationState.onDemand` in `MigrationState.swift` (the mark lasts until the processing's published snapshot, and a migration batch waits on it too since L5K-R17), and `SyncEngine`'s type doc (an `await` cites its ledger row, an RE-S, f-n or k-n row, not only RE-Snn). (5) The shim public API WP-K added (ADR-033's tag; ADR-033 amendment): the types `CDNInstallPoint` (K-2) and `CDNShimError` (K-5, K-9), the explicit-client initialisers `CDNLocalStore.init(client:)`, `init(installPoint:)`, `CDNAssetResolver.init(client:)` and `CDNSyncCoordinator.init(client:cdn:)`, `CDNSyncCoordinator.lastReconciliation` (K-7), `NetworkAvailabilityMonitor.init(conditions:)` (its only public initialiser, K-8), and `Equatable` on `StaticManifestCDNProviderError` and `Hashable` on `BackgroundAssetsCDNProviderError` (K-5); also the read-only `client` of `CDNLocalStore`, `CDNAssetResolver` and `CDNSyncCoordinator`, `CDNLocalStore.installPoint`, `CDNInstallPoint.resolver` and `installResolver(_:)`, and `CDNReconciliationSummary`'s `Hashable` conformance with its public `init(migratedCount:…)` and `init(report:)` (the prior art's is `Sendable` only, with no public initialiser). The complete surface is the shims' public declarations, decided in `k.md` K-1…K-15.
- **Authorised edits outside WP-K** (ratified; ownership unchanged; `git diff --name-status l5g-merged 7d90719`; the draft's "Authorised edits outside WP-K (close-out)" lists them cycle by cycle; ARCHITECTURE §1.1, §1.2). WP-F sources, internal state and bodies only: `Engine/EnginePresence.swift` (the pre-transfer read: L5K-R1, L5K-R14, L5K-R16, L5K-R18, L5K-R21, L5K-R23, L5K-R25), `EngineRuns.swift` (the parked launch run, the migration loop's re-arm, `batchCalls`: L5K-R1, L5K-R16, L5K-R17), `EngineState.swift` (L5K-R1, L5K-R16, L5K-R17), `EngineLifecycle.swift` (`reconcileLaunch` returning `Bool`, the re-arm after launch reconciliation's publication, shutdown releasing the seam), `EngineTimers.swift` (`applyStoreDeadline`, `fireDue`, the seam) and `EngineCommands.swift` (the lease release restructured into `releaseLease(_:)`, two re-arms, the tripwire), all under L5K-R16 with L5K-R22's ratification (L5K-C3-10), `EngineDispatch.swift` (the `publish` re-arms, L5K-R16, and `drive`'s guard, the one edit L5K-R25 authorises there), and `EngineFailures.swift` and `EngineManifest.swift` (re-arm additions L5K-R16's enumeration found). WP-F's tests: the new `Engine/EngineLegacyMigrationTransferTests.swift` (28 tests: 15 R-STO-11, 12 R-STO-13, 1 R-SYN-14) and `EngineModelTests.swift` (L5K-R16's tripwire after every operation and at idle, through `idleViolations()`, a superset of the earlier check; no assertion weakened). WP-C: `Migration/LegacyPhases.swift` (L5K-R13, L5K-R14, L5K-R17, L5K-R18, L5K-R20, L5K-R23), `MigrationState.swift` (L5K-R13), `StoreMigration.swift` (L5K-R14's parameter and its doc comment) and the new `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Cleanup/LegacyOnDemandRaceTests.swift` (14 tests: 10 R-STO-11, 4 R-STO-13). Not changed: any Kit or WP-B file, `IntegrityBookkeeping.swift` (L5K-R25). WP-K's own `docs/MIGRATION.md` (L5K-R10). None is a pre-existing CDN test (MIGRATION.md §3 maps those one to one).
- **Benchmarks.** None run by WP-K (L5K-R11, L5K-R26: WP-K owns no benchmark). C2 of an adoption by a sync (L5K-R1; a release-mode probe on macOS 27 at a load of 3.1–4.7, never committed; `k.md` "L5K-R1"): the engine's longest turn 0.084–0.123 ms with 150 prior-art files, 0.170–0.288 ms with 1,000 and 1.589–1.949 ms with 10,000, the store's 0.113–0.179, 0.350–0.475 and 1.084–2.295 ms, and no transfer in any case; measured on cycle 1's code (one install per adoption) and not re-measured after L5K-R14 (each install was an O(1) swap). The whole suite, P-13 (owner item, ADR-050) included, is the orchestrator's near-idle run (Open).
- **Ratified decisions** (rulings L5K-R1…L5K-R28; normative detail in `k.md`).
  - *CCR-K1, approved and widened to the class (L5K-R1).* While legacy migration is pending (set in the turn the engine becomes `running` when `open()` reported it; cleared only when `migrateLegacy` reports `finished`), no transfer starts for an asset whose prior-art file can still be adopted, whatever created the job. Done with option (b) in the one funnel: the pre-transfer read (`answeredFromStore`, rows f-11 and k-4) asks `adoptLegacyNow` instead of `location(for:)`; CCR-K1's option (a), `performSync` joining the migration's launch run, covers only the explicit sync and was ruled insufficient alone. Every transfer path is enumerated (`k.md` "L5K-R1"). The parked launch run is settled by option (a) of its own decision (`k.md` "The parked launch run"), a choice distinct from CCR-K1's option (a): a run that starts while the launch run waits takes its place, and the migration loop's end starts the launch run only if the flag is still set, clearing it in every case (ARCHITECTURE §7.10; pinned by two engine tests and the resumed-migration test's flag pin, L5K-C1-05).
  - *The class that followed, written as class rulings once it repeated (ARCHITECTURE §4.7, §5, §7.1).* **L5K-R13:** one adoption per prior-art file, whoever asks (`MigrationState.adopting`, `onDemand`; rows k-5, k-6), the ruling's acceptable alternative widened (a file a batch has not finished hashing stays adoptable at once), with every state and pair of processors enumerated. **L5K-R14:** `adoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)`; the funnel passes `.userInitiated` for the user-initiated lane and `.bulk` otherwise, so ARCHITECTURE §5's install-rate bound holds during migration. **L5K-R16:** every coalesced publication's trailing install is scheduled: the RE-S40 re-read after every store call that can leave an install due (every engine call of a store method enumerated), crossed answers keep the earlier deadline (`applyStoreDeadline`), and the idle tripwire `invariantViolations(storeDeadlineDue:)`. **L5K-R17:** no hot loop around a store call (row k-7; every engine loop around a store call enumerated). **L5K-R18:** a job promoted during its adoption is installed before its user-initiated answer, since L5K-R21 by the answer site's check. **L5K-R20:** a store call never suspends after a coalesced publication of its own: a batch that leaves files returns right after it (`remaining > 0`), the last batch installs at once (option (a) for that batch only: a return there would be RE-S29's back-off signal, which WP-F's `deferredMigrationFinishesInMaintenance` pins). **L5K-R21, as scoped by L5K-R24:** in the funnel a user-initiated asker is answered only after a check of the job's interests made after the last ordinary await, with one user-initiated publication when an answered id came from a read that installed nothing at once. **L5K-R23:** what is left is read after the batch's publication (FIXLAST), the re-arm follows each bulk-urgency read (PERREAD), and ids that join during the reads are read until none is new; the fixer's extension to ids that join during the answer site's publication is **ratified**. **L5K-R25:** the answer site tries the earlier answered ids, latest first; `drive` sends no request for an attempt the reads left no longer current; the liveness tests wait on `CorePoll.until`.
  - *Accepted exceptions and records (L5K-R27).* **The dooms exception to L5K-R20** (L5K-R25, L5K-C4-11): a doom's removal-only install ahead of `recordFailure`'s and `recordIntegrityFailure`'s ordered commit is the one store call that suspends after marking an install due; it holds back no adopted or committed asset, I-3 keeps the bytes until a snapshot without the asset is acknowledged, and the engine re-reads the deadline after each such call; `IntegrityBookkeeping.swift` is not changed. **The next-id fallback** (L5K-R25, L5K-C4-07) is defensive and unpinned: an id the reads answered present stops being present only through a store mutation that installs the whole state itself, so no seam reaches the fallback's second iteration (ARCHITECTURE §14).
  - *Shim decisions approved as decided.* K-1 (the public API only: `Foundation`, `ZTronAssetKit` and, for the coordinator, `Combine`; no `package` member, no `@testable`; the test seams stay internal in the adopting module). K-7 with L5K-R9 (a `.complete` run with `manifestFresh == false` maps to `.complete` and writes no stamp, so the next launch retries as the prior art did; the coordinator's `.complete` on an app-version change is a partial pin, accepted). K-8 with L5K-R7 and L5K-R6 (the monitor observes `AssetEnvironment.conditions`, not a client's events, because an idle client observes nothing, R-SYN-14, and failing test `c623b34` proved the event-driven version misses the prior art's retry moment; `shared` builds its own live source, a second path monitor in the process; retries run on every satisfied conditions report, Low Power Mode and thermal reports included, documented with no behaviour change). K-10 with L5K-R3 (the auto-advancing clock, accepted with its conditions; ARCHITECTURE §14). K-11 with L5K-R9 and L5K-R5 (the differences inherited from the module, ADR-032/L4-R16 and ADR-037, accepted and pinned; `existingLocalURL`'s lifetime is ADR-017's consumer contract). K-13 (above). *L5K-R2:* L5-R14's patterns (a)–(g), plus the auto-advancing clock, checked for every test and helper of the compatibility target (the table in `k.md`), with stress runs ×20 on a loaded host, ×5 under TSan and on the simulator. *L5K-R4:* every migration-window passage rescoped to "a launch on which legacy migration is still adopting the prior art's files". *L5K-R10:* `docs/MIGRATION.md` is WP-K's file. *L5K-R12:* the gate's per-target figures stay above their floors (Sync 93.42 %, Storage 97.36 %, Kit 97.83 % on the merged head, against 93.54 %, 97.41 % and 97.83 % at `l5g-merged`); the shims are at 98.18 % (≥ 70 %), with the uncovered lines accepted as listed. *L5K-R11, L5K-R26:* no benchmark; a verification budget (Process). *L5K-R15, L5K-R19, L5K-R22:* the clearly right items of cycles 1–3, done.
  - *Recorded by reference* (normative detail in `k.md`): K-4 (`hasAsset` and `existingLocalURL`), K-9 (deprecations, and calls through protocol requirements in the tests), K-12 (failing tests first, with compiling skeletons), K-14 (`localURL(for:)` stays inside the legacy root), the L5K-R1 transfer-path table, the L5K-R13 state-and-pair table, the L5K-R16 store-call table with its "suspends after publishing?" column, the L5K-R17 loop table, the L5K-R21 answer-site table and the L5K-R2 table.
- **Acknowledged corrections** (the code was checked against each). (1) L5K-R1's line "the store's install-rate limiter coalesces snapshot installs" was wrong: `adoptLegacyNow` published at `.userInitiated`, which the limiter never coalesces; the orchestrator corrected it in cycle 2 and L5K-R14 fixed the consequence. (2) L5K-R18 and L5K-R21 stated an invariant stronger than the architecture (ARCHITECTURE §5 makes a user-initiated *publication* install at once, not every user-initiated *answer* wait for the readers' snapshot); L5K-R24 scopes it to the funnel, the code WP-K changed. (3) L5K-R28's list of authorised WP-F edits omits `EngineFailures.swift`, `EngineManifest.swift` and `EngineModelTests.swift`, which L5K-R16 authorises ("re-arm call additions … in any other engine file the enumeration finds"; "the existing engine model"); they are recorded under L5K-R16 here and in ARCHITECTURE §1.2. (4) "Option (a)" names two things: CCR-K1's (joining the launch run, ruled insufficient) and the parked-run decision's (a run that starts while the launch run waits takes its place, taken); this ADR and ARCHITECTURE §7.10 name them apart.
- **Rulings over the draft** (stale text; ADR-055 and ARCHITECTURE hold what is true). The header's "all are main-actor awaits of `CDNSyncCoordinator` … ARCHITECTURE §10 may list them under a new heading or by reference": rows k-4…k-7 are engine and store awaits, and all seven are in ARCHITECTURE §10.7 (L5K-R28; the header's wording was corrected in the draft in this pass). CCR-K1 as filed (options (a) and (b) as "WP-F's choice"; MIGRATION.md step 4 keeping the coordinator out of the launch task) is superseded by L5K-R1, as its status line says. K-13's "DECISIONS.md's owner review list": the owner options are recorded here, not on that list (corrected in the draft in this pass). The L5K-R14 section's "every id a job answers a user-initiated asker with, whenever that asker joined, is installed before the call returns" holds for the funnel only (L5K-R24). The L5K-R20 section's proposed §5 sentence predates L5K-R23 (a batch that finds nothing left once its publication returned also installs at once; ARCHITECTURE §5 carries the corrected sentence). The L5K-R1 C2 table's last column describes cycle 1's code. L5K-R18's per-read second ask is folded into L5K-R21's check. The L5K-R21 enumeration's request for a commit-path ruling was ruled by L5K-R24 (no change; Stage 3), and L5K-R25 authorised `EngineDispatch.swift` for `drive`'s guard only. The proposed RE-S40 amendment's list omits `publish` when the store's call threw, which the L5K-R16 table names (ARCHITECTURE §10.2 includes it). The draft's "the orchestrator numbers them at merge": decision and row identifiers are final.
- **Assignments to later work packages** (ARCHITECTURE §11, §14 set accordingly; §11 credits R-CAR-6's 68 WP-K names, grouped by the ruled behaviour and the NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW decision each pins, and the R-STO-11, R-STO-13 and R-SYN-14 names WP-K wrote in WP-F's and WP-C's targets).
  - *Stage 3 backlog (L5K-R27).* (1) `LegacyOnDemandRaceTests.swift`: `waiterTakesOverACancelledProcessing` (`R-STO-11: an adopt-on-demand waiting for another call whose caller was cancelled takes the file over and adopts it (L5K-R13)`) and the file's other tracked-call reads wait on `CorePoll.until` before reading results, so a lost resume fails at the time limit, as `closeWhileTheBatchWaits` and `closeResumesAWaitingAsker` do since L5K-R25. (2) Before Stage 5's `--full` (a note for Stage 5 too), make WP-C's `R-STO-11: phase 4 stops listing the legacy tree after the unit in flight once the caller cancels or the store closes` (`LegacyEdgeTests.phaseFourStopsListing`, `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Cleanup/LegacyEdgeTests.swift:417-426`, `:446`) deterministic: under TSan it fails with `listings() → 3` in about 1 of 4–5 fresh launches, at the same rate on `l5g-merged` (2 of 10), so pre-existing (L5K-R26); the cycle-5 requirements verifier (`wf_02981dcc-c86`, its non-blocking issue on this test) traced it to a check-then-act race in the test's poll, not in the product (after the stop exactly one listing runs in every case), and offered two fixes: (a) read `heldUnits` before `listings()` in the poll and release only that unit, or (b) capture `listings()` right after `requireHeldUnits(atLeast: 1)` and assert exactly one listing after it. (3) The optional package `AssetStore.installIfDue()` with L5K-R21's answer-site check at the commit path, `ensureAvailable`'s present members, the resolve's fast hit and the ledger joins, if a device test shows L5K-R24's lag matters; at the commit path the check would follow `store.publish` (the lane read before the call not user-initiated, the job's lane after it user-initiated: one `adoptLegacyNow(id, urgency: .userInitiated)`, then the lifecycle and the epoch re-checked) and must decide how a binding lost during that extra await is answered (`committed` treats it as refused); it needs `EngineDispatch.swift` authorised. (4) and (5) need no work (above).
  - *WP-F and WP-C / Stage 3 (comments only).* `EngineDispatch.swift:743-744`'s REENTRANCY comment ("an attempt it did not end goes on below, which re-validates them after `remote.transfer`") is incomplete since L5K-R25: the lifecycle, the epoch and the attempt are also re-checked before `remote.transfer` (`:750-763`). `LegacyPhases.swift:426-431`'s comment above L5K-R17's wait ("so it finishes the migration itself once the last one ended") omits the return when a processing it waited for put its file back in the queue, which the k-7 REENTRANCY line below it states (ARCHITECTURE §4.7 (3)). Both are `//` lines, outside this pass's doc-comment edits.
  - *Stage 5 and the WP-B and WP-H owners (L5K-R25, environment).* WP-B's `R-STO-5: a process kill followed by a power loss in the next lifetime preserves I-1…I-5` (`StoreCrashTests.swift:167`) and WP-H's `Test support: start racing stop ends every start exactly once, with a port or stopped, and never hangs` (`LoopbackHTTPServerTests.swift:400`) exceeded their one-minute limits at loads of 441–834 from parallel probe campaigns; they are non-blocking when they pass at moderate load; run the gates near-idle, or track them as test-robustness items, and take verdicts from exit status (`.verify-logs` is shared).
  - *WP-I (end of Stage 2).* `docs/MIGRATION.md` is WP-K's file (L5K-R10): WP-I does not rewrite it as unowned (ARCHITECTURE §1.2).
  - *Owner adoption (ADR-006, ADR-033; Stage 4 as replaced).* Copy `Shims/` into ZTronCarouselCore as MIGRATION.md describes: the shims use only `ZTronAssetKit`'s public API, and their test seams stay internal in the adopting module (`NetworkAvailabilityMonitor.flush()`, `StaticManifestCDNProvider.init(…executor:)`, `CDNSyncCoordinator.init(sharingClientOf:cdn:)`); install a configured client first so the key is kept (step 3); the launch-task sync is safe (step 4); `verifyFullyAvailable()` needs a manifest source that sends the key; `BasicVideoPage` must look a URL up again on a failed read or hold a lease; run Q-10 there.
  - *Orchestrator (before tagging `l5-merged`).* Whole-package TSan and `verify.sh --perf` on a near-idle host (Open).
- **REENTRANCY rows.** The proposed rows k-1…k-7 keep their identifiers as final numbers in the new ARCHITECTURE §10.7 (L5K-R28; the code cites "k-n (proposed)"; the word is historical, as for h-n, b-n, c-n, f-n and g-n): k-1…k-3 are main-actor awaits of the shim `CDNSyncCoordinator`, k-4 a `SyncEngine` await in WP-F's `EnginePresence.swift` (§10.2 points to it), k-5…k-7 `AssetStore` awaits in WP-C's `Migration/` (§10.1 points to them). k-4 is copied with three precisions the code needs: the re-arm follows every bulk-urgency read (one that answered nothing or threw included), the answer site's fallback tries the earlier answered ids latest first until one answers a location (a thrown error stops it), and a thrown adoption leads to the transfer only for a running engine whose attempt is still current; k-5 with one: the `onDemand` mark lasts from the claim to the processing's published snapshot, installed at once only at `.userInitiated` (the draft's "installed snapshot", corrected in the draft and in ARCHITECTURE §4.7 in this pass). Amended rows: f-11 as the draft proposes (with the same precisions), and its stale clause "any other outcome (a job no longer current, an absent id) goes on to `remote.transfer`" replaced by L5K-R25's guard; RE-S40 and RE-S27 as proposed, RE-S40's list adding `publish` when the store's call threw; f-8 gains the store-deadline read hold (`holdNextStoreDeadlineRead()`, L5K-R16, ratified by L5K-R22); R17 the exception of `migrateLegacy`'s batch publication, after which the queue, the claims and `installDue` are read again (L5K-R23); R18 points to k-5 and k-6. A scripted count over `Sources/` and the shims at the merge: k-1 ×5, k-2 ×1, k-3 ×1 (`CDNSyncCoordinator.swift`), k-4 ×3 (`EnginePresence.swift`), k-5 ×4 (`StoreMigration.swift` ×1, `LegacyPhases.swift` ×3), k-6 ×6 and k-7 ×1 (`LegacyPhases.swift`); every k-n of the draft is cited, and no other; every `// REENTRANCY:` in `ZTronAssetSync` cites an RE-S row, an f-n row or k-4 (RE-S21 ×29, RE-S40 ×31, f-8 ×9). The shims' other awaits (`CDNAssetResolver`, `StaticManifestCDNProvider`, `NetworkAvailabilityMonitor`) run in nonisolated types and need no row.
- **Not verified** (carried to `docs/VERIFICATION.md` at Stage 5, L5K-R28): `k.md` "Not verified here" by reference (ARCHITECTURE §14). Notably: the shims at run time below the iOS 17 test-bundle target (the test target builds at iOS 17 / macOS 14, ADR-051; the shims ran on the iOS 27 simulator and macOS 27 only; their iOS 15 compile is the typecheck recorded at `409db9a`, and only their doc comments changed since); the live provider and monitor (`NetworkAvailabilityMonitor.shared` over the live reachability conformer, `StaticManifestCDNProvider` against a real origin and the 403 a keyless client gets there, `defaultAppVersion()` inside an app bundle, `BackgroundAssetsCDNProvider` on a device); `CDNLocalStore(baseDirectory:)`'s placement and migration over the live file system in an app container; Q-10, the adoption inside ZTronCarouselCore (ADR-006: MIGRATION.md describes it, and nothing outside this repository was built or written); the migration window's length on a device (K-15), and the device cost of the close-out's extra reads, re-arms and installs (L5K-R16, L5K-R18, L5K-R20, L5K-R21, L5K-R23; no benchmark, L5K-R11); the main actor's enqueue order (`legacyStoreAdoptsThePriorArtFiles`'s determinism argument); one adoption per prior-art file with the live executors and on a device (L5K-R13); L5K-R16's crossed deadline answers under production priorities (reproduced with a seam); the L5K-R21 tests' gated planner, which blocks one cooperative thread for a few actor hops (a host with a single cooperative thread would deadlock it); how often a device makes a batch's install wait at the main actor long enough for an adopt-on-demand to end during it (L5K-C4-00); the size on a device of the lag L5K-R24 keeps at the other answer sites. Found at the merge and not in `k.md`: the shims across a facade reopen after a replaced root (ADR-054's WP-K note; G-1, G-2): no shim test or MIGRATION.md passage covers the lookups' `nil` between the mirror reset and the replacement's first install, or the coordinator's subscription across a reopen.
- **Open.** Nothing in the documents. Before the tag, the orchestrator ran the near-idle checks at `a9e464d` (the WP-K merge head; logs `.build-orch/l5k/`). Whole-package TSan: every target passed (151, 351, 459, 84, 85, 71 and 104 tests) with 0 ThreadSanitizer reports, at a 1-minute load of 2.5 → 6.7. `verify.sh --perf` at a load of 6.7 → 3.5: 34 of 35 benchmarks passed. Among them: P-5 durable p95 23.4 ms (budget 40 ms, ordered p95 13.7 ms), P-11 speedup 3.43 (min 3.0), every C2 benchmark's longest turn ≤ 6.1 ms (10 ms), and WP-G's five within budget (P-1 p99 0.458 µs, P-9 heartbeat p99 15.1 ms, P-12 0.005 ms over `.live()`). The one miss is WP-B's P-13: the longest deletion unit took 8.70 ms against the 5.0 ms per-batch budget. It awaits the owner's ruling on ADR-050's proposal, so `--perf` ends `VERIFY: FAIL (1 failed: benchmarks)`. The orchestrator confirmed the "Acknowledged corrections" (L5K-R29) and applied the verifier's last 3 notes.
- **Process.** Class rulings converged where case fixes had not: CCR-K1's family took the download, the store race and the install scheduling, then a stale read across an await, a lost per-read re-arm and late joiners, before L5K-R16, L5K-R17, L5K-R20, L5K-R21 and L5K-R23 were written as class rulings with enumerations, a tripwire and mutants (the L5 lesson: demand the enumeration as soon as a class repeats). A ruling's invariant must not exceed the architecture's (L5K-R24 corrected L5K-R18 and L5K-R21). The harness's install interval of 0 hid L5K-R16's class, so one shim test runs at the production interval (PK-R14b). A verification cycle cost 7–10 points of weekly usage by cycle 4, hence L5K-R26's budget: each lens runs the required gates once, probes only to prove or disprove a suspected defect (at most 20 launches per probe, one heavy command at a time), and reports behaviour in code WP-K did not change as non-blocking `pre-existing` unless WP-K made it reachable or worse. Verdicts come from each agent's exit status (`.verify-logs` is shared); parallel probe campaigns pushed the load to 441–834 and tripped unrelated time limits (L5K-R25).
- **Amendment (Stage 2 WP-I documentation merge, 2026-10-24, ADR-056).** The WP-I assignment is done: WP-I did not rewrite `docs/MIGRATION.md` (L5K-R10); the file's only change since `l5-merged` is the orchestrator's WPI-R7 pointer in step 3 (`52936d6`), ownership unchanged. The Context's `--quick` counts name Model and Transport the wrong way round, as ADR-054's do: at `7d90719` Transport has the 151 tests (16 suites) and Model the 84 (18 suites), as `.build-orch/l5k/summary.txt`'s "151 tests in 16 suites" and "84 tests in 18 suites" and the two targets' `@Test` and `@Suite` counts show; the other five names are right.
- **Amendment (Stage 3 merge, 2026-11-07, ADR-057).**
  - L5K-R25, WP-H half: the test cited at `LoopbackHTTPServerTests.swift:400` is at `:404` and carries a three-minute guard with S3-R4's measurements (S3AH-5); its 400 rounds, yields and assertions are unchanged, and each round starts with `try Task.checkCancellation()`.
  - Stage 3 backlog (1), `LegacyOnDemandRaceTests`' tracked-call reads, is done by S3C-5 (all 23 joins follow an "ended" poll); (2), a deterministic `phaseFourStopsListing` before Stage 5, is done by S3C-1 (50 fresh TSan launches passed, twice), so the WP-C TSan flake ORCHESTRATION lists is resolved; (4), the dooms exception to L5K-R20, is unchanged (S3-B-041; the S3C-9 guard skips only present records).

## ADR-056 — Stage 2 WP-I merge: documentation and README decisions · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (WPI-R5: the consumer toolchain)
- **Context.** WP-I (documentation: the README and the consistency of WP-I's documents; the "end | I docs" row of ARCHITECTURE §1.2) ran on `l5-merged` at the owner's request ("Run WP-I: documentation and README", 2026-10-24), with the usage stop at 75 % (the owner raised it from the orchestrator's 70 % default): workflow `wf_3045417c-704` (`.claude/workflows/assetkit-wp-i-docs.js`: an author, then an accuracy verifier and a usability verifier). Round 1: 3 blocking out of 19 findings (README facts: the budget default, the prior-art symbol list, the fallback chain's prior art); the fix round fixed all 19 (I-8); round 2: **0 blocking out of 11**. The orchestrator applied round 2's 11 last notes in `f234cda`, checking each fact against the code first ("Acknowledged at merge" (1)). Merged into `feature/asset-kit` as `70b339b`; the orchestrator's WPI-R7 pointer is `52936d6`. The orchestrator's checks on `52936d6` (`.build-orch/wpi/summary.txt`): `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS` (151, 351, 459, 84, 85, 71 and 104 tests); `--gates-only` `VERIFY: PASS` (selftest, harness unchanged since `harness-frozen`, trace with 1,181 tests with IDs, unsafe 0, docs 1281/1281). The decision record is `docs/adr-drafts/i.md` (I-1…I-8, the quick-start compile evidence, "Checks before hand-off" and "Left for the orchestrator"); that draft, with the orchestrator rulings WPI-R1…WPI-R8 (`.build-orchestrator/wpi-rulings.md`, which is not in the repository; recorded here), is normative detail for this ADR. Where the draft and a ruling disagree, the ruling wins (see "Rulings over the draft").
- **Ratified contract changes.** None by WP-I: `git diff --name-only l5-merged 52936d6 -- Sources Tests Package.swift Scripts` is empty, so no public or package signature and no Swift, manifest, harness or test file changed. Doc comments made true of the merged code in this pass, `///` lines only (`git diff 52936d6 -- Sources` holds nothing else; the docs gate stays at 1281/1281). *Ruled:* `StorageLocation.custom` (the Model contract file `Policy/StoragePolicy.swift:9-11`; WPI-R6's wording: an App Group container is unsupported "unless the app shuts the client down when it is backgrounded and creates a new one when it returns", because `AssetClient` has no close and `shutdown()` is final, `AssetClient.swift:419-421`). *Not ruled*, for the orchestrator to ratify (precisions this pass made under its `///` permission, to text that predates this merge): `AssetEnvironment`'s type doc (the Model contract file `Environment/AssetEnvironment.swift:3-6`) and `AssetEnvironment.live()` (`Composition/LiveEnvironment.swift:8-17`), which called the composition root the only place that names live conformers: it is the only place that names the environment's live conformers, and three seams outside the environment default to their own (`AssetFallbackChain(client:bundle:)` to `MainBundleLookup`, `FallbackChain.swift:99`; `AppleHostedTier.resolve` to `LiveAssetPackBackend` when no backend is passed, `AppleHostedTier.swift:46`; the background backend's `makeDriver` to `LiveBackgroundSessionDriver`, `HTTPRemoteSource.swift:93`; as ARCHITECTURE §1 now says); and `AssetEnvironment.memoryPressure` (`AssetEnvironment.swift:26-27`) and `.live()`, which now say that `.live()` sets no source (WPI-R3's fact; WPI-R3 itself orders only ARCHITECTURE §8 and the ADR-044 amendment).
- **Authorised edits outside WP-I** (ratified; ownership unchanged; ARCHITECTURE §1.2). The orchestrator's `f234cda` in WP-I's `README.md` and `docs/adr-drafts/i.md` (the 11 last notes), and its `52936d6` in WP-K's `docs/MIGRATION.md` step 3 (WPI-R7: a configured `appleHosted` has no fallback, so pass it only in a build that ships the Apple-hosting setup; it points to the README's "The Apple-hosted tier" and APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §1.7 (c)). WP-I itself never edited `docs/MIGRATION.md`, as ADR-055 assigned (ADR-055 amendment).
- **Benchmarks.** None: WP-I owns no benchmark and changed no code.
- **Ratified decisions** (rulings WPI-R1…WPI-R8; normative detail in `i.md`).
  - *The consumer toolchain (WPI-R5)* · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (toolchain floor for consumers, owner information for ZTronCarouselCore). SPEC §3's Swift 6.3 is for building and testing this repository. A consumer compiles the package's sources, and three `@concurrent` functions (SE-0461, whose status line reads "Implemented (Swift 6.2)") have no compiler guard (`ZTronAssetSync`'s `Engine/EngineLifecycle.swift:350`, `EngineRuns.swift:312` and `EngineCommands.swift:1186`; no `#if` in those files, no other `@concurrent` in `Sources/`), so a consumer needs a Swift 6.2+ toolchain; 6.2 is a lower bound, since only Swift 6.4 built the package. Tools version 6.0 lets a manifest on tools 6.0 declare the dependency; it does not let an older toolchain build it. The README states it (`README.md:16-20`). No code change: letting an older toolchain build would be a code change outside WP-I. No ADR-005 amendment and no note on SPEC §3 (the owner's); `Package.swift:2-3`'s comment is left unchanged (a shared file outside this pass; WPI-R5 orders no code change).
  - *Approved as decided (WPI-R1).* **I-1** the README is an integrator's guide: evergreen (no stage status, date, run id, usage, test count, coverage or benchmark figure), each statement taken from the merged public declarations' doc comments or from an ADR or ARCHITECTURE passage and citing it, a NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW fact stated as the code does it with its ADR, and MIGRATION.md linked rather than repeated. **I-2** the quick start is top-level code (it runs on the main actor, SE-0343): 20 lines, 19 of them code, compiled verbatim under `-warnings-as-errors` with no wrapping and cross-built for `arm64-apple-ios15.0`; `lookup` then `resolve`, `subscribe()` (registered when it returns) rather than `events`, and an obviously fake key under R-SEC-1. **I-3** the five other Swift snippets compile, each wrapped over a prelude of stubs; the manifest fragment uses the two forms of the check package's own manifest. **I-4** links name files, never anchors, and the section or ADR is named in the text, so no link breaks when a heading changes. **I-5** a one-line historical status note on line 2 of the three designs and the six reviews replaces the blank line, so no line moves (WPI-R4 amends ADR-051 and ADR-053 accordingly; ADR-052's "store.md §6.5 is not amended" stays true). **I-6** the README carries ADR-051's simulator commands and says that `VERIFY: PASS` alone does not show the simulator run (ADR-051's "Open" on CLAUDE.md stays the owner's). **I-7** the documentation map: one row per tracked file under `docs/`, the authoritative, historical and generated files marked, and a pointer to the owner review list. **I-8** the fix round: each finding checked against the code or document it cites, all fixed, none declined.
  - *Ownership (WPI-R2).* ARCHITECTURE §1.2's "end | I docs" row also excludes `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` (the orchestrator's, now also in §1.2's orchestrator-only list) and the Stage 3/5 reports `MUTATION-PROBES.md`, `VERIFICATION.md`, `DEVICE-TEST-PLAN.md` and `RESIDUAL-RISKS.md` (Stage 3's, and the orchestrator's at Stage 5); `README.md` stays WP-I's.
  - *Memory pressure under `.live()` (WPI-R3).* `AssetEnvironment.live()` passes no `MemoryPressureSource` (`LiveEnvironment.swift:20-31`; `AssetEnvironment.memoryPressure` is a public `var` that defaults to `nil`, `AssetEnvironment.swift:28`, `:47`), and the engine owns its observer only when `environment.memoryPressure` is non-nil (`EngineCommands.swift:1327`), so a registered `PurgeableCache` is purged only when the app supplies a source (ADR-044 amendment; ARCHITECTURE §2, §7.8, §7.10, §8).
  - *WPI-R4, WPI-R6 and WPI-R7* are applied as above and in the dated amendments of ADR-051 and ADR-053.
- **Acknowledged at merge** (the code was checked against each statement). (1) The facts `f234cda` added to the README: `prepare(kinds:)` replaces the bundle index, which starts empty (`FallbackChain.swift:82`, `:116`); `PurgeRequest.scope` deletes pinned and essential entries (`Reports.swift:148-149`); `TierSelection.select` has no fallback for a configured Apple tier (`TierSelection.swift:59-67`); the manifest defaults (`manifestPath` `"manifest.json"`, `RemoteConfiguration.swift:98`; the envelope, `ValidatedManifest.swift:8`); shutdown's scope (`AssetClient.swift:419-421`); document precedence (PROMPT.md's conflict rule). None contradicts ARCHITECTURE; §6.6 and §8 now state the Apple tier's lack of fallback and the replaced index. (2) Figures at the merge: `README.md` has 102 relative links (none external, none anchored, every target present), its documentation map names all 31 tracked files under `docs/`, the quick start is 20 lines (19 code, 1 comment, no blank), and the README's nine fenced blocks are byte-identical to `4ac324c`, where I-2 and I-3 took their evidence, so that evidence covers the merged README. (3) ADR-055's WP-I assignment holds: WP-I did not rewrite `docs/MIGRATION.md`, whose only change since `l5-merged` is `52936d6`. (4) ADR-054's and ADR-055's Contexts name Model and Transport the wrong way round in their `--quick` counts (Transport has 151 tests in 16 suites, Model 84 in 18, at both heads and now); their dated amendments correct it, and the counts above carry no target names, as the log gives none.
- **Rulings over the draft** (stale text; ADR-056 and ARCHITECTURE hold what is true). "Left for the orchestrator" item 1 ("no NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW item arises from WP-I's own choices … item 9 is a fact about the existing code"): WPI-R5 tags the consumer toolchain, in this heading and on the owner review list. Item 9's options (beside ADR-005, or a note on SPEC §3): WPI-R5 records it here only. Item 8's wording for ADR-051 and ADR-053: WPI-R4's dated amendments, ending "(WP-I, I-5; ADR-056)", with the original lines unchanged. Items 5, 6, 7 and 10 left open the MIGRATION.md pointer, the §8 sentence, the §1.2 row and the `StorageLocation.custom` doc comment; WPI-R7 (made at `52936d6`, so item 5's premise that MIGRATION.md does not tell the owner to add the downloader extension no longer holds), WPI-R3, WPI-R2 and WPI-R6 decide them. I-1's three facts that "the authoritative documents do not state outright" (the live environment's missing memory-pressure source, the downloader extension, the consumer toolchain) are now stated by ARCHITECTURE §8 with the ADR-044 amendment, MIGRATION.md step 3 and this ADR. I-8 and "Checks before hand-off" count 98 README links, true at `08ebba4`: 102 since `f234cda`. I-8 records fix round 1 only; round 2 and the orchestrator's last notes are in the context above. The draft's "the orchestrator numbers them at merge": I-1…I-8 are final.
- **Assignments to later work packages** (WPI-R8; ARCHITECTURE §11 gains no row: no test is assigned, and WP-I owns none).
  - *Stage 3 and Stage 5.* When `docs/MUTATION-PROBES.md` (Stage 3) and `VERIFICATION.md`, `DEVICE-TEST-PLAN.md` and `RESIDUAL-RISKS.md` (Stage 5) exist, add one row each to the README's documentation map, which lists every tracked file under `docs/` (I-7); `README.md` stays WP-I's (WPI-R2), so that edit is recorded as the orchestrator's.
  - *Stage 5, and after any change to a public signature the README uses.* Re-run the quick-start check (I-2, the iOS 15 cross-build included) and the snippet build (I-3). The quick start uses `AppKey.init(_:)`, `HTTPSourceConfiguration.init(baseURL:…appKey:…)`, `RemoteConfiguration.init(http:…)`, `AssetClientConfiguration.init(remote:…)`, `AssetClient.init(configuration:environment:)`, `start()`, `subscribe()`, `AssetEvent.assetAvailable`, `PrefetchWindowController.init(client:radius:)`, `update(ids:current:)`, `lookup(_:extensions:)` and `resolve(_:priority:)`, and the snippets use more. No gate fails when the README drifts. The scripts exist only in the git-ignored `.worktrees/i/.build-i/tools/` (`check_links.py`, `extract_qs.py`, `extract_snippets.py`, `split_hunks.py`), and the scratch packages `.build-i/qs` and `qsx` depend on `.package(path: …/.worktrees/i)`: before that worktree is removed, copy the scripts (or rebuild them from I-2, I-3 and "Links") and point the scratch packages at the main checkout.
  - *When the owner rules, and at release* (`README.md`; MIGRATION.md step 1, WP-K's). Once the owner rules on a NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW decision the README states, update that sentence: the storage location (ADR-001, with the App Group sentence at `README.md:113-116`), pack retention (ADR-027), background transfers (ADR-026, ADR-039 / D-10), the fallback-chain deviations (ADR-037), the migration deviations (ADR-052), the shims (ADR-033, ADR-055) and the consumer toolchain (this ADR, `README.md:16-20`). At release, show the `.package(url:from:)` form in the Installation snippet (`README.md:39-47`; lines 36-37 already name both forms) and replace the path in MIGRATION.md step 1 (`docs/MIGRATION.md:12-13`, which already says to switch on release).
  - *Stage 5 (`docs/DEVICE-TEST-PLAN.md`, `VERIFICATION.md`).* SPEC §8's device test plan covers memory warnings: with `.live()` alone a memory warning on a device purges nothing, so a device test of R-CLN-11 needs an app-supplied UIKit source (WPI-R3), and the plan says so. Carry this ADR's "Not verified" items into VERIFICATION.md beside the earlier levels' (ARCHITECTURE §14).
  - *Every later documentation pass.* The README names ARCHITECTURE and APPLE-API-VERIFICATION sections and ADRs by number in its text (I-4), so renumbering any of them needs a README update: add text in place, as this pass did.
- **REENTRANCY rows.** None: WP-I changed no Swift file and proposes no row, no code cites an i-n row, and every identifier `Sources/` cites already has a §10 row.
- **Not verified** (carried to `docs/VERIFICATION.md` at Stage 5): `i.md` I-1, I-2 with its "Quick-start compile evidence", I-3, I-6 and "Left for the orchestrator" item 9 by reference (the draft has no "Not verified here" section; ARCHITECTURE §14). Notably: the Swift 6.2 consumer floor (a lower bound from the three unguarded `@concurrent` functions and SE-0461's status line, read from swift-evolution in WP-I's session and not re-read at the merge; only Swift 6.4, swiftlang-6.4.0.34.1 with Xcode 27.0, built the package; neither a Swift 6.2 or 6.3 toolchain nor ZTronCarouselCore's toolchain was tried); the quick start was compiled verbatim on macOS and cross-built for iOS 15, never run (no origin, no network use), so what it implies at run time (`subscribe()` registered when it returns, with the current state replayed; `lookup` on the main actor) rests on the code and the doc comments; the five other snippets compiled only wrapped over stubs, with two "never used" warnings, so not under `-warnings-as-errors`, and none ran; the Installation fragment (`README.md:39-47`) was not compiled, its identity rule resting on the check package's resolving `package: "i"`; the README's iOS Simulator commands (`README.md:399-402`, quoted from ADR-051) were not re-run, and `VERIFY: PASS` alone does not show a simulator run; the Apple-hosting setup that the README (`README.md:306-330`) and MIGRATION.md step 3 ask of the app (the downloader extension on StoreKit's `StoreDownloaderExtension`, iOS 26, from the SDK declaration; download policies as build-time data, from a labelled web cross-check; the Info.plist keys and the App Group) is not verified beyond APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §1.7, no key is named and nothing ran on a device (beside the existing device-only items, APPLE-API-VERIFICATION §5); the link check (`.build-i/tools/check_links.py`) proves only that each relative link names an existing file, not that the section or ADR named beside it says what the text claims, which the verifiers and the orchestrator checked by reading.
- **Open.** Nothing in the documents. The orchestrator closed this pass's three follow-ups at merge: (1) `README.md`'s toolchain line now cites this ADR beside SPEC §3 (I-1's rule: a NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW fact cites its ADR); (2) `docs/MIGRATION.md` step 1 (WP-K's file) now tells the adopter to build ZTronCarouselCore with Swift 6.2 or newer, pending the owner's review of WPI-R5; (3) `docs/ORCHESTRATION.md` named Model and Transport the wrong way round in three `--quick` count lines, as ADR-054 and ADR-055 did, and now reads Model 84 and Transport 151 (`k.md`'s copies stay as historical text under ADR-055's amendment). The orchestrator also applied the verifier's last 2 notes: the live seams in ARCHITECTURE §1 and `AssetEnvironment.live()`'s doc comment are listed as examples, now including `HTTPRemoteSource`'s foreground backend.
- **Process.** I-5's first commit inserted the notes above line 3 and moved every cited line of the nine files by two (about a hundred citations across the repository); the follow-up commit put each note on the blank line 2 instead, so a historical note moves no cited line. A documentation work package still compiles its snippets (I-2, I-3), because no gate fails when the README drifts; links that name files, with the section named in the text, survive heading changes, and ADR headings change whenever the owner rules (I-4).

## ADR-057 — Stage 3 hardening merge: the WP-A/WP-H, WP-B, WP-C, WP-D, WP-G and WP-F close-outs, and the owner's close of WP-F · **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW** (re-ratified: S3-B-001, S3-B-002, C-12…C-15, D-10, D-19.2, `startDownload`; owner items: f3c's two open shapes with OS-0, the uncommitted `Package.swift`)
- **Context.** Stage 3 (hardening) ran discovery on `s3-base` (a backlog survey, adversarial hunters with failing probes, mutation probes and an independent confirmation of every claimed bug; `.build-orchestrator/s3-hunt-result.json`), then one close-out per affected work package: fixes, then two-lens independent verification until 0 blocking, under the orchestrator's rulings (`.build-orchestrator/s3-rulings-s3ah.md`, `-s3b.md`, `-s3c.md`, `-s3d.md`, `-s3g.md` and `-s3f.md`, which decide where a draft differs). The close-outs merged `--no-ff` into `feature/asset-kit`: s3ah `f68eb1b`, s3b `48b662d`, s3c `e92e456`, s3d `768e9c0` and s3g `d59bcc4` on 30 Oct, and s3f `e840b0e` on 6 Nov; `8d86acb` regenerates the traceability table on the merged head. Their decision records, normative detail for this ADR by reference, are `docs/adr-drafts/s3ah.md`, `s3b.md`, `s3c.md`, `s3d.md`, `s3g.md` and `s3f.md` (cycles 1 to 23).
  - **WP-A/WP-H (`docs/adr-drafts/s3ah.md`).** 6 items (S3AH-1…5 required, S3AH-6 record) and 16 findings, none blocking; one verification cycle, 0 blocking. Failing tests first (`1a41f29`), then `dea6d83`, `af7c0d1`, `87d108c`, `8b2e979`, `a4a7542` and the record `7c1015c`; merged `--no-ff` as `f68eb1b`. One finding was routed `other-wp:d` (the `RemoteURL` doc, fixed in this pass). The close-out ran `--quick` (PASS), the release and iOS builds, the unsafe and docs gates, coverage, the Model tests three times, Model and Storage under TSan, P-3 and the simulator suite, but not `--gates-only` or `--full`; the merged head's `--full` covers them.
  - **WP-G (`s3g.md`).** Close-out cycle 1, 0 blocking, in fix run `wf_7d85c00a-ec5` (shared with WP-D): `b483477` (failing first), `8688503`, `a3aae8b`, `09abe48`, `d2e9409`, `a03a96c`. Evidence on `a03a96c`: `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS`; Kit 90 tests in 12 suites; release build clean; UNSAFE 0 of 3; DOCS 1281/1281; Kit coverage 97.99 % (1,464/1,494), overall 96.55 %; TSan Kit 35.292 s, 0 reports; simulator 1310/1310 (iPhone 17e, iOS 27.0); iOS generic build clean. Merged `--no-ff` as `d59bcc4` on 30 Oct, before WP-F (see Process). No finding was routed to another package (S3-R8).

Gates on `a5edd1e`, as the draft quotes them: `--quick` `VERIFY: PASS` (Model 151, Sync 351, Storage 474, Transport 84, Kit 85, Compatibility 71, Apple-hosted 104); release build 43.23 s; unsafe 0/3; docs 1281/1281; Storage three times 91.176, 75.873 and 68.528 s; TSan 213.749 s, no report; coverage Storage 97.56 %, overall 96.65 %; iOS generic build exit 0; simulator 1320/1320 (iPhone 17e, iOS 27.0; benchmarks built, not run); release benchmarks with one issue, P-13's maximum under the pre-ruling assertion.

The cycle-1 verification report was lost to a network outage (30 Oct, about 01:57-02:00 CET), so cycle 2 ran with no findings; the fixer found the defect again from the verifier's scratch path and reproduced it with its own failing test, and the close-out script now retries a verifier that returns no result twice and ends a cycle missing a lens.

- **Ratified contract changes.** No public or package signature changed in Stage 3 (S3-R6; the signature changes the close-outs asked for are pending change requests or declined, Ratified decisions), and `Package.swift` was not touched. The contract-file bodies, internal members and doc comments below are ratified as merged.
  - **WP-A/WP-H.** No file added and no edit outside the two packages. `Identity/TransferID.swift`: the body of `TransferAttempt.init?(token:)` (`:126-145`) and its `///` (`:124-125`), ratified here: "Parses `<epoch>-<sequence>` in the canonical decimal form `token` writes (no sign, no leading zero); `nil` otherwise." `Environment/Time.swift`: the body of `MonotonicInstant.adding(seconds:)` only (`:33`, `.rounded()`). `Sources/ZTronAssetTestSupport/ManualTimeSource.swift`, a Stage 1 contract file under §1's TestSupport row that the draft does not list: the body of `advance(to:)` and its four-line `///` paragraph (`:144-160`), both ratified here. No public or package signature changed; no authorised edit outside WP-G.
  - **WP-B.** `Store/StoreCoreState.swift` (contract file) gains three internal stored properties, in memory only and never encoded (`StoreCoreState.swift:177-189`): `countedReleases` and `provisionallyRescued` (S3B-9), reset at open (`AssetStore+Open.swift:162-163`), and `lastReap: ReapStatistics?` (S3B-5), the work list of the latest `reap` that listed a key, written only by `reap` (`AssetStore+Reaper.swift:216`) and not reset at open; ratified here (S3-R6 authorised no contract-file change; §1.1 lets WP-B add fields to its own sub-struct). WP-B writes WP-C's contract field `state.eviction.releasedBytes` (`AssetStore+References.swift:93-96` `countRelease`, `:104-111` `takeBackCountedRelease`; `AssetStore+Apply.swift:261-278` routes through `releaseCountingBytes`), so the field is no longer monotonic: S3B-9's wider scope (admit's releases, the previous blob of a replaced detached record or an adopted record, and cycle 2's take-back) is ratified here, its `///` (`EvictionState.swift`) corrected in this pass; readers take differences, so a lower value frees more, never less. New WP-B file `Store/ReapQueue.swift` (`ReapQueue`, `ReapStatistics`) and test file `Core/StoreUpdateCrashTests.swift`; read-only use of WP-C's test helpers (`EvictionOverlapTests`, `CleanupSteps`) by `StoreApplyTests`. WP-F relies on WP-B: `publish` returns locations only for ids of `binding` (`AssetStore+Publish.swift:176-196`).
  - **WP-C.** No public or package signature and no production file added. New internal functions `evictAcrossTurns(_:chunk:rules:)` and `acrossTurnsSlice(_:_:of:rules:first:)` (`QuotaEviction.swift`), `purgeAcrossTurns(_:request:chunk:reclaim:)` and `purgeTurnIDs(_:request:chunk:known:offset:first:)` (`StorePurge.swift`). `Integrity/StoreIntegrity.swift` (contract file): the presence guard (`:71-75`, authorised by S3C-9) and the rewritten `///` of `recordIntegrityFailure` (`:51-56`, outside the authorised range), ratified here since it describes the guard; the draft's "no contract file edited" (`s3c.md:9-10`) is inaccurate. Test targets: `CorePoll.releasing(_:_:until:sourceLocation:_:)` and `CorePoll.Release` extend WP-B's `CorePoll` from WP-C's new file `Cleanup/CorePoll+Release.swift` (authorised by S3C-1); `CleanupAuditSink` and `settleDeletions(audited:)` are new; `CleanupSteps.run(_:keeping:_:)` returns the units it released, and WP-B's `StoreApplyTests` now depend on it.
  - **WP-D.** `Background/BackgroundSupport.swift` (contract file), declarations the S3D-1 and S3D-2 designs require, ratified here: added the private `priorityRequests` and `lastPriorityRequest` (`:187-188`), `Waiter.controls` and `Waiter.end(with:)` (`:156-165`), the internal `BackgroundTransferHandle.Control` (`:732-739`), the private `controls` (`:751`), the internal `controlTask` (`:754`), the internal init's `controls:` parameter (`:756-763`) and the handle's `deinit` (`:786-789`); removed the handle's private `coordinator`; changed the relay's private storage to `[String: [() -> Void]]` (`:823`) and the behaviour and public docs of `accept` (appends, never replaces) and `complete` (calls the oldest) (`:812-819`, `:828-851`), which the draft's contract paragraph omits; package docs changed at `:121-123`, `:439-445` and `:723-729`. No public or package signature changed; `TransportContractTests.swift` is unchanged. Contract doc comments of `TransferHandle`, `RemoteSource.handleBackgroundEvents` and `AssetClient.handleBackgroundEvents` stay true.

Internal changes, acknowledged: `followApplied(from:)` moved between WP-F files with its signature; `supersede(_:settling:)` gained a defaulted internal parameter; `park(_:reason:)` and `failFast(_:reason:only:)` gained the defaulted `answeredAt:`, `at:` and `answerEnd:`; `endClaim` lost `private`; `resumeReleases`, `withdrawPurged`, `releaseEntryGroup` and `applyConditionsToJobs` were split out so the barrier's drain can replay them; the test seams `holdReleaseFollowUps()`/`resumeReleaseFollowUps()` and `holdAttemptTerminals()`/`releaseAttemptTerminals()` were added, and no production path sets them. S1 adds `SyncEngine.turnTimeLimit`, an internal static (`EngineCommands.swift:961-964`), and the private `logLongPurgeTurn` (`:1006-1013`), in a file that is not a contract file; its one new commitment is the log line's soft contract (Ratified decisions), a decision, not an API (d87). *Cross-WP reliance* (d86): `publishTerminal`'s `allBound` fast path relies on WP-B's `AssetStore.publish(_:binding:…)` returning locations only for ids of the binding; WP-B's code guarantees it today and no contract states it, so ARCHITECTURE §4.3 records it, and a later change to `bindRecords` or `stillBound` must keep it (only T-ORDER's `sweepFollowsFirst` case would catch an unbound id announced). *Authorised edits outside WP-F* (S3-R27 11.6, its first condition as S3-R28 item 4 amends it; ARCHITECTURE §1.2): the eight benchmark files `BenchSyncCycle21.swift` (`1645233`), `BenchSyncJoinChains.swift` (`7876a28`), `BenchSyncReroutePins.swift` (`f4f35bd`), `BenchSyncRerouteTakeOff.swift` (`c99d82a`), `BenchSyncReroutes.swift` (`94795ca`), `BenchSyncWaitersBound.swift` (`06d5e40`), `BenchSyncCycle22.swift` (`06c9ddf`) and `BenchSyncCycle22Purge.swift` (`e174f81`) are WP-F's, each with its helper types (`DeferBehindShape`, `DeferBehindRun`, `ManyMovesRun`, `PartialMoveRun`, `IndexCostRun`, `CommitShape`, `CommitRun`, `HeldPublicationBench`, `CommitTurnClock`; `JoinChainRun`; `TakeOffDrainRun`; `CommitRerouteShape`, `CommitRerouteBenchRun`; `ReannounceRun`; `PurgeShape`, `PurgeRun`, `PurgeJoinsAt`, `PurgeJoinsRun`). The condition reads: "Each file holds only `SyncBenchmarks` and `SyncBench` extensions and the helper types they use, declared in that file. No helper type's name collides with another work package's, and no file edits another work package's benchmark file." The draft said "None, in any cycle" until cycle 23 (the false 11.6 record, reqs #6, corrected in place by `79e1e0b`). The `///` the close-out left stale (Sync's `SyncEngine.swift`, `JobState.swift`, `EngineJobs.swift` and `EngineOriginGate.swift`, and the Model's `NetworkPolicy.swift` for S3F-7(b)) are corrected in this pass (Process).

    - *The margin list* (above 8.5 ms at a quiet load; a known edge, not a regression): `commitTwoTokensTurns` 9.02 ms (cycle 22, load 5.88), 8.95 ms (the close, alone at 1.75-1.88) and 9.27 ms (the verification, 3.67); `userSharedApplyTurns(.keep)` 9.04 (3.01-3.11) and 8.50 ms; `divergenceTurns` 8.83 and 8.35 ms; `concentrationCommitRerouteTurns` 8.71 and 8.05 ms (the last three added by c23 #3 with the reqs verifier's figures, not independently verified). At the merged head all pass: 8.80 ms (at a load of 14.35), 8.28 ms (no load printed), 7.60 ms and 7.31 ms. This host's run-to-run spread reaches about 17 %.

A load-only figure under rulings :763, not a cause of the FAIL. S1's row in item 9's inventory is O(1).
- **Ratified decisions** (the rulings decide where a draft differs; NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW as the rulings mark it: the tagged items stay as ratified, no close-out decision adds a tag, and the heading also names the owner items that WP-F's close left open).
  - **WP-A/WP-H.**
    - **S3AH-1** (supersedes A-4.7 for `.`; amendment to ADR-049). Exact diagnostics: `/abs` [absolute]; `//cdn.example/x` [authority, absolute]; `https://evil/x` [scheme, authority]; `///x` [authority, absolute, parentSegment]; `https:///x` [scheme, authority, parentSegment]; `a/?x` [parentSegment, queryOrFragment]. Two pre-existing WP-A tests became stricter (`a/./b` moved to the rejected list; the bridged-string test gained `.remotePathInvalid(.parentSegment)`); neither is a CDN test, so `MIGRATION.md` (G3) does not apply. Cost: one table load per ordinary byte and at most three byte compares at a `/`. Before the fix: 54 issues over the 14 cases, 2,122 in the seeded test and 4 in the old rules test.
    - **S3AH-2** (canonical tokens; amendment to ADR-013). A planted `c-<h>.01-2.part` parsed as `c-<h>.1-2.part`, so staging GC applied the age rules instead of "foreign: always removed" and two entries collapsed onto one inventory key: a robustness gap, with R-SEC-2 and I-4 intact. The confirmer refuted the other half: `TransferID(rawValue:)`'s acceptance of any `c-[a-z0-9-]*` of 1-96 bytes is its documented contract, and such names round-trip.
    - **S3AH-3** closes L4-R24(2) (amendment to ADR-053). **S3AH-4** closes OI-13 with the lowest of three CPU-time samples, a QoS pin rejected (amendment to ADR-052).

  - **WP-C.** **Decision S3C-2, ratified** (amends C-1; amendment to ADR-052): options (a) stop on a failed check between turns, keeping the records already taken, (b) roll them back, (c) one turn (the R8 exception); decision (a): nothing is released, the blob stays live with the remaining records (C-3's `stillReferenced` state) and the next run takes the rest; only for blobs of more records than a chunk (1,000) and only for a change between their turns (a change before the first turn keeps the blob whole); a started blob is finished even when other releases met the need; consequence: a split blob's records are downloaded again on demand and dedupe onto the live blob. Records: S3C-1 (`CorePoll.releasing`, 26 polls converted; `PollReleaseTests` failed against the old order at `c8ef118`, `PollReleaseTests.swift:58:9`; 200 debug and 20 TSan repetitions; `phaseFourStopsListing` passed 50 fresh TSan launches twice; no WP-B `Core/` site needed the authorisation); S3C-3/S3C-4 (M-S-07, M-S-08 and M-S-20 killed); S3C-5 (23 joins after an "ended" poll); S3C-6 (oracle reaps drawn outside the 40 operations, 192 reaps and 59 unlinks over 16 seeds, I-3 audited after every step by `CleanupAuditSink`; Z and G killed); S3C-7 (seven enumerations, 15/14/22/8/7/32/13 points per mode; X killed); S3C-8 (128 legacy pair cases, 11,606 pairs, and 8 OS-purge cases); S3C-9 (`70e1dba`, `100d1c2`; `EvictionOverlapTests`' `.integrityFailures` case now targets a stale record, expectations unchanged); S3C-10 (comment only); S3C-11 (S3-B-033 stays accepted; S3-B-041's doom exception unchanged).

Writers that bump in their own turn: `setResidencyInternal` (every WP-C doom and WP-B's; presence-reducing; covered by the serial), `bindRecords`, `recordAccessesInternal` (`.lazy`; S3-B-027's liveness), `performAdmit` (one record; a `.contentChanged` admission does make a present record stale, still covered because admit bumps in its own turn and changes the applied manifest's digest), `performMarkSynced` and `performMarkSyncDue`, `reap` and `doomOrphans`, `installLoadedState` (inside `open()`), and WP-C's pins, failures, quarantine, purge, eviction and legacy phases; the reference helpers bump through their callers or hold no record presence. `performApply` bumps once, in its last writing turn behind the fence, and is covered by the manifest digest (`applyDiff` installs the new applied manifest in its first writing turn; `finishLegacyMigration` compares `manifestDigest` in the turn after the pass). Result: no gap phase 3's decision can fall into.

S3D-2 (fixed; `1e3bee2`, `fdf5a3d`; `AssetClient` untouched): the relay keeps every handler, oldest first; `complete` calls the oldest still waiting, because by the k-th `complete` k waits that began after their handlers were accepted have returned, so the k-th handler's events were staged (calling every waiting handler, or a ticket per handler changing `AssetClient`, were not taken; on `s3-base` one completion called only the second handler). S3D-3 (`6f27ad7`, a test, kills M-N-22).
